# Questbound delivery preferences

- Whenever sharing the playable game, provide separate labeled **Desktop** and **Phone** links, as requested by the user. Keep both on the same current source version.
- The name is changing (October 2026): the host is choosing a new name for the game (Fablebound, or an alternative); "Eternal Tales" was dropped because it is a registered trademark in Ireland and the UK for games. Until the host confirms the new name, the product is still called Questbound in code, file names, storage keys (`questbound.*`), services, the launcher and these notes: rename nothing until the host says which name it is.
- Desktop development normally runs at `http://localhost:8081/`. Phones use only the encrypted HTTPS share link (`Questbound-Share.cmd` / `launch.ps1 -Share`, served by `phone-server.cjs --public <https origin>`), never localhost or a LAN address: the plain-HTTP Wi-Fi gateway is retired (`phone-server.cjs` without `--public` refuses to start, and a remote plain-HTTP request gets 426). Read `.questbound-share-session.json` for the current link, invite code and expiry; verify the share process and its tunnel are running before presenting it as live. Do not reuse stale links or codes.
- Rebuild `dist-phone` with `npm.cmd run build:web` after changes before sharing an updated Phone link. `start-phone.cmd` now calls `Questbound-Share.cmd`; `npm.cmd run phone` builds and then only reports that remote HTTP play is disabled.
- Phone access requires the PC, the share gateway and its tunnel, the existing private DM service and the account service running. During the beta nobody has to sign in: a phone plays with an anonymous guest identity (see Guest play) and the gateway's `/usage` check with the account service accepts it; once `accountsRequired` is turned on, a phone needs a signed-in, verified account to ask the Dungeon Master. Saves stay in each browser (per account profile), with a copy on the player's account and a recovery-code copy; a campaign shares one table. Do not claim live cross-device sync of solo play or a tested native app.
- Keep the DM service and API credentials private on loopback. Phone clients use the paired same-origin `/api` gateway. Never serve workspace files as static content; only serve the web export directory.
- Test new UI at phone and desktop widths and check phone endpoint routing when changing AI, image, or character generation.
- `Questbound.cmd` (runs `launch.ps1`) starts everything and reuses running services: DM on 8084, the account and campaign service (`sync-server.cjs`, a shim over `account-server.cjs`) on 8086, desktop on 8081 (`desktop-server.cjs` serving `dist-phone` and proxying `/api/sync/*` to 8086, or Expo with `-Dev`), and with `-Share` the share gateway on loopback 8087 behind the tunnel. No Wi-Fi gateway is started (port 8085 is no longer used; `-TrustNetwork` only points to `Questbound-Share.cmd`). It rebuilds `dist-phone` when top-level sources are newer. `-Stop` stops only what it started. Services run in their own hidden consoles with logs in `.questbound-logs/`. `Apply-Security-Update.cmd` (`restart-game-services.ps1`, `restart-service-rules.ps1`, checked by `verify-restart-services.ps1`) restarts running services after a server change.
- The API key may be remembered only DPAPI-encrypted in `.questbound-key.dpapi` (user's choice at the launcher prompt); never write it in plain text or pass it on a command line. Never run the key prompt on the user's behalf.
- The shared table is a set of campaigns (see the security update at the end and Server turns and campaign edits): `campaigns.cjs` keeps each in `.questbound-table/campaigns/<32 hex>.json` (at most 100 campaigns, 12 members each), and browsers reach it only through `/api/sync/campaign-*` (the desktop server's proxy, or the share gateway on phones). A stale `baseVersion` gets a conflict answer with the newer state, which the browser adopts; a turn already running answers 423. Rejected AI replies are logged (mechanical fields only) to `.questbound-diagnostics.jsonl`.
- Internet sharing (`Questbound-Share.cmd` / `launch.ps1 -Share`) runs a Cloudflare quick tunnel to the share gateway (`phone-server.cjs --public <https origin>`, loopback port 8087) that accepts only that host/origin, sets Secure cookies, throttles pairing per `CF-Connecting-IP` plus a global 50-wrong-codes-per-hour brake, and caps each session at 60 non-art DM requests per 10 minutes (see Request limits). The gateway invite only opens the site: each player still signs in to a verified account and joins a campaign with its own invite. The share session file is `.questbound-share-session.json` (git-ignored). Never expose the DM, account or desktop servers directly, and never type an invite code into a page yourself.
- The DM server queues requests (default 3 at once, `QUESTBOUND_DM_CONCURRENCY`); art polls bypass the queue. Restarting the DM needs the user's key: ask the user to run the launcher, never run the key prompt yourself. A DM started with `start-dm.ps1` runs in the user's own PowerShell window and is not in the launcher's pid file: `Questbound.cmd -Stop` does not stop it (and would stop the tunnel, changing the testers' link), so the way to restart it is Ctrl+C in that window, then `Questbound.cmd`. Do not build a way for the DM to relaunch itself with its inherited key: that was tried on 2026-10-02 and stopped by the assistant's safety check; it needs the user's explicit approval. Only `generate` (and the modules it requires fresh) reloads per request; `createServer`'s HTTP layer changes only after a restart. Edit `dm-server.cjs`, its modules (`dm-instructions.cjs`, `dm-schemas.cjs`, `dm-replies.cjs`) and `adventure-generator.cjs` in a temp copy (the copy of dm-server.cjs requires the modules beside it), `node --check` and test it (`QB_DM=./copy node verify-dm-stream.cjs`), then copy it over: a syntax error breaks every tester at once.
- Turns and rests (`adventureRules.js`, `recoveryRules.js` (draughts drunk or given, `shortRest`, `longRest`), `classActions.js`, `verify-turns.cjs`): a turn in a creature fight is one action and one bonus action. A healing draught (drunk or given), Second Wind, Lay on Hands, a Monk's strike, the off-hand light weapon and bonus-action spells are bonus actions (`bonusOptions`; healing only counts when hurt). After the action, if a bonus action is worth offering, the engine sets `game.actionUsed` (and `game.dodging` for a Dodge) and waits: only a bonus action or `end-turn` is accepted, and a bonus action taken then ends the turn. With nothing to offer, the foe answers at once as before, so tests that expect that use fixtures without a draught, a hurt companion or uses (`potions:0`, `resources:{wind:2,surge:1}`; a Fighter of level 2+ also pauses while Action Surge is ready). `short-rest` follows Baldur's Gate 3 (`shortRestLimit`: two between long rests, three for a Bard of level 2+; half HP for the hero and each following companion, and a companion knocked down comes round; Second Wind, Action Surge, short-recharge subclass uses and Warlock slots back; `game.shortRests`). `long-rest` restores HP, slots, features and short rests and keeps everything else (world, people, pack, feed): never rebuild the adventure with `newAdventure` for a rest. `dmChoices(hero,game,health)` and `quickActions(hero,game,health)` take the hero's health so healing is only offered when hurt. `restWouldHelp` (dmChoiceRules.js) treats health null as full hit points, so no short rest is offered to the player or the Dungeon Master when there is nothing to recover: the engine refuses such a rest ("already rested"), and on 2026-10-03 the DM took "catch my breath" as one and the whole turn failed. The action economy (2024 rules, `verify-action-economy.cjs`) holds in creature fights, brawls and the party's turn order:
  - A turn is one action and one bonus action. `turnCost(hero,game,action)` says 'action', 'bonus' or 'free'. Extra Attack is part of the one Attack action. At most one spell slot is spent a turn (`slotSpentThisTurn`).
  - `castError` refuses an action spell once `game.actionUsed` is set, and under Action Surge, so dmCommand fails before any Dungeon Master ruling is asked for. `fightTimingError` keeps reactions and castings of a minute or more out of fights.
  - An improvised attempt (the Dungeon Master's check, `commitWorldCheck`) in a creature fight is the action: it runs `{type:'improvised'}` through adventureStep, so the turn pauses, the foe answers, or the party turn passes on. It is refused once the action is spent, and in brawls. dm-rules `validateGroundedReply` refuses such check replies too.
  - Giving a healing draught (`give-potion`, including a party Revive) is a bonus action like drinking one. `potionGifts` in classActions.js feeds `bonusOptions` and `bonusChoices`.
  - Action Surge (`surgeLimit`/`surgeReady`, choice `class:surge`, typed "action surge"): a Fighter of level 2+ takes it once the action is spent. It clears `actionUsed` and sets `game.surged` and `resources.surge` (twice per rest from level 17, once a turn). The extra action can never cast a spell or work a magic item. The turn pauses while it is ready. A short rest restores it. Long rests, `endHeroTurn` and `fightOver` clear `surged`, which is a party turn field. Saves allow `surged` only in stage combat, and dodging with `actionUsed` or `surged`.
  - In a brawl a draught, a stronger draught, Second Wind, Lay on Hands, a bonus-action spell or a draught given to someone is a bonus action taken before the action (which still ends the turn there). The people fighting do not answer it.
  - A typed sentence with two moves (`splitTurn`/`twoMoves` in dmCommands.js, never inside a spell's own name): two blows at one target are one attack; in a fight an action plus a bonus or free move is `{type:'turn',steps:[a,b]}`, played by `wholeTurn` while the turn is still the hero's; anything more resolves the first move, with a 'Not this turn: "…" must wait for your next turn' line ('One thing at a time' out of fights).
  - `turnLeft(hero,game)` goes to the Dungeon Master as `turnResources.left` (with `attacksPerAction` and `actionSurge`) and into `context.now`. The Cast… picker offers only spells `castableNow` accepts. The opening attack stays a turn of its own (house rule).
- Speaking to the Dungeon Master (`turnMemory.js`, `verify-direct.cjs`): the DM switch beside the message box, or a message starting "DM," / "(ooc)", sends `context.outOfCharacter` (`canRewind`, `lastTurn`); the server answers out of character, changes nothing, and may return `rewind:true`, and `fault:true` when the player reports, or the DM admits, something the game got wrong (the game then offers to tell the host). `Adventure.act(action,conversation,random,{from})` then applies the exchange to the state remembered before the player's last turn (`rememberTurn`/`turnToRewind`: one turn deep, in memory only, never at a shared table). The exchange is an ordinary no-action turn (`conversation.direct`), so it shows in the feed and the journal. When the Dungeon Master cannot be reached, `offline()` in `useDmTurn.js` tells the host what to start and a guest only that it is not answering (the old "open your private AI setup window" confused phone players).
- Typing on phones (`webLayout.js`): `#root` is pinned to the visual viewport (`--questbound-height`, `--questbound-top`), so the message box rests above the keyboard on Android and iOS. While the message box is focused on a touch device (`touchKeyboard`), `DungeonMaster` reports `onTyping`: the battle buttons, the Suggestions bar, feed header, bottom tabs and (on short screens) the HUD and strips step aside, and the feed stays on its last lines when its height changes. Controls beside a field carry `dataSet={{keepfocus:'true'}}` so a tap does not blur the field first (a blur closes the keyboard, moves the control and loses the tap). Fields in scrolling pages are scrolled into view when the visible height shrinks; dialogs (`data-qb=scrim`) are sized to the visible area. While a keyboard is up the bottom safe-area inset is dropped (`--sa-b` 0; see Filling the screen). Test by focusing a field and resizing the viewport to about 375×400.
- Filling the screen (`webLayout.js`, `public/index.html`, `public/manifest.json`, `theme.js`/`themeRules.js` `pageBackground`, `DisplayControls.js` `HomeScreenHelp`, `verify-fit.cjs`; 2026-10-11, the host's words: "double check that the game fits properly on all phones and is able to fill the full screen"): the page is `viewport-fit=cover`, so the game draws under the notch, rounded corners and home indicator (before this, `#root` carried the insets as padding and every screen had a bare ink strip top and bottom). `#root` is never padded; the safe-area insets are style variables (`--sa-t/r/b/l` from `env(safe-area-inset-*)`, `--sa-b` 0 while a keyboard is up) given as transparent borders to the screens' frames (`dataSet={{safe:'pad'}}` on App.js's two shells and the launch card; every `[data-qb=scrim]`, the story cover included), so backgrounds still fill the insets while content stays clear. The HUD and the phone tab bar reach into the insets beside them (negative margins plus borders, `background-origin:border-box`; removing the two `html [data-qb=hud|tabbar]` rules in webLayout.js brings back a plain strip if the host prefers it); floating cards near the top (deeds, experience: CinematicLayer.js) carry `dataSet={{safe:'top'}}`; a sheet inside a scrim is capped at `max-height:100%`. A new full-screen layer or dialog must take one of these, never pad `#root`. `index.html` carries the Home Screen tags statically (`theme-color`, `mobile-web-app-capable`, `apple-mobile-web-app-capable`, black-translucent status bar, `apple-mobile-web-app-title`, `format-detection`; the viewport also says `interactive-widget=resizes-content`; `fullscreen.js` adds them only when missing), and theme.js keeps `theme-color` to the page's ink in each theme and mode (`pageBackground(theme,mode)`), so the phone's bars match the page. The manifest (`"id": "/"`) opens installs full screen (`display_override` fullscreen, standalone) in any orientation. iPhone pages cannot use the Fullscreen API: `HomeScreenHelp` (the menus' corner button, Settings → Display, and on an iPhone not running from the Home Screen the pause menu's Full screen cell) explains Add to Home Screen (Share under ••• in newer Safari), scrolls on a phone held sideways, and says the Home Screen game keeps its own saves apart from Safari's (carry a hero with Move your hero); Android and desktop keep the real full-screen toggle. On Android a browser page cannot hide the address bar without the Fullscreen API (the page never scrolls), so the Full screen button or installing is the way. The invite page in phone-server.cjs keeps its own insets (`max(16px, env(safe-area-inset-*))` per side), a clamped title (`clamp(24px,8.6vw,34px)`) so it fits 320 px, and a 96 px seal on a phone held sideways (a change there needs a share-gateway restart). Choice cards in the builder (`CharacterBuilder.js`, whose step track is 28 px tall to tap), the ready-made hero cards (`QuickHeroes.js`) and the Inventory's equipment slots shrink (`flexShrink:1`, `minWidth:0`) rather than run off a 320 px screen or a phone held sideways. `verify-fit.cjs` checks all of this statically; on 2026-10-11 every screen from the boot card to dying was measured at twelve phone sizes (320×568 to 932×430) with iPhone insets in headless Chrome (CDP `Emulation.setSafeAreaInsetsOverride`, mobile emulation, `getBoundingClientRect`/`elementFromPoint`: no sideways scroll, nothing off screen or under an inset, no overlapping controls, no bare page at the edges). A real device was not available.
- Lands and maps (`mapRules.js`, `mapLayout.js`, `mapArt.js`, `AdventureMap.js`, `verify-regions.cjs`, `verify-map-layout.cjs`): every written story has its own `story.geography` (where its three starting places lie and which are joined: a line, a fork or a loop), made by `makeGeography` from the story writer's `layout` or from the story id; a save without one keeps the old fixed triangle (`mapCoordinates`; only stories from before geographies: a game with no story at all is never played, see No crossroads). The writer also gives the land a name and a terrain (`story.region`, `story.terrain`; `terrains`), a kind for each of the three places, up to six `landmarks` that `freshStoryGame` puts on the map as unvisited places (`maxLandmarks`), and one or two `lands` beyond this country (`farLandPlaces`: each a region `r1`/`r2` with one known place 15 to 300 miles from the start, joined to it by a road). Places lie a little off their compass bearing (seeded, repeatable). Regions: the home region plus `game.world.regions` (`r1`…`r99`, at most 59: `maxRegions` 60 with home, never forgotten; see Beyond the map); a place carries `region` and inherits it from where it was found; the DM's `discovery.region` ({name, terrain}, miles up to 300) opens a new land, and a sequel turns the earlier country into a region of its own. `regionLayout(game,w,h,{region})` lays out one region's sheet and returns `exits` (roads to other regions, drawn to the edge and tappable); `regionMapSvg` draws it to suit the terrain (`mapThemes`: sea along a coast, a river, ranges, woods, pools, cavern walls, all seeded). Long roads are walked eight hours a day (`travelRoute`). `regionLabels` names what fits; `RegionSheet` zooms and `Full map` opens it in a dialog. Three views zoom out one from another (2026-10-23, the host's words: "we should add the ability for the map to help guide the player around cities. we can have it zoom out to show all areas around, or zoom in to focus on different areas in the city that the player may want to explore. while you're making that function possible we should make it possible for the map to zoom out even further once the player leaves the initial map, to make navigation back to previously explored areas possible. make sure the game is able to fit onto all different kinds of screens"; `mapLayout.js` `landCities`/`cityOf`/`cityLayout`/`scaleBar`/`landsLayout`/`landsGap`/`landsSheetScale`/`placeKindOf`, `mapArt.js` `regionMapSvg` (`city`, `outside`, `bar`, `nameSize`) and `landsMapSvg`, `AdventureMap.js` `MapFrame`/`CitySheet`/`LandsSheet`, `verify-map-layout.cjs`): City, Whole land and All lands, chips over the sheet, widest first; − at a sheet's widest steps out a view. A city is worked out from the map alone (never from the AI): a settlement-kind place (a found place's kind, or a starting place's kind in its story) and the places of its own land within 1.5 miles of it (`cityReach`) of a kind a town is made of (`districtKinds`: settlement, road, water, ruin, cave, shrine, landmark), at least three in all (`cityLeast`); the settlement with the most such places round it is a city first, then the next not yet taken, so two towns are two cities, a second settlement within reach is a quarter of the first, and a place counts in one city only. Camps, lairs, forest, wilds and mountain are never districts: they, and places past the reach, are `outside` when a road joins them to the city or they lie within three miles of its middle (at most eight, `cityOutside`), drawn as small pointers where they lie or at the sheet's edge (spread round it, clear of the scale bar, the compass and the zoom buttons) with an arrowhead and their miles from the walls. The City view is drawn to scale (linear, north up, a quarter mile across at least, nudged only as far as markers need; roofs over the open ground, a scale bar, names at 12.5 px); it is the view the map opens on when the hero stands in a city of the land on show (else Whole land), grows taller (up to twice, the page scrolling) until every district is named and only then opens zoomed; tapping a district zooms in to it and centres it, and its card gives the kind, the description in brief, who lives there (people met, or residents of a place visited, alive and not following: `livesAt`) and the same Travel move the map always sent. A land with two cities gets a row of cities. The test case is the written tale "The Siege of Dunscaur" (Medium): Saltmarket Terrace, the Damhold and five quarters are one city of seven, the Iron Camp outside it; verify-map-layout checks a city laid out the same way under names of our own, and the tale itself when the PC has it (never in the repository). All lands is offered once known places lie in more than one land: every land a patch of its terrain's paper at the middle of its places, laid out like a region sheet (from the home land, each land off a neighbour in its road's true direction at a log of its miles, nudged at least 46 px apart; `landsSheetScale` makes the sheet larger than its frame, and scrollable, when there are many), the roads between lands with their true miles, the hero's land in red, lands never reached dashed. Tapping a land gives its card (terrain, places, visited or not, the nearest place by road and how long) with Its map and Travel to the nearest place a road reaches (an ordinary `travelTo` move), and the list under the sheet is the lands. Land tabs and the compass of ways out are 36 px tall since that day.
- Beyond the map (2026-10-11, the host's words: "make sure travel is possible outside of the region of the map, with more map generated outside if the player decides to move to a new map. then make sure the game remembers the old map when a new one is generated, there can be a limit to this"; `mapRules.js` `beyondError`/`edgePlace`/`beyondPlan`/`goBeyond`/`roomToMake`/`letRumoursGo`/`mapHasRoom`/`discoveryRoom`, `dmChoiceRules.js` `beyondChoices`/`mapMoves`, `dmSceneRules.js` `placesNamed`/`withNamedJourneys`, `AdventureMap.js` `BeyondCard`, `verify-beyond.cjs`): `{type:'beyond',bearing}` (`beyondStep` in travelRules.js, with the arrival ambush check, the victory-stage scene and the brawl block that travel has) takes the hero off the edge of the land they are in: a new region (a name and terrain from our own word lists, leaning by direction, never the land just left) joined by a road from the place of this land farthest that way (`edgePlace`), an arrival place 25 to 70 miles on (usually a town or camp) and one or two places heard of a few miles further (some risky); all seeded from the story id, land, edge place, direction and map size (`beyondPlan`), so every device and the campaign server agree; the walk follows `travelRoute` (eight hours a day, so a day to three). Offered as `beyond-N`…`beyond-NW` in `dmChoices` (never chips: `quickActions` skips them; dropped before journeys when the list passes thirty), typed ("I head north beyond the map": dm-intent's "beyond the map" second look, settled by the game when a direction is named and its choice is offered, else the model is asked which way), or from the map's Beyond the map compass (`BeyondCard`, under the current land's sheet and on the Full map: eight ways, disabled where the game refuses; pick, then Set out; its card says "Lands you leave stay on your map, and you can always travel back."). Lands travelled are never forgotten (2026-10-12, the host's words: "previous lands travelled should not be forgotten. a player should be able to return to those lands."; the old `landsToForget`/`forgetLand`/`forgetLands` and their "The memory of <land> fades." are gone): up to sixty lands (`maxRegions` 60: home and r1…r99) and 150 places (`maxWorldPlaces`, ids p1…p999, save pattern `p\d{1,3}`; coordinates up to 1e9 ft) are kept, and regions carry `last` (map minutes at the last arrival, set in `travelTo`). When something new needs room (`roomToMake`, worked out once per game) only what the hero merely heard of gives way (`letRumoursGo`/`discoveryRoom`): a place never visited with nothing found from it (so no road back runs through it), nobody living there, no companion waiting, no fallen unsearched, not where the hero stands, not the errand's place, not one the tale still names (chapter under way or next, open leads, errand), never the last place of its land (other lands' rumours first, oldest first); or a whole land never reached (none of its places visited or kept, not named by the tale, no road to another land through it), oldest first. The log (not the story log) says "Your map is full, so the rumour of X fades from it: you never went there." or "Word of <land> fades from your map: you never went there." When nothing may give way (the hard cap) the road beyond goes no further ("The road goes no further: your map is full, and every land and place on it is one you have been to or still need. Travel back to a land you know."; no beyond choices), a DM discovery is refused ("Your map is full: …") and `canDiscover` is false; with only the lands full a far discovery joins the land the hero is in. Every place stays travelable; in practice 150 places is some 50 to 60 lands of beyond trips. New ids are the first free ones (`freePlaceId`, `freeRegionId`, `freePersonId`), never ids let go in the same turn. `travelChoices` adds the nearest place of every other land (past thirty choices the farthest give way); `dmContext(hero,game,health,input)` adds the journey to any land or place the player's words name (`placesNamed`: the whole name, "the" optional; `withNamedJourneys`, dropping a far journey or a way beyond to stay within thirty), and useDmTurn.js and server-turn.cjs pass the input and look an `actionId` up in `dmChoices` plus `mapMoves`; `mapMoves(game)` is every journey a road reaches plus the ways beyond, which the map's Travel buttons send and `commitDmTurn` and the campaign server (`server-turn.cjs`; `rules-engine.cjs` exports it) accept, so any land can be travelled back to: on the map, among the choices, by name, solo or at a table. Saves from before load as they were (r1…r19, p1…p99 and n1…n48 sit inside the new patterns); a land an older build let fade cannot be brought back, and its old "fades" lines stay in that save's log. Routes (`routeMemo`: one Dijkstra per start place while the places array is the same), place look-ups (an id index per places array), `npcIdsOf` (per `game.people` object) and room are worked out once, so the DM context for the largest world takes about 120 ms. The map's row of lands puts the land the hero is in first, then the one on show, home, and the rest by last visit (Show all: four lines, `openRows={4}`, then it scrolls). Once the hero has more than one land the map's All lands view (see Lands and maps) shows them all at once with the roads between them, and its card or list opens a land's map or travels back to it; the Beyond the map compass sits under the Whole land and All lands views, not the City. A first arrival in a land pays experience only with a place that still pays (ten a tale), so walking off the map again and again is no way to level. The DM gets BEYOND THE MAP in FAR JOURNEYS (select the beyond choice; a `discovery.region` only for a land the player names; narrate the days on the road and the arrival as the game names it; bring someone in at a town; lands travelled are never forgotten: to go back select the travel choice, else return discovery with that place's exact name, its land as region and travel true, which walks there; a rumour the game let go was never visited), `context.world.landsRemembered` and `context.world.farLands` (see Who is here); dm-intent counts farLands place names when deciding whether a word names one place. New lands keep the names the game gives them (the DM describes, never renames). Every tale lets the hero leave (2026-10-23, the host's words: "make sure every single one of the stories allows players to leave the map if they so desire, whether that is by easy travel, or by overcoming some sort of obstacle put in their way"; `beyondBarred`/`roadWatchFor` in mapRules.js, `beyondLabel` in dmChoiceRules.js, `verify-beyond.cjs`): `beyondError` no longer refuses a written tale before its last chapter. A tale whose staging keeps its hero close (`noBeyond`, by default for short and medium, false for long; WRITTEN-TALES.md 4.5) **bars** the road out instead, while it is before its last chapter, still active, and the hero sets out from the tale's own country (its home land, or a land the tale itself speaks of: seatStaging keeps their ids as `land:<id>` in `pace.fired`; a land beyond already reached and the lands of earlier tales are never barred): `beyondPlan` puts on the new land's arrival place `danger:'risky'` and a `threat` drawn from the same seed (`roadWatchFor(terrain, game.encounterLevel)`: by the land's terrain, one creature alone below third level, never a pack, the goblins' band, the zombies or the orc; names and looks in our own words, "Tollgate Cutthroat", "Gatepost Spider"…) and no places heard of past it (no journey goes round it), so `arrivalAmbush` starts the fight on arrival, on every device and at the campaign server alike. goBeyond keeps the arrival's id as `beyond:<id>` in `pace.fired`; once one such place is cleared (the fight won) the road out is open for the rest of the tale; fleeing takes the hero back where they set out, the watcher waiting with its wounds. While barred, the beyond choices read "Go beyond the map to the north (the road out is watched while this tale's trouble lasts: you will have to fight your way through)" (the map's moves too), the map's Beyond the map card adds "The road out is watched while this tale’s trouble lasts: you will have to fight your way through.", and BEYOND THE MAP says "A road out that is watched: the game starts a fight on arrival, told as what bars the way." AI journeys, the Skirmish, long tales and tales with `noBeyond:false` travel as before. Once there, "A tale waits in <Land>" offers a tale set where the hero stands (solo; see The campaign's screens under Next tales). While the road out is watched the scene says `world.roadWatched:true` (dmSceneRules.js), and the server turns a reply that opens a far land by discovery (its `region`) into the beyond choice that way (dm-server.cjs, noted "a far land while the road out is watched: the way beyond"), so the watcher is met: live on 2026-10-23 a far-land discovery walked the hero out of The Last Ferry's chapter 1 untouched (verify-beyond).
- Species (`characterRules.js` `species`/`speciesTraits`/`speciesProfile`/`heroSpecies`/`fixedLineage`/`baseSpeciesOf`/`speciesSkills`, `verify-species.cjs`; the host on 2026-10-11: "make sure wood elf and high elf are two different classes with their respective values"): the elves are three species of their own, High Elf, Wood Elf and Drow, each the SRD 5.2 Elf with its lineage (`fixedLineage`: High Elf, Wood Elf, Drow; the builder shows no lineage picker for them) and `baseSpecies` 'Elf' (`baseSpeciesOf`, for anything keyed on elves), listed together in the builder as the Elves (`speciesGroups`: one bordered panel with a heading and a line about elves, at the place of the first, between Dwarf and Gnome; `optionCards` takes groups; the host on 2026-10-12: "maybe drow should be listed under the elves with the others."). Plain 'Elf' and 'Dark Elf' (the Drow's stored name until 2026-10-12; new heroes store 'Drow') are in `legacySpecies`: a hero made as one stays valid and is never rewritten (the adventure save is tied to the hero's exact JSON); `heroSpecies(hero)` reads an old Elf as the species its lineage names, or a High Elf when it has none (30 feet as before, plus the High Elf spells), and an old Dark Elf as a Drow (`speciesName` for a bare name, e.g. a roster summary), and the builder turns either into that species when the hero is edited. Nothing on screen says "Dark Elf" or "Dark Elf (Drow)". Show a hero's species with `heroSpecies`, never `hero.species` (App.js, the story writer's `context.player.species`, GameHud, Inventory, HeroRoster, the epitaph, CharacterSheet, entityRules, partyRules, taleRules and dmContext do); portraits (`worldArtRules`), the regard seed and feedback records keep the raw species, so nothing is repainted or re-rolled. `speciesTraits` gives every species its size, speed, darkvision and traits in our own words, each marked whether the rules play it (`played: 'game'`: Dwarven Toughness, species spells, size, Keen Senses) or the Dungeon Master weighs it (`'story'`: Fey Ancestry, Trance, Halfling Luck, Relentless Endurance and the rest); `combatBasics` returns `speed`/`speedFormula` (Wood Elf and Goliath 35 feet; Chain Mail without Strength 13 takes 10 off), `darkvision` (Dwarf, Orc and Drow 120) and `size`; nothing in the engine moves by feet, so speed is shown and told, not rolled. The builder's species cards, the Character Sheet's species panel and `context.player.speciesTraits` {size, speed, darkvision, traits} show them. Every elf's Keen Senses trains Insight, Perception or Survival (`form.keenSense`, chosen on the builder's species page, Perception for elves made before; `trainedSkills` adds `speciesSkills`; `buildError` refuses it on a non-elf). `relationshipRules.js` has plurals and reputations for High Elf (as Elf) and Wood Elf (a little warmer), and for Drow under both names (plural 'drow', the old Dark Elf's weights; `regardMemory` says "A drow"; `sameKind` treats drow as dark elf, so elf, drow and dark elf people are kin to a Drow hero under either name). The ready-made wizard Ilyra is a High Elf. Checks that inject names for `entityRules.js`/`partyRules.js` need `heroSpecies`; `rules-engine.cjs` exports it.
- Reactions to the hero's kind (`relationshipRules.js`, `verify-regard.cjs`): `speciesRegard(game,id,hero)` is each person's view of the hero's species (kin, warm, curious, indifferent, wary, scornful, with a reason): written by the story writer or the DM (`regard` on the NPC or person) or drawn from who they are and how that species is commonly met (`reputations`, seeded; a human among humans is indifferent). It sets the first impression (`firstImpressions`, `introducePerson(game,sketch,hero)`), sways Persuasion, Deception and Performance aimed at that person (`regardSway`: +2 to −3, until they are won over), reaches the DM as `regardsYourKind`, and shows in the People panel while it still says something true.
- Portraits and gender (`worldArtRules.js`): NPCs and people carry `gender` (woman, man, other) from the writer or the DM; without it `genderOf` reads their appearance (a hero's description, then backstory). A woman's portrait description opens by saying she has no beard, any facial hair written into it is cut, and her art id gets `-w` so portraits painted before this are painted afresh. Never write a beard into a woman's appearance (the built-in captain once had a "beard ring"). A ready journey's own subjects (its three places, keeper and mara, its residents by name as `resident-<slug>`, and its main foe) are painted under `story.artId` (`artCampaign`/`residentArtId` in worldArtRules.js), once for every player of that journey; people and places met in play keep the copy's own story id. `validStory` accepts `libraryId` (uuid) and `artId` (`'journey-'+libraryId`). A journey's card cover is its opening place's picture (the same key as the opening scene). A person with a library face (a `pool` id or a look-alike) is not painted at all; see The people library and Look-alike portraits and shared creatures.
- The story log (`storyLog.js`, `journalRules.js`, `verify-story-log.cjs`): `game.storyLog` holds one short line per major event (place found, reached or returned to, person met, joined or lost, fight begun and how it ended, deaths, grudges and debts, gold and finds, rests, levels, the end of the tale), worked out by `storyEvents(before,after,hero,{action})` and appended by `withStoryLog` (in `settleTurn`, see Server turns); level-ups and new chapters append their own lines. The Log tab shows it newest first; the engine's raw lines sit under "Latest rolls and rulings".
- The story so far, remembered for good (`memoryRules.js`, `verify-memory.cjs`; the host, 2026-10-13: "fix the dungeon master's memory so that it remembers everybody's adventure from start to finish"):
  - **The chronicle.** `game.memory` (save and shared world, never a hero field) keeps the chronicle: every tale `{no, title, status, began, summary?, chapters:[{n, title, done, beats, folded}]}`, folded by the engine from the story log as turns settle (`withMemory` in `settleTurn`, after the deeds; `memoryOf` folds lazily anything not yet seen and builds an old save's memory from its storyLog, storyHistory and "Previously" note; an old save's memory is also built once when it is upgraded, see Saves and their formats). A tale-start line opens a tale, "Chapter N:" a chapter, "Adventure complete:" completes it. A chapter keeps 14 lines while played, 8 when done and 4 once its tale closes; only the newest 4 closed tales keep chapters, then a 400-character summary (160 past the 30th), and past 120 tales an `era` line. A line let go (least important, oldest first, `beatRank`) is kept as names and counts in `folded`. Nothing is dropped, only compressed.
  - **Facts.** The DM's reply field `remember` (an item of the effects list, at most 2, `{text, about, settles}`; empty out of character, in a spell ruling or a scene cue; dropped from a reply that starts an action, as relationships are; tidied by `cleanRemember`) asks the game to keep a fact it cannot see itself (a promise, a secret, a name, a debt in words, a plan, who knows what). `rememberFacts` (in commitDmTurn) keeps it with ids and names, or settles an open fact by id ("Settled: ..." in the chronicle). At most 40 stay open; older ones are folded as "Remembered: ...". 20 settled ones are kept.
  - **What the DM reads.** Each turn the DM gets `context.memory` (`memoryBlock`): at most 4,500 characters, in shares: the tale under way, facts bearing on who and what is here or named (with ids), the chronicle lines about anyone or anywhere named, settled facts here, other facts in brief (`moreFacts`), and earlier tales shorter with age ("Before that, oldest first: ..."). THE STORY SO FAR and REMEMBER are in `contractInstructions`.
  - **The story writer.** Since the compendium (2026-10-23, see The compendium under Ready journeys) every tale the Dungeon Master writes is written ahead, so dm-server.cjs never passes `context.storySoFar` to the writer (a shared tale cannot weave one hero's past; the story so far still reaches the Dungeon Master in play through `game.memory`). The device may still send it, and `generateAdventure` called directly still reads it: a tale written fresh for a hero who has lived one carries `context.storySoFar` (`storySoFarForWriter`: the memory block with the tale under way closed: the tale just lived and the earlier ones, and the threads still open, within the same budget); adventure-generator.cjs tidies it (`tidyStorySoFar`: 16 tales, 40 threads, plain lines) and its STORY SO FAR paragraph lets the new tale pick up a thread or two, never contradict, revive the dead or repeat a settled conflict (verify-memory).
  - **Carried.** `carriedBase` closes the tale (`closedMemory`) and `newAdventure` carries `memory` into next tales and somewhere new alike. `joinRegion` remaps fact ids (`remappedMemory`).
  - **The journal is bounded.** Now that the chronicle keeps the history, the journal (`appendJournal` → `compactJournal`) keeps the newest `journalLimits.talk` 60 records of the Dungeon Master's turns (conversation, spell rulings, checks: `journalTalk`) and `journalLimits.entries` 200 entries in all; past that the oldest go first (those records, then milestones, then the player's notes; never the tale's first entry), renumbered from 1. Old saves with longer journals are accepted and trimmed at their next entry. Before this a long shared table would have met the 2,000,000-character snapshot limit after some 1,000 to 1,500 turns; `verify-long-play.cjs` plays 5,000 turns of one tale (the save levels off near 140 kB, the request near 26 kB, a four-hero table's campaign file near 150 kB).
  - **Log tab.** The Log tab's "Earlier tales" fold reads `chronicleView`.
  - **Request size.** To leave room, dmContext sends skills as lines, only present people's defenses outside fights, the latest 8 world facts (300 characters each) and nearby people without their memories. The largest request (verify-dm-trim) is about 57 kB, 4.5 kB of it memory.
- Inventory and descriptions (`Inventory.js`, `ActionGuide.js`, `descriptions.js`, `verify-descriptions.cjs`): the Inventory tab shows equipment slots around the hero's portrait and everything carried; every item, action chip and spell has a description (`itemDescription`, `actionDescription`, `spellSummary`), and the check fails if anything is left with a generic one. Loot items carry the DM's `note`. `game.wield` (set by Take in hand) makes a carried weapon the main one (`gearedHero` → `equipment.wield` → `weaponAttacks`; a two-hander taken in hand slings the shield: see Hands). Magic items are attuned there (`attuneItem`, at most 3; the oldest gives way; at a table the `attune` campaign edit). The ⓘ chip at the end of the Actions chips (and of the battle buttons) opens the action guide.
- The story's main foe (`storyFoeStats` in `storyRules.js`): a written story gets `foeStats` when it begins (18 HP, AC 12, +3, 1d6 at levels 1 and 2 after `foeEase` (see Survival odds), 1d6+1 from level 3, growing with the hero's level; type from the writer's `foeDamageType`); stories from before keep the lighter default. Against more than one hero it is scaled to the side (see A foe against a bigger side). `node balance-check.cjs --foe story` simulates it.
- Long tales (`storyRules.js`, `adventureRules.js`, `dmContext.js`, `dmChoiceRules.js`, `verify-chapters.cjs`): a written story has `chapters` (two to six of {title, goal, turn}; `story.chapter` is the one under way) and `leads` (`l1`…`l4`, each {title, hook, done?}); `questState(game)` reads where it stands and `currentGoal` what to do now. The Dungeon Master gets `context.quest` (the chapter under way, what achieving it reveals, the next one, open leads) and never the later chapters' truths (`context.story` is sent without them). The tale's road (2026-10-23, the host's words: "each pre made story should have a clearly written plot line for the DM to work off of, but should be freely explorable by the player"; `taleArc`/`arcCut` in dmSceneRules.js): `quest.arc`, one line a chapter, "1. <title>: <goal> (done)", "2. <title>: <goal> (now)", "3. <title>: <goal>" (titles cut to 60, goals to 120, at a word; none for a tale of one chapter), never a truth, the secret, the staging, an ending or the resolution, in the part of the scene that lasts the tale (it changes once a chapter), read out of fights only (briefer drops it in one; a telling out of a fight keeps it, so it still shares that part with the turn before); THE LONG TALE says "quest.arc: the road the tale means to take; steer toward (now), never tell, skip to or count as reached a later chapter; the player may wander anywhere." It moves the tale on with the choice `story-advance` (the engine's `advanceChapter`: log lines, journal entries, a world fact) and closes a lead with `lead-done:<id>`; `story-complete` is offered and accepted only in the last chapter. Neither is ever a chip (`quickActions` skips them). Finishing a chapter, a lead or the tale is worth experience (see Experience and levels); `advanceChapter` no longer touches levels. Each written tale has a `hoard` ({item: a catalogue id, name, note}) that its foe carries; `tidyStory` drops an unsound one and the Dungeon Master is not shown it. The feed shows chapter, lead and level lines as cards (`describeEvent`), the Quest tab the chapter, the aim and the leads (the player's own tick is a note to themselves, never the lead seen through: see Making it move), and a new chapter is announced with the `area` cue. Stories without chapters play as before. `offeredChoices` keeps the DM's choices to thirty (the server refuses more). A written tale (story.staging) shows its tier beside the chapter ("Short tale · Chapter 1 of 2"), its goal as Aim and what is at stake (TaleState.js); it offers no `story-complete` (the engine ends it).
- People speak first (`greetings.js`, `engage` in useDmTurn.js, `verify-greetings.cjs`; since 2026-10-22 with no request, the host approving it: the game already had written greeting lines, so use them for the opening line "and only call the Dungeon Master when the player actually says something"): the Speak chip (under Suggestions → Speak) opens the conversation and the person speaks first in a line the game writes, `greeting(game,id,hero)` → {mood, scene, aside, line}: the line from `greetingPools` by mood (grudge, hostile, forgiven, unfriendly, following, bond, friendly, then their view of the hero's kind (kin, warm, curious, wary, scornful), then `again` for someone who has talked with the hero before (a line of theirs in the feed or a memory), else plain; 14 moods, 87 lines), mixed for anyone not set against the hero with lines of their work (`greetingRoles`, 37 lines matched on the role: inn, smith, guard, priest, healer, merchant, scholar, farmer, hunter, fisher, bard, cook, miner, noble, thief) and, meeting the hero for the first time where they live, of their place (`greetingHomes`, "Not many find their way to Stillwater Market. What brings you?"); friends may say the hero's first name (`{hero}`). The aside (narration) says what they are doing as the hero comes up (`greetingAsides` by mood, `greetingRoleAsides` by work: "Brom rests the hammer on the anvil."); an aside's second form (after `|`) names the hero for a table of several heroes (never "you"), and the line's `to` names them in the feed as before. Seeded per person and turn; never a line said in the last two turns, everyone present a different one (`standInGreetings`), and not a line this person greeted with in the latest 40 turns while others are left; the aside not one told lately. No request at all: `engage` plays it through `act` at once (offline too), and the first request comes with the player's words; Suggestions → Say stands in with `replyIdeas`. `shouldGreet` keeps someone who spoke in the last turn from greeting again, and nobody greets in a fight. The greeting is a trigger turn: no player line, not remembered for rewinds. At a campaign table the greeting is a server turn (`table.turn(scene,{npcId,greet:true})`; `resolveTurn` in server-turn.cjs checks `shouldGreet`, works out the opening itself with `greeting` and settles a trigger turn with that aside and line, asking the Dungeon Master nothing); scene cues are still skipped there (`table.joined`). Two scene cues are written the same way (`cueGreeting`, `writtenCues` in greetings.js; sceneTriggers.js marks them `written` with `fallback` and `aside`; the cue effect in useDmTurn.js asks nothing for them): back at the tale's starting place (`return-inn`) and the morning after a long rest (`rest`), by warmth (cold, plain, warm). Waking from near death, a fight lost or won and first arrivals are still told by the Dungeon Master. Each written greeting saves one request (about $0.0004).
- Talking one on one (`speakingPeople` in playbackRules.js, `oneOnOneInstructions` in dm-instructions.cjs, `verify-social.cjs`): with more than one person present, a conversation sends `conversationWith.oneOnOne` (the "Just you two" / "Everyone here" switch in the conversation header, per device, back to Just you two for each new conversation). The server then allows only that person to speak and no new person to be introduced (never on a scene cue). Anyone addressed answers in their own words, even someone hostile or grudging (curt, refusing, threatening, but never silent or walking off), and words never forgive a grudge. During a brawl `conversationParticipants` comes from `speakingPeople`, so those fighting may shout, plead or yield (their deeds still come only from the game). The Speak chips keep to `conversationPeople`, so a fight has no conversation screen.
- Different words from different people (2026-10-11, the host's words: "make sure npcs have different things to say when interacting with multiple"; `distinctDialogue`/`echoes` in dm-replies.cjs, `standInGreetings` and `replyIdeas` in greetings.js, `verify-who-is-here.cjs`, `verify-greetings.cjs`): DIFFERENT WORDS in GROUP CONVERSATION and the scene-trigger phase ("never two greetings alike"); the server drops a later line of the same reply that repeats or echoes an earlier one (same words, most of the same content words, or two short lines opening alike or with the same greeting word), never the first line and never the whole reply (noted as the second look "echoed line"); written greetings give everyone present a different line and never one said in the last two turns; `replyIdeas` speaks to two different people by name when several are here, and offers each person lines of their own (by role and whether they live here); the story writer's opening lines each say something of their own and `freshStoryGame` drops a line said twice.
- Suggestions (`suggestions` in the DM reply, `cleanSuggestions` (dm-replies.cjs) and `suggestionInstructions` (dm-instructions.cjs), `replyIdeas` in greetings.js, `inBattle`/`speakAction` in quickActions.js, DungeonMaster.js with its strip `SuggestionsBar` in DmSuggestions.js, `verify-social.cjs`, `verify-greetings.cjs`, `verify-suggestions.cjs`; the host on 2026-10-11: "compress all npc pop ups appear underneath a "speak" tab. that tab itself within a "suggestions" tab that will contain all actions as well. we want this to be heavily player driven so we want the player to type most responses. this will be excluded during battle as the buttons being available helps the player to remember what they are able to do."): every reply carries 0–3 short first-person things the player might say next (last property so narration streams first; required; empty out of character). The server drops OOC/DM lines, HP figures, button talk, repeats of the player's words, duplicates and lines over 110 characters, and never refuses a reply over them. `settled()` in dm-intent returns []. Out of battle the play screen has no person chips and no action row: one slim Suggestions switch sits above the message box, closed unless the player keeps it open (`questbound.suggestions.v1` {open, tab}, per device, default closed on Say; nothing switches tab by itself, only the player's tap calls `savePanel`; a dot on the closed switch when a highlighted chip waits: an offer, Loot, Level up, an approach). Opened, it has three sub-tabs, each with its count (not under 360 px) and a quiet line when empty ("Nothing to suggest just now. Type what you say or do, in your own words."): Say (the lines; a tap fills the box with keepfocus and never sends), Speak (a chip per person here, "Take X along" offers (`join:`, now inside a conversation too), a fallen companion or party hero to revive (`give-potion:`): `speakAction(key)` decides) and Actions (every other chip: travel, rests, Loot, Level up, items, the vault, Attack… and Cast… opening in place, the ⓘ guide, the ongoing effects last). A conversation offers Say and Speak only; a dead hero gets no person chips. In battle (`inBattle(game)`: a creature fight, dying, a brawl, a party's `turnOrder`, or any `turnOrderView`) the buttons stay in view as before (Attack…, Cast…, bonus actions, End turn, Death save, the party wait row), with an Actions | Say tab pair (Actions the default; Say only appears when the DM wrote lines, since `replyIdeas` offers none in a fight; in a brawl people may shout, plead or yield). On a short screen (under 520 high) it all stays one row: the switch and icon sub-tabs (quill, speak, compass) sit before one WrapRow and step aside for an open picker. Number keys press only what is in view (the battle row or its Say lines; else the open sub-tab; nothing while Suggestions is closed). Hidden while typing on touch; Say is empty out of character, dead, dying or during another's turn. Still open for the host: whether a greeting on arrival should keep switching into the conversation by itself (it does; `conversationTarget` relies on it), and whether a first-time player should see Suggestions open once. The legacy non-fill DungeonMaster layout (unused by App.js) keeps its old list. When the latest turn has no DM suggestions for this device (another player's turn, a reload, offline), `replyIdeas(game,hero,personId)` stands in (seeded per turn; none in a fight, dying, dead or with a spell waiting). Suggestions live only in the device's memory: never saved or synced (a campaign turn returns them without storing them). Only what can happen (2026-10-21, the host's words: "the dm was giving suggestions for actions that were not available, it should only recommend actions that are capable of actually occurring"; `suggestionRules.js` `suggestionPossible`, `dmTurnRules.js` `ideaFacts`/`possibleIdeas`, `verify-suggestions.cjs`): SUGGESTIONS tells the model only what this hero can do now (a spell of theirs in spellReference with something left to cast it with, a draught or item carried, a journey among choices, a rest only when offered, an attack only in a fight or at someone here, words only to someone here). `suggestionPossible(text,facts,spellNames)` (one pure rule with no imports, a helper in `helperFiles`) drops a line naming a spell the hero does not know or has nothing left for, a draught not carried, a rest the choices do not offer, a journey in a fight or to a named place the game does not know, an attack outside a fight at nobody here, or words to (or a hail of) someone not here or unknown; talk, questions and plans stay. `cleanSuggestions(list,input,context)` in dm-replies.cjs applies it with the scene it was given (dm-replies loads suggestionRules.js as text through `rules-files.cjs moduleSource`, live per request: deploy it with the DM files), quietly, never refusing or retrying a reply; the device applies it again in DungeonMaster.js (`possibleIdeas`, memoised per fingerprint) with facts from the game and the game's own reading of the words (`dmCommand`, then a preview with `adventureStep`: no draught, no slot, a spell not known, not this hero's turn), to the Dungeon Master's lines and the stand-ins alike; `replyIdeas` only offers a journey the map allows now (`travelError`). The panel state lives in `suggestionPanel.js` (`panelKey`, `loadPanel`); since 2026-10-21 a turn sent with it closed asks for no suggestions out of battle (`suggest:false`, see Round 3 under The request in parts and a leaner turn). A written tale's own lines lead Say (its first lines on the opening turn, an open choice's lines), and Suggestions opens on Say once for a device that never touched it (see Written tales (the shelf)).
- Stories as background jobs (`story-jobs.cjs`, `writeStory` in `dmConnection.js`, `verify-story-jobs.cjs`): the game asks for a story once with `context.job` and then asks after it with `context.mode:'art'` plus `storyJob` every three seconds; see AI-SETUP.md. The story writer's effort is the host's choice in `.questbound-story-effort` (`medium` since 2026-10-20, the host's choice to lower the cost of a tale: about four fifths of a tale's output at `high`, 2026-10-02 to 10-20, was the model's thinking; change it only when the host asks). A tale takes about three minutes at that effort; `StoryLoading` says so after twenty seconds. A finished job's tale joins the compendium like any tale written for a player (`write(effort,420000).then(kept,…)`, see The compendium under Ready journeys): the job's result is then `{story, ahead:true, journey:true, added:true}` with the story's `libraryId`/`artId`, else `{story}` as before.
- A party at the shared table (`partyRules.js`, `verify-party.cjs`; 2026-10-03, stage one of a real party): every table is a party: each player who joins brings their own hero (up to four, `maxPartySize`; the one-hero kind of campaign is retired: see Joining a table with a hero). The world is shared; each hero's own state (`heroFields`: draughts, pack, slots, resources, deeds, experience, buffs, Shield and the last hit, dying or death...) lives in `game.party.members[seat]` (each member's opaque per-campaign seat, `seat-<16 hex>`, never their account id; `campaigns.cjs` gives older tables seats on first read) with their sheet, hit points, `status` (up, down, dead) and `stats` (AC, HP, from the geared hero: `statsOf`). A snapshot has one hero in the lead; the campaign server sends every member the table with their own hero in the lead (`partyView`), so the rules engine always plays one hero. `settleParty` reads the lead's state back into the members; `canonicalParty` is the same from every point of view. Foes strike any standing hero (`partyTargets`/`pickFoeTarget`; companions draw a quarter when present; nothing changes without a party) via `foeHitsPartyHero` (their AC and HP, resistance, then a subclass ward, which works on another hero's turn too; at 0 down and dying with `fight:true`, killed outright past their maximum; the hidden survival odds apply there too). A dead member can be brought back by another hero's Revivify while the party is still where they fell (see Death, revival and laying to rest); otherwise their player brings another of their heroes to their seat from the table screen (`campaign-join` with the campaign id), or leaves. `fallAtZero` marks `fight` on a fall in a fight: in a party the fight goes on for the others (`stageAfterFall`), unless nobody is standing; a hero fallen in a brawl lies dying while the others fight on. A down hero rolls death saves on their own turn while the party stays where it is (`party.stage`); another hero can give them a draught (`give-potion` with `target:'party:<id>'`, a Revive chip). Every living hero (down ones too, not the dead) gets their own full experience award from their own level for each fight won, chapter, lead, place and tale end (`withExperience`; `partyVictory` is gone); `xp` is a hero field, `xpTally` a world field, and each device's Level up appears from its own hero's xp. Saves accept a party (`validParty`) and any encounter level in one; leaving makes the adventure solo at the hero's own level (`soloFromParty`, the foe back to its numbers for one hero). The Dungeon Master gets `context.party` (name, kind, class, level, condition) and the PARTY instruction: never speak or act for another player's hero. Turns by others show the player's table name in the feed. Stage two (2026-10-03, asked for by the host; `verify-party-turns.cjs`): fights go in turns. `adventureStep` sends every party game through `partyStep` (partyTurnRules.js, with `beginPartyFight`, `passTurns`, `foeTurn` and `partyAftermath`): the first move of a fight (the opening attack) is anyone's; then `beginPartyFight` rolls initiative for every standing hero and the foe (or each person in a brawl, `npc:<id>`) and keeps the order in the shared world as `game.turnOrder` ({order:[{id,init}], at, round, dodging}; `validTurnOrder` in adventureStorage.js). A hero acts only on their own turn (out of turn the engine and the campaign server refuse, `dmChoices` offers only `party-pass`, `commitDmTurn` refuses any action; talking is free). The engine's turn options `foeWaits` (a hero's turn ends without the foe answering; the result says `turnOver`/`dodged`) and `partyFoe` (the foe's own turn: companions first, per-target Dodge and Blur, the foe keeps fighting while anyone stands) do the work; `passTurns` hands the turn on and plays the foe's turn (on the campaign server, with its dice) from a standing hero's point of view; lines about another hero are told by name (`toldOf`). A hero who took the Dodge is harder to hit until their next turn; a spent action stays with the hero whose turn it is (`settleParty` keeps it as `members[id].turn`); a down hero rolls their death save on their turn and the party stays where it is; a hero who joins mid-fight takes the last place; a won or fled fight drops the order. The host may always pass a hero's turn; any other member may once the turn has run 90 s, or at once when that hero's player has not been seen for 15 s (`party-pass`, the Skip chip; `turnClock` in `campaigns.cjs`, kept on the server and not in the snapshot; otherwise 403). Only `direct===true` is talk to the Dungeon Master out of turn. A party member who is down blocks travel (`downPartyMember`/`travelError` in mapRules.js); a member who falls or dies keeps the table valid (`validWildFight`, `validFoeAllies`, `validNpcCombat`), the others fight on (`partyFightsOn`), and `partyAftermath` releases a fallen hero's holds at once and clears marks once the fight is over (`verify-party-falls.cjs`). The play screen shows whose turn it is (`turnOrderView` in encounterRules.js: the action row's "<Name>’s turn" or "Your turn" heading, the fight banner, "<Name>’s turn." in the feed) and `PartyPanel.js` (in the table bar) shows every hero's hit points and, in a fight, the order with the foe. Healing spells reach the other heroes (`request.partyTarget` from "I cast Cure Wounds on Bram", `partyHeal` in spellBuffRules.js, a Cast… chip per hurt friend; the mass healing spells heal the whole party), and so do buffs (see Spells for friends). Not yet: concentration saves for heroes struck on another hero's turn, a per-device Shield setting for heroes struck during another's turn (they get the default, on). A next tale at a table is a new tale for everyone (`campaign-tale`, see Every table plays a tale); there is no "continue in this region" at a table yet. A table's lands are kept from tale to tale too (2026-10-23): `partyInTale(snapshot, lead, story, introId, {previous})` joins the table's previous world (`previous`, by default the host's view; null for none; a Skirmish or a table with no tale brings none) with `joinRegion` as a device does, near (4 to 7 miles) and anchored where the party stands, placed by `seededRandom('table-tale:'+<new story id>)` so the same request gives the same map; campaigns.cjs needs no change (verify-no-crossroads.cjs).
- Joining a table with a hero (2026-10-21, the host's words: "any players joining a game should either pick a character from their pre made characters or make a new one for the adventure. nobody should be "playing" the multi player function without a character to actually take control of."; `campaigns.cjs` `heroless`/`broughtHero`/`partied`, `SharedTable.js` `HeroChoice`, `App.js` `heroForTable`/`takeReadyHero`, `useHeroSaves.js` `playFromRoster(entry,{stay})`, `startTale.js` `tableSnapshot`, `verify-campaign-edits.cjs`, `verify-no-crossroads.cjs`): whoever joins or hosts first chooses the hero they will control on the table screen (Your hero for this table: the current hero, a hero waiting in the roster, a ready-made hero, or Make a new hero, which opens the builder and comes back to the table chosen); a hero other than the current one becomes this device's hero first (the current one waits in the roster), then the join or create carries that hero's solo snapshot (`tableSnapshot`: their written tale as it stands, else a fresh start with what they carry). The server refuses a join without a living hero (400), a hero already at the table (the same character, e.g. the same ready-made hero twice or a copy of another player's hero: 409) and a full party. Every new table is a party (`party:false` is ignored). A table saved as the retired one-hero kind is read as a party with the host's hero in the lead (`partied`, persisted); any member whose seat holds no hero (from such a table, or whose hero died before a new tale) gets `needsHero` in every state, is sent no snapshot (never another player's hero), may not take turns or make edits (409 `needsHero`), and brings a hero to their seat with `campaign-join` {campaignId, snapshot} (no invite needed: they are a member); the table screen shows them Bring your hero with the same choice. Members' rows show the hero each brought ("with Kara", "no hero yet": `members[].hero`). Seats, bans, mutes and receipts are unchanged.
- Every table plays a tale (`campaign-tale`, `campaigns.cjs` `tale`, `partyRules.js` `partyInTale`, `useSharedTable.js` `startTale`, `App.js` `beginTableTale`, `AdventureIntros.js` `forTable`, `verify-no-crossroads.cjs`): the host begins a tale for the whole table, host only (403 otherwise), out of a fight, never over a tale under way unless `replace:true` (the table screen asks first: Begin a new tale for the table → Yes, choose a new tale), with `baseVersion`. The body is `{journey:<uuid>}` (the account service asks the Dungeon Master as a device does: `{mode:'adventure', introId:'easy', journey, easyStart:true}` with the host's guest label, so the library's play count and the guest's tale allowance apply) or `{story, introId}` (a tale the host's device had the story writer write: Your own tale, or a written opening when no journeys are on offer). `partyInTale` sets the tale up for the host's hero (`freshStoryGame` on `newAdventure(hero, carriedBase(...))`, the lived titles refused, `storyHistory` kept), then seats every other living member's hero with what they carry (pack, draughts, training, experience, deeds); a dead member's hero is not seated (they bring a new one). The result passes `validSnapshot` and is saved like any turn; the response carries `tale` {title, libraryId} (the host's device remembers the journey as played). The host chooses on the New Adventure screen in table mode (`AdventureIntros forTable`: no Skirmish, no next-tale-in-the-region; "Choose the table’s tale"; Back to the table), opened from the table screen's Choose the tale. A table opened when the host had no written tale under way (none, the crossroads, the Skirmish) starts with no tale (`needsTale`): the host sees Choose the tale and the invite only once a tale has begun; others see "The host is choosing the table’s tale."; turns are refused (409 `needsTale`). An old table on the crossroads tale is the same: its host chooses a tale and it starts for everyone, members and heroes kept.
- No crossroads (2026-10-21, the host's words: "I've noticed that original story with 3 locations where the map is an overly basic triangle keeps appearing. get rid of that story and make sure it doesn't appear anymore, it doesn't line up with our current model of the game and doesn't allow external travel."; `storyRules.js` `hasTale`, `startTale.js` `playScreenFor`, `verify-no-crossroads.cjs`): a game with no story (`newAdventure` alone: a new hero's empty adventure, or an old save from the built-in crossroads tale: inn, bridge, tower, the lantern wisp) is never played, solo or at a table. `playScreenFor` decides where Continue, Play as and every game screen lead: the adventure only with a tale (a written story or the Skirmish), else the New Adventure screen (no hero: the heroes; at a table without a tale or a hero of one's own: the table screen); App.js redirects any game screen reached without one, `playCharacter` goes to the adventure only with a tale, the title offers Continue only with a tale (`saved&&taleNow`; the hero strip leads to New Adventure), and the area cue no longer falls back to "The Lantern at the Crossroads". The crossroads' "Begin another adventure with this character" (`restart-adventure`, `restartAdventure`) and the dead `onRestart` props are gone. Old crossroads saves still load (nothing is deleted until a new tale is started). The paths that once led there: Play as after a reload with a hero who had no tale (`newStoryRequested` false), Continue on a crossroads save, the roster or a save code holding one, the crossroads' own restart, and a table opened before a tale. `newAdventure` stays the base every tale is built on (`freshStoryGame`, `hostileEncounterGame`) and the engine keeps the crossroads moves (crossroadsRules.js) for the checks and old saves only.
- Campaign invites (2026-10-21, the host's words: "make the invite codes for multiplayer much shorter and simpler. I'm thinking 8 characters shown as 4 characters, a dash, and 4 more characters, all either numbers or capitol letters, without capitol exclusion. that way players can type lower case letters and it will count as correct if the letters are correct."; `campaigns.cjs` `newInvite`/`inviteForms`/`inviteHash`, `operations.cjs` `keyed`, `invite-guard.cjs`, `SharedTable.js` `typedInvite`/`inviteReady`, `verify-no-crossroads.cjs`): eight of A-Z and 0-9 (`crypto.randomInt`, nothing excluded), shown as XXXX-XXXX in large type with a Copy button; typed in any case, with or without the dash and spaces (the field capitalises and adds the dash as one types). Only `'v2:'+HMAC-SHA256(private storage key, code)` is kept (`inviteHash`); expiry stays 7 days. A long code from before (16 characters, case as written, an unkeyed SHA-256) still joins until it expires. Guessing (36^8, about 2.8e12): `invite-guard.cjs` counts wrong codes (`inviteMiss`) per caller (the gateway's `X-Questbound-Client`, hashed; this PC is 'desktop'/'this-pc') and overall, kept in `.questbound-invite-misses.json` across restarts: 10 an hour and 30 a day per caller, 100 an hour and 500 a day for the whole game (`inviteLimits`); past them every join by invite waits (429 with `Retry-After`, "Too many wrong invite codes from here…" / "…have been tried today…", never the busy-retry words), right codes included; joins by campaign id (members bringing a hero) are not counted. On top of that the account service's `campaign-join` quota (20 per 10 minutes per identity) and the gateway's per-browser and per-address limits still apply. At 500 wrong codes a day and at most 100 live tables, finding one by chance takes about 150,000 years.
- Named at a table of several heroes (2026-10-21, the host's words: "make sure that when a player is being talked to or interacted with, that character is named instead of just saying "you" so players know who is being interacted with and who is available to respond."; `playbackRules.js` `heroesAtTable`/`heroTold`/`toldToMe`/`addressedTo`/`visibleTurn`/`recordedTurn`, `chronicleRules.js`, `TurnPlayback.js`, `fightLines.js`, PARTY in dm-instructions.cjs, `dm-intent.cjs`, `verify-table-names.cjs`): every member reads the same feed, so at a table with more than one hero (`heroesAtTable`) a turn another member took is told of the acting hero by name. `recordedTurn` keeps on the turn `by` {seat: the acting member's seat, name: their hero's name}, on the player's line `heroName`, and on a person's line `to` (`addressedTo`: the acting hero when it says "you" and names no hero, `everyone` for "all of you"/"both of you"/"everyone"...; none when it names a hero); all optional extra fields that older builds and saves ignore. `visibleTurn(turn,game)` (DungeonMaster.js reads every turn through it) tells the engine's lines (kinds roll, initiative, action, effect) of that hero for every other member (`heroTold`: the first mention by name with the verb agreeing, then they/them/their, "vs your AC" as "vs Kara’s AC", quoted words untouched; checked against a corpus of party-fight lines), marks them `toldOf` so `describeEvent` reads the same cards (the weapon as the title, `who` for "Kara’s roll", "X attacks Kara:" as the foe's attack, "Kara searches …", "Kara has earned a level"), tells the foe's blows at the viewer's own hero back as "you" (`toldToMe`, for lines partyTurnRules.js `toldOf` wrote by name), and gives a person's line `toLabel` (the hero's name, "you", or "all of you"); the acting player keeps "You". The feed shows "KARA VELL · SAM" over another member's typed line and "MARA → KARA VELL" over a line said to one hero; the arrival plate reads "Kara travels to …" too. The Dungeon Master is told (PARTY): with context.party, never "you" in narration, the third person naming context.player and anyone the turn touches, people naming the hero they speak to ("Thalion, …") or saying "All of you …"; suggestions stay first person. The game's own one-line tellings (dm-intent's settled replies) name the hero at a table ("Kara sets out for …"). Alone, everything stays in the second person exactly as before. What to watch live: narration at a table in the third person, dialogue naming whom it addresses.
- Ready journeys (2026-10-13, the host's words: "refill the pre written tales list and make each of them a much more inviting journey, I want the players to have to think hard between which inviting journey reaches out to them the most, while not having to wait forever on new journeys and simultaneously using up less API costs overall"; `tale-library.cjs`, `topUpLibrary`/`startLibrary`/`journeyCheck`/`journeyCovers` in dm-server.cjs, `storyChecks` in rules-engine.cjs, `journeyLibrary.js`, `verify-journeys.cjs`, `journey-fixtures.cjs`; replaces the tale pantry, `tale-pantry.cjs` is gone): the Dungeon Master keeps a library of ten finished journeys (`librarySettings`) in `.questbound-tales/` (git-ignored; under QUESTBOUND_DATA when hosted) as `journey-g5-<uuid>.json` with `library-state.json`. Each journey is written once in the background at the host's story effort by `generateAdventure` with `context.ahead` (hero unknown: only "you"; people's `regard` dropped; never a story so far), as an easy start (errand schema) and with `context.libraryBrief`: the terrain and mood (`journeyMoods`, 10) the library has least of, a seed (`journeySeeds`, 24) that no journey on offer nor the last 12 written used, and the others' pitches to differ from (the READY JOURNEY paragraph, `journeyInstructions`). The writer also returns a `pitch` {hook ≤140, blurb ≤420, mood ≤40, special ≤160}, kept beside the story and trimmed or filled in by `tidyPitch`; a browser's `libraryBrief` is dropped. A journey is offered only when the rules accept it (`journeyCheck`: a ready-made Fighter seated by `freshStoryGame`, `validAdventure`, an errand, an introduction, two chapters) and its title and region are not on offer already. Many players play it, each their own copy (`start`: a new story id, `libraryId`, `artId:'journey-<id>'`); it retires after 20 plays (8 until 2026-10-20, when the host chose more players per journey so fewer are written) or 14 days (then it stays in the compendium: see below) and is replaced one at a time: freely on the first fill and below 6 on offer, else at most 4 a day (attempts count), pausing ten minutes after a failure. Topped up on DM start (`createServer` → `startLibrary`, live key only; `QUESTBOUND_LIBRARY=off` skips only this start-up fill), on every live turn and on every listing, by the host's writer (`adventure-ahead`, no guest label), only while `spending.background()` allows it (see Spending ceilings) and the library is not paused. The pause (2026-10-22, the stories spec Phase 0; `libraryPaused` in dm-server.cjs, `verify-journeys.cjs`): while `.questbound-library-paused` exists (any content, under QUESTBOUND_DATA when hosted; `Questbound.cmd -PauseLibrary` writes it, `-ResumeLibrary` removes it) no new journey is written by a top-up, the start-up fill, a listing or a turn, read live with no restart, and again before each write, so a fill under way stops at its next journey (not noted as a failure); journeys already written are still listed and started; `node dm-report.cjs` says whether it is paused. The host decides between pausing until the writer gets the written-tale structure and letting the library refill from the same formula. The game asks `{mode:'art', journeys:'list'}` (pitches only, never secrets; a cover only when its picture costs nothing: already painted, or a ready-made place picture) and starts one with `{mode:'adventure', introId:'easy', journey:<id>, easyStart:true}` (answered at once with `{story, journey:true}`; 409 when it has left the library). A game from before the library asking for `easy` or `surprise` gets any journey on offer (with none on offer, a tale from the compendium). `revision` 6 (bump it with dm-server's `loadLibrary` literal; 5 since a pause stopped counting as a failed write, 2026-10-22; 6 since the compendium, 2026-10-23) and `generation` 5 (raise it when the writer's output changes shape; earlier generations and the old per-opening stock are cleared when the library is made). Since the Written tales shelf (2026-10-22, see Written tales (the shelf)), the journeys sit folded under it ("More journeys from the story writer"), a journey id is tried on the written shelf first, and an older game's Easy to get into or Let fate decide gets a Short written tale before any journey.
  - **The compendium** (2026-10-23, the host's words: "once the player completes the story or decides to move to a new area, new potential stories should be written. every time a new story is written, it should be added to the compendium of potential stories to save on data usage."; the campaign spec G4, C:/Users/itsbr/qb-work/notes/campaign/SPEC.md; `tale-library.cjs` `add`/`match`/`trimmed`, dm-server.cjs `taleLand`/`taleMatch`/`sharedTale`/`keptTale`, written-shelf.cjs `match`, adventure-generator.cjs `landOf`, `verify-journeys.cjs`, `verify-written-tales.cjs`): the library's folder is the compendium. A retired journey is never deleted: it is no longer listed but is still started by its id and matched. Every tale the story writer writes for a player is added (`source:'player'`, straight into the compendium as retired, so the list on offer stays the library's own varied ten; entries from before carry no `source` and read as `'ahead'`). At most `librarySettings.compendium` 500 tales; past that the retired ones least played go first (the longest unplayed, `playedAt`, first among equals), never a journey on offer and never the tale just added. `add(story,{pitch,brief,source,accept})` refuses a non-story, one `accept` (journeyCheck) refuses, and a title or region already in the compendium (`{ok:false,kind}`: no-story, source, rejected, too-alike, unwritten). `match({terrain,exclude,titles})` (read only; `start` counts the play) returns an entry on offer or retired of exactly that terrain (no terrain is related to another in the rules; none given: any), never one in `exclude` (ids the device played) nor one whose title is in `titles` (any case), the least played first, then by id; written-shelf.cjs `match` is the same over the listed shelf tales (least started, then the shelf's order). The Dungeon Master (`generate`, mode adventure, no `journey`): a request that carries `continuing` or `context.land` {name, terrain} (or an older game's `easy`/`surprise` with nothing on offer) is first matched (`taleMatch`: the land's terrain, else for `continuing` the summary's `land` or `region`/`terrain` when the game sends them; never blind: a next tale with no terrain is written; the written shelf first, then the compendium; `context.playedTales` (library and shelf ids, at most 200) and `previousTitles` plus `previousStory.title` excluded) and a match answers at once `{story, ahead:true, journey:true, matched:true}` (`written:true` from the shelf), the player's own copy, no AI and no `'tale'` allowance (the host's ceilings and pause still hold; a tale gone meanwhile is written and counted). Never matched: the player's own idea and a chosen written opening. Everything that is written (`sharedTale`) is written the ahead way whatever the device sent: `{mode:'adventure', introId, ahead:true, easyStart:true, libraryBrief, land?, idea?, job?, choices, variation}` and nothing else (no `player`, `storySoFar`, `continuing`, `previousStory`, `previousTitles` or `playedTales`), so the tale suits any hero; the brief is the library's own (`brief()`, else one made from its lists) with the land's terrain, without a seed for an idea or a chosen opening (their own direction).
