With `context.land` the writer is told LAND (adventure-generator.cjs `landInstructions`) and the story's `region` and `terrain` are set to the land's (`landOf`: a name of letters, spaces and a little punctuation, 2 to 60, and one of the nine terrains; anything else is dropped). The land's name is checked by the content check with the idea (`typedByPlayer`). The written tale is then added (`keptTale`, journeyCheck) and answered as a journey start, `{story, ahead:true, journey:true, added:true}` (its `libraryId`/`artId` shared); a refused or failed add still answers `{story}`. The Easy start switch no longer changes a written tale (every one begins with orders in hand). The guest who asked pays for the write as before (`'tale'` allowance, usage `adventure` with their label); the pause (`libraryPaused`) stops only background writing. A next tale in a region may now cast library people (the old people come with joinRegion; the writer cannot know whom the hero met).
- The ready journeys on the New Adventure screen (`AdventureIntros.js` `JourneyShelf`/`JourneyCard`, `journeyLibrary.js`, `App.js`): when journeys are on offer the list is Your own tale, then **Ready journeys** (a card each: the cover when free, mood, "N chapters", "Ready now", title, region and terrain, hook, blurb, the special line; Begin this journey under the chosen card; two columns from 680 px of content width, which widens to 1080 px on this screen, one on phones, never sideways), then Let fate decide (one unplayed journey by chance, at once: `fateJourney`), then the Skirmish; Easy to get into and the written openings are offered only when the library cannot be reached (an older DM, offline, or none yet). App.js asks for the journeys whenever the screen opens and moves the selection to the first journey unless the player chose Your own tale, Let fate decide or the Skirmish. Easy start shows on and locked for a journey. A device never offers a journey it has played (`questbound.journeys.v1` per account profile, the last 200, written by `rememberJourney` once the tale is saved) nor one whose title is in the hero's `storyHistory`. At a shared table the host chooses the table's tale on this screen (table mode; see Every table plays a tale).
- Written tales (format) (2026-10-22, the build specification's Phase B, "Freeze the format first: half a day, as WRITTEN-TALES.md plus written-fixture.json"; revised the same day after two reviews; `WRITTEN-TALES.md`, `stagingRules.js`, `check-written-tale.cjs`, `written-fixture.json`, `verify-written-format.cjs`): the format of a hand-written tale for the coming Written tales shelf (Short, Medium and Long), frozen so writers can start. The runtime's engine and the Dungeon Master's side landed the same day (Written tales (runtime), below); `written-shelf.cjs` and the shelf on screen come next.
  - **The file.** `.questbound-written/<slug>.json` (git- and docker-ignored by `.questbound-*`; a real tale never goes in the public repository, where every twist would be readable): `{format:1, id (a fixed UUID), slug, edition, order, listed, addedAt, length, minutes, difficulty, party, levels, firstTale?, notes?, shape, pitch, story, walkthrough}`. `story` is what the story writer returns, without what the game sets (`id`, `status`, `written`, `libraryId`, `artId`, `foeStats`, `errand`, `regard`...), within the written-tale limits (opening at most 450, a chapter's turn 200, secret 600, the introduction at half its limits, 1–2 opening lines, every prose text ending on a full sentence), plus `foeVoice` (at most 300) and `staging` (the chapters' finds, beats, complications, pressure steps, set pieces and choices; what ticks the clock; stakes, leads, the boss, endings). `shape` ({engine, twist, endings}) places a tale on the variety grid, and `walkthrough` scripts the golden path and a path to every ending for the checker (never served). WRITTEN-TALES.md is the authoritative description: every field and limit, the condition and effect vocabularies with their exact JSON, a turn in order (4.4), the tiers, the name, text and picture rules, the craft rules and the critics' rubrics, `game.pace`, what was decided and what the catalogue must change (16). A writer authors from it and their tale's brief (its catalogue entry and its line of the name register). Change it in the same change as the validators (verify-written-format.cjs checks that every condition and effect kind is shown there).
  - **Two levels of checking** (`stagingRules.js`, a rules file listed in `rulesFiles` after storyLog.js; nothing at its top level reads another file's values). `validStaging(story)` (in `validStory`) and `validPace(pace,game)` (in `validAdventure`) run on every build, every save and every table snapshot, and check **structure and size only** (`stagingHardLimits`: texts 1000, lists 16, depth 6, 32 conditions in one, 64,000 characters of JSON; ids of their fixed shape, unique): a condition of a kind the build does not know never holds, an effect of one does nothing, an unknown due kind means nothing is due, and staging and pace `v` may be any whole number from 1, so a later build's tale or save loads on an older device, an open tab or a campaign server not yet restarted, and a rollback keeps every save. A later version only adds kinds and keys, never changes what one means, never removes a pace key, never changes an id's shape. `stagingProblems(story,{strict:true})` is the authoring checker's mode: the whole vocabulary, every authoring limit (`stagingLimits`), references and unknown keys. Also: `beatHolds(game,cond,pace)` (whether a condition holds, total: never throws, fuzzed), `openingPace(game)` (a chapter's pace as it opens: where the hero stands counts as reached), `validWrittenTag` (a later length accepted), `stagingIds` (exact names to ids, memoised), `stagingNamesUsed`, `stagingLeaves`, and the vocabularies: `stagingConditionKinds` (reach, at, clear, won, searched, checked, spoke, met, with, fate, foe, carry, mark, flag, told, step, rest, days, level, any, all, not), `stagingEvents` (reach, won, searched, checked and rest hold once they have happened since the chapter began: `pace.seen`), `stagingEffectKinds` (bring, send, arm, place, stake, give, attitude, offer, fact, lead, flag, decide, clock, advance, end, boss), `stagingMechanical`, `paceDueKinds`, `stagingPacing`, `stagingTicks`. `game.pace` (world state, never a hero field) is {v, ch, turns, quiet, quietMax, since, day, step, owed, held, due, told, fired, seen, marks, flags, stakes, spoke, offers, leads, sign, fight, ending, clockAt} (WRITTEN-TALES.md 14; clockAt optional, added with the runtime). freshStoryGame seats it and the Dungeon Master never reads it (Written tales (runtime)). A story a table's host posts keeps none (`campaigns.cjs` `tale` strips `staging` and `written`; written tales come by their journey id).
  - **The checker** (deterministic: no AI, no ports; the libraries are only read): `node check-written-tale.cjs <tale.json> [--json] [--people <dir>] [--creatures <dir>] [--places <dir>] [--shelf <dir>]`, from a checkout or worktree of the build branch. The folders default to the live data folders under QUESTBOUND_DATA, the checkout, then the git repository's main checkout (so a worktree finds them); a missing people, creature or place library fails, a missing shelf warns. Checks: 1 shape and limits; 2 seating (the ready-made Fighter, Rogue, Wizard and Cleric at the tale's lowest, middle and highest level through the developer options, parties of 2 and 4 through `partyInTale`; every resident where `at` says, opening lines, landmarks and lands kept, nothing tidied away); 3 names (every staged name resolves; unique in the tale and across the shelf, people's name parts too; the avoid list; the deny lists: other publishers' names anywhere, and verify-subclasses.cjs's subclass names in proper names, which the checker copies and verify-written-format keeps in step; no part of a ready-made hero's name; people cast from the library keep its name, species and gender); 4 structure (finds: two different routes, one mechanical, talk standing alone only in two exchanges, a warning with someone at the start, and in an `all` only beside deeds; no route that holds as its chapter opens, chapter 1 read exactly with `beatHolds` against every seated hero; none that can never hold in its chapter by order of what the tale gives, arms, decides and tells; `clear` of a place armed earlier only when armed again; beats and choices that are their chapter's own route, beats waiting for each other's telling, conditions that cannot hold together, options settled at once, leads that close at once or never, endings never chosen; keeper and mara never moved by `bring`/`send`, `place` and `arm` never at the three starting places, a place armed once a chapter; the clock's stall bound (steps × drift: 24, 40, 48), `quiet` at most the tier's default plus 2, a hard tale's `ticks.rest`; the staging's size (16,000, 32,000, 48,000); the tier's budgets, options a choice (3, 3, 4), chapter 1's set piece 0.1–0.3 mile out (with `otherwise` when it waits for a level); the shelf's ids, Start here tale and variety); 6 pictures (the cover's kind and terrain unique on the shelf, every creature (`otherwise` too) fits the creature library by `fitsFor`, every place the place library, every person of the thirteen common peoples); 7 text (the opening ends on a demand or question, at most 900 characters read before the first move, no digits or rule talk in tell, line, sign and colour, no rule talk in what the player reads from the staging, a warning elsewhere; rule talk is matched in its rule sense by `ruleTalk`, so "round the fire", "turn back", "level ground", "in check" stay free; overused words once). 5 walkthroughs (their shape and names: steps go, say, fight, confront, attack, search, check, rest, choose, join, find, advance, lead, wait; then played through the rules: see Written tales (runtime)) and 9 experience (warnings); 8 (balance) plays the tale's fights (see Written tales (fight shape)). Exit 0 on a pass (warnings allowed), 1 on a failure. `verify-written-format.cjs` runs the fixture and some 120 broken variants (every broken tale the reviewers wrote among them) against libraries it makes itself (no host data needed), what the format allows, later builds' tales and paces, `beatHolds` case by case, and a fuzz of the validators and `beatHolds`.
  - **Decided while freezing it** (where the specification was open or contradicted itself; WRITTEN-TALES.md 16): `{"foe":"slain"|"subdued"|"beaten"}` counts as a mechanical route; events count for the chapter (`pace.seen`), `{"at"}` is "here now"; a long rest ticks the clock only with `ticks.rest` (every hard tale, as its card says; any tale may), `ticks.days` steps a day; Medium and Long clocks are planned per chapter (each chapter's last step closes it, so nothing stalls); `{"clock": 1|-1}`, a pressure step's `if` and `spared`, an option's `if`, a set piece's `fromLevel` and `otherwise`, `{"level"}`, `{"foe":"bloodied"}`, `hold`, `boss.if`/`objective`/`deadline`/`hold` express the catalogue; at most four endings and four options (Long only); talk may stand alone in two exchanges; arming a place again makes it uncleared; an `offer` waits until the person is with the hero; keeper and mara are never moved; `found.item` is given however the chapter closes; one thing is due at a time, `{kind, id}`, a beat marked told by the id its request carried; the variety grid became the file's `shape` slugs; the subclass deny list applies to proper names only; `<slug>.json` is required on the shelf, a warning elsewhere; regard is never written. The catalogue needs: S4 four endings, not five; S5's "Pip Saddler" renamed (the ready-made rogue) and its sent person a resident; S6's species `Human`; S1 and S6 as specified break the gentle definition (a warning); beats that are their chapter's own route moved.
- Written tales (runtime) (2026-10-22, the build specification's Phase B engine rows, SPEC.md 2.3 to 2.7, 3.1 to 3.4; WRITTEN-TALES.md 4.4, 14 and 17; `stagingRules.js`, `written-walk.cjs`, `verify-staging.cjs`): the engine plays a written tale's plan, and the Dungeon Master performs what it decides. The shelf that serves them, the screens, the Quest tab and the counts are in Written tales (the shelf), below; a tale with no staging plays exactly as before.
  - **Seating and the turn.** `freshStoryGame` ends with `seatStaging`: chapter 1's set piece armed (`fight` at a landmark or far place: a lair with the creature by the errand's level rules, `fromLevel`/`otherwise`), the stakes as written, `game.pace`; the opening card shows the introduction's where, what is happening and what to do first (the journal and the Dungeon Master keep all five). `settleTurn` runs `withBeats(before,after,hero,{action,conversation})` right after the step and before experience (so a chapter it closes pays), only where the move is made (the device alone, the campaign server at a table, never `applyRemote`), in the order of WRITTEN-TALES.md 4.4: the beat the requests carried is told; counters (`turns` and drift count calm moves only, events latched for the chapter in `pace.seen` for named places, `spoke` from Dungeon-Master-told dialogue, quiet: talk +1, other quiet moves +2, something happening 0; steps owed by drift, `ticks.rest` and road days; a rest does not move the map's clock); a choice the player made applies its option's `then`; a chapter closed outside (the DM's `story-advance`: the next chapter's opening due); the find (closes the chapter with advanceChapter, gives `found.item`, then the chapter's own `then` and its choice's `late` from the next chapter's opening, arms the next set piece; the last chapter completes the tale, sets `late` when the clock ended it and picks the ending: `endingOf`, the first that holds, else the last; "Adventure complete" and "Ending: <title>." lines); beats; the choice (an option's `when` settles it; else it opens when `opens` holds and nothing is due); the clock (at least two calm moves apart: an owed step, else on quiet a complication that may, else the next step; `clock -1` holds one back; a step whose `if` fails is spared; the last step closes the chapter, the last chapter's ends the tale late, whatever they say). Then leads closed by their `done` (paid once, open from their `opens` chapter or an effect) and offers that wait for their person (a `joinOffers` entry once they are with the hero, until they join). One thing is due at a time (`pace.due` {kind, id}; a closing step's id rides with the truth or the ending); nothing fires, opens or ticks in a fight. Every effect is applied by kind in a fixed order (advance and end last), each skipped when it cannot apply (keeper and mara are never moved, nothing armed or changed at a starting place, never armed where the hero is fighting, a place armed once a chapter; `boss` sends the foe's minions off or hurts it: see Written tales (fight shape)). Pure and total (fuzzed).
  - **Which beat was told.** Each request names the beat its scene carries (`beatSent`: none on a scene cue, out of character, for a spell awaiting a ruling; in a fight only the fight's own: see Written tales (fight shape)); `useDmTurn.js` (`request`) and `server-turn.cjs` (`post`) record them and the commit carries them (`conversation.beats`), and only those are marked told, so nothing is told twice. Both apply `stagedPreview` to every preview (a move's, a check's, a join's, a spell's) before the game tells a routine move and before a request is built, so a beat this move fires rides its own reply; a check or a join that brings a beat due asks for a telling (not the outcome written ahead). `routineTelling` (fightLines.js) gives way while a beat is due (unless the reply that chose the move carried it). When the Dungeon Master cannot answer, the game tells the move with the beat's own line (`withBeatLine`, `conversation.beatTold`).
  - **Choices, the foe, the map.** While a choice is open (out of fights) `dmChoices` offers `tale-choice:<choice>:<option>` ("The player commits to: <label> (ONLY when …)", options whose `if` holds), never a chip (quickActions.js), kept within the thirty (`offeredChoices` drops the rest first), accepted by the campaign server as a DM choice; the move (stepHandler `'tale-choice:'`, `taleChoiceStep`) records it and withBeats applies it ("You chose: <label>."; `decide` and `late`: "Left to fate: <label>."). The foe is held back to its chapter (`bossOpen`: `confrontFrom`, default the last, and `boss.if`): no `approach` or attack at the stronghold before, and the engine refuses them (`bossBlocked`). The road beyond the map is barred before the last chapter, never refused (`noBeyond`, by default for short and medium: `beyondBarred`, a fight on arrival; see Beyond the map). A written tale offers no `story-complete`. `introducePerson` refuses the foe's name with or without "The".
  - **The Dungeon Master.** `storyWorld` leaves `staging` and `written` out of `context.story` (dm-server's `sceneFor` too); `quest` gains the chapter's `where`, `agenda`, `pace` ("Short tale, chapter 1 of 2.") and `bossLater` (`stagingQuest`; with what lasts the tale: it changes once a chapter), and only open leads; `context.taleNow` {beat, stakes, sign} (`taleNow`), which `situation()` (dm-intent.cjs) turns into `now` lines (At stake, Out of sight, beyond reach) and dm-server makes the turn's note (`beatNote` in dm-instructions.cjs, when the model's own telling is kept; see The review of 2026-10-22 for `beatUntold`: STORY BEAT THIS TURN for a beat, the chapter's truth with what closed it and the next chapter's opening, a choice's question, or the ending; after the scene and before NARRATOR THIS TURN; never in the standing prompt); briefer keeps `where` with the truth and `agenda`, `pace`, `bossLater` out of fights; `encounter.objective` is a set piece's or the boss's objective. The WRITTEN TALES paragraph is in `questInstructions` (the standing prompt 9015 → 9159 tokens; verify-dm-cache's budget 9101 → 9160, STORY BEAT THIS TURN turn-only, a written tale's calm and fight turns held to 2270 and 1070). verify-dm-trim measures a staged Long tale at its largest in the largest world (under 64 kB).
  - **Around it.** calmLines.js: a written place's `colour` replaces the arrival line, and the clock's latest sign is added once to a journey or a rest the game tells. The story log keeps stakes changed, choices and the ending (`stagingLogLines`). `taleSayLines` (firstSay on the opening turn, an open choice's `say` lines) leads the Say lines (see Written tales (the shelf)). `writtenCheck(story)` is the shelf's check of a copy (seated, valid, every name resolving, nothing at a starting place, keeper and mara never moved). rules-engine.cjs exports withBeats, stagedPreview, stagingChoices, validStaging, writtenCheck, beatKey, beatSent, withBeatLine, taleNow, endingOf.
  - **The review of 2026-10-22** (three reviewers, about 600 adversarial games and 844 requests; `verify-staging.cjs`, `verify-dm-intent.cjs`, `verify-written-tales.cjs`):
    - **The preview and the commit agree whatever the reply adds.** `commitDmTurn` returns `stepped` (the game as the rules left it, before the reply's relationships, loot, introduction and facts) and `settleTurn` passes it to `withBeats`, which decides quiet from what the engine did (before, the reply's gold or a person met reset quiet at the commit, so a pressure step the preview fired, and the Dungeon Master told, never fired; with the last steps a chapter's truth was told and the chapter stayed open). Who spoke counts at once on a move of talk alone (no preview), else after this move's beats are decided (from the next move, as the preview counted). And what a request of the move carried that has not fired (the chapter's next beat, complication, pressure step or choice; a `turn:`/`ending:` key names what closed it) fires at the commit as the preview fired it (`stgCarried`, after the find, as in the preview; `stgNextStep` is the clock's step, shared).
    - **story-advance only after the chapter's own part** (`stagingAdvanceOpen`): in a written tale `dmChoices` offers it only once the chapter's set piece, when one was armed, is beaten (its place cleared or won) and its choice, when it has one, is made; until then `quest` carries no `truth` or `revealedWhenAchieved` (dmSceneRules.js), so the truth reaches the Dungeon Master as the STORY BEAT that closes the chapter. (Before, story-advance on the first move skipped a set piece, paid the chapter's experience and let a choice lapse unasked.) WRITTEN TALES says a chapter's truth is told only as its STORY BEAT or with story-advance once offered, and that the foe is heard only in narration, in story.foeVoice (the foe can never be a speaker: `introducePerson` refuses its name). The standing prompt 9159 → 9184 tokens (verify-dm-cache's budget 9160 → 9185). written-walk plays a walkthrough's `{"advance": true}` before then as an outside close (the engine's own story-advance) and check 5 warns that no player can take that path (the drafts' ending paths that skip chapters this way).
    - **Not in a brawl:** `encounter-attack`/`encounter-throw` at the foe are not offered while `npcCombat` is active at the stronghold (the engine left the foe untouched and the commit refused the move; on main too).
    - **Leads not yet open stay hidden:** the Quest tab lists and counts only leads open now (`leadOpenNow`), `noteLead` refuses one not yet open (its hook would reach the journal), the table's `lead` edit answers 400 "That lead is not open yet.", and replyIdeas (greetings.js) names only an open lead.
    - **A beat the game's own line replaced is not told:** when dm-server answers with the game's own telling (dm-intent `settled` with a line of its own, or the rescue's "The moment settles as the dice fell.") on a turn whose note carried a beat, the reply says `beatUntold:true`; `useDmTurn.js` and `server-turn.cjs` then do not record the key, so the beat stays due for the next reply.
    - **The shelf refuses a chapter with no pressure steps** (`writtenCheck`: it could stall for ever; the non-strict `validStaging` allows none).
    - **The checker for newcomers:** check 4 refuses minions and a foe that calls for help (`bloodied.kind` call) in a gentle tale (they were warnings), and check 8 refuses the Start here tale when its chapter-1 set piece is won under 80% or kills in one fight in ten or more for any of the four ready-made heroes alone at its lowest level (`firstFight`; last-ferry's wolf: a Wizard won 68% and died in 13%; its author must lighten it, e.g. a lone bandit, with `fromLevel` and `otherwise`). The gentle target of 90% stays a warning: no creature meets it for a level-1 Wizard alone (a bandit 80%, a skeleton 83%, a goblin 50%). Easing a lone hero's first fight at level 1 in the engine would touch the survival tuning and is left to the host. Both fail drafts that the authors must change (last-ferry, prize-pig-of-wending-fair: a gentle foe that calls).
    - **What it costs** (corrected): the provider caches none of the scene (verify-dm-cache), so a written tale's own scene fields (`quest.where/agenda/pace/bossLater`, `foeVoice`) are paid in full: about 110 to 190 tokens a Dungeon Master turn (verify-dm-cache's counter overshoots by about a quarter), $0.00001 to $0.00002, not the specification's $0.0000035 cached; a beat note rides 25 to 40% of golden-path moves (not 1 in 5); moves sent to the Dungeon Master only because a beat was due are 0 to 2 a tale (well under 0.06 a move). Net about +5 to 8% a turn, under the +20% goal. (SPEC.md 3.3/3.5 still carries the old table: it is outside this worktree.)
  - **The checker plays it** (`written-walk.cjs`, check 5 and 9): the golden path, each ending's path, a passive and a violent player, move by move as a device sends them (preview and staging, the game's tellings, requests that carry the due beat, settleTurn), seeded (1 to 3), by the ready-made Fighter at the tale's lowest level taking each level; `choose` is said again while something else is due; a lost fight is fought again after a rest. `node check-written-tale.cjs <tale> --walk golden` prints one walk move by move. verify-written-format plays the fixture's walks (its variants skip them: `walks:false`); verify-staging.cjs checks the engine (seating, events, every effect, one due at a time, preview against commit, the clock and the stall bound, choices, the foe, story-advance, what the Dungeon Master reads, a beat at a table through server-turn.cjs, a rollback onto main's rules from before, the 167 fixture saves, fuzz).
- Written tales (fight shape) (2026-10-22, the build specification's Phase C, SPEC.md 6; WRITTEN-TALES.md 4, 6, 13 and 14; `stagingRules.js` `withFightShape`/`fightFloor`/`bossRaged`/`fightShape`/`stagedThreat`, `storyRules.js` `storyBossStats`/`bossShape`, `encounterRules.js` `aloneFoe`/`foeScaling.alone`, `tale-balance.cjs`, `fight-tactics.cjs`, `verify-fight-shape.cjs`): a written tale's set pieces and its foe are fights with a shape, played by the engine after every step (`adventureStep` → `withFightShape`, before the spoils; on a device's preview, its commit and the campaign server alike; nothing rolls there). survivalOdds and foeEase are never touched.
  - **The foe.** A staged tale's foe is `storyBossStats(level,type,cls)`: the story foe's numbers with more hit points and, from `bossShape.secondFrom` (3), a second blow a turn (`foeStats.swings`, 1 to 4; `validFoeStats` accepts it, older builds ignore it). Met alone it is sized to the hero's class (`bossShape.alone`, by level 1 to 12, the last after: the Fighter, Barbarian and Paladin's foe has 1 to 2.5 times the hit points and the second blow from level 3; the others' none of it, and hit points tuned per level, as low as 0.3 times for a Rogue of level 12: the engine does not play a hero Rogue's Sneak Attack (classFeatureCatalog.json automation reference), so a Rogue alone falls further behind with every level; the Warlock, Bard and Monk have tables of their own since the review of 2026-10-22 (they had the Rogue's, which made the foe a formality: all won, 3 to 25% spent at levels 5 to 12; now 80 to 100% won with 18 to 62% spent from level 5), and the classes with no ready-made hero still take the nearest one's); at a table (`freshStoryGame(…,{party:true})` from `partyInTale`, `foeForLevel` with no class) it is `bossShape.hp` (1, 1.1, then 1.2 from level 3) with no second blow of its own (the side's scaling adds the blows: two of its own scaled for a pair were four, and a pair with a Wizard lost nearly every fight). `foeForLevel(game,level,cls)` grows it by the same rule (App.js passes the hero's class). `scaledFoe` keeps a foe's own blows and adds to them. Stories with no staging keep `storyFoeStats` exactly.
  - **Blows against a hero alone, by class** (`foeScaling.alone`, `aloneFoe` in encounterFoe and for the creature at a foe's side): a creature's damage bonus is softer against a lone Rogue, Bard or Warlock (0.85), Monk, Druid or Artificer (0.9), Wizard or Sorcerer (0.75); never below +0, never harder than written, nothing with a party or anyone fighting beside the hero; worked out live, nothing kept. Measured with `balance-check.cjs` (150 fights a cell, the ready-made heroes at 1, 3 and 5, `--alone off` for before): mean wins 87% → 90%, falls 13% → 10%, deaths 4% → 3%, deadly cells 4 → 1 (a level-5 Wizard against an orc 33% → 69%, a level-3 Rogue against a wolf 69% → 90%); at level 1 the bonuses are too small for it to matter. Also below level 3 no pack and no orc springs out of a risky place for any class (it was only for heroes under 10 hit points), and an orc or a pack the Dungeon Master sends as an ambush comes as a lighter creature of its kind (`errandSingles`, as an errand's lair).
  - **Minions** (`boss.minions`): seated as `game.foeAllies` when the fight with the foe begins (approach, or a blow at the stronghold), from `fightShape.minionsFrom` (2), a pack as one of its kind and the orc a goblin below 3, each a creature `fightShape.minionDrop` (4) levels below the hero (kept as the ally's `level`, read by `allyStats`; older builds read it at the hero's level) at 60% of its kind's hit points times the side's strength; the foe pays for them (`bossBudget`: a fifth of its own hit points for each minion that stands at this level, 15% for a rage, 10% for a call), so foe and minions are one fight; they scatter when the foe falls and leave with the fight when the hero runs or falls (adventureStepCore); met again they come again, unless all were beaten (`boss:minions` in `pace.fired`, also set by the effect `{"boss": {"minions": false}}`, which sends them off mid-fight too). `{"boss": {"hurt": true}}`: a quarter of its hit points gone when the fight first begins.
  - **Bloodied, once** (`bloodied:boss`): the first time the foe is at half its (scaled) hit points or less. `rage`: one blow more a turn (`bossRaged` → `encounterFoe`); `call`: one creature joins beside it in a free place (`bloodied.ally`, else the first minion; from `fightShape.callFrom`, 2); `flee`: the fight is over (stage back to `bridge`, nothing of it kept: initiative, the opening, a spent action, marks, the turn order, its minions go with it), the foe has at least seven tenths of its hit points when met again (`fightShape.fledShare`) and is gone after with Approach; it breaks away once.
  - **Deadlines and holds** (a set piece's `fight.deadline`/`hold`, the foe's `boss.deadline`/`hold`): `pace.fight` {turns, src, due, close} counts the fight's rounds (a round, or a party's turn-order round). A hold's `tell` is told as the fight begins and the creature cannot be brought below 1 hit point until `rounds` have passed (`fightFloor`, read by `creatureRound`: "will not go down yet"); then its `then` applies. A deadline's `then` applies once `turns` have passed (its `tell` told then). Either's `advance` or `end` waits for the fight to be over and then closes the chapter (`withBeats`, `close`). Each fires once.
  - **Told in the fight.** A fight's own due thing (deadline, hold, bloodied: `pace.fight.due`, key `bloodied:boss`, `deadline:c2`…) is the one beat that is due in a fight (`beatDue`); the move that brings it goes to the Dungeon Master (`routineTelling` gives way) with STORY BEAT THIS TURN (in the fight) as the note (`beatNote`), or the game's `line` when it cannot be reached; a request that carried it marks it told. A foe that broke away is due next out of the fight. Companions follow the hero's intent: a foe the hero set out to take alive is subdued by a companion's blow too, for the rest of that turn (`subdueTurn`, a turn field cleared by endHeroTurn and fightOver; a bonus action or End turn may come between), and "knock <a name of up to four words> out" is that intent (dmCommands.js subdueIntent).
  - **Balance** (`tale-balance.cjs`, used by `node balance-check.cjs --tale <file>` and check 8 of `check-written-tale.cjs`): each chapter's set piece (by its level rules: `stagedThreat`) and the foe (minions, bloodied, hold; a foe that breaks away gone after at once), for the ready-made Fighter, Rogue, Wizard and Cleric at the tale's lowest, middle and highest level, alone and with one more hero (seated as a table seats it), through `adventureStep` with the plain tactics of `fight-tactics.cjs` (shared with balance-check). Check 8 (40 fights a cell, seeded; `--balance N`, `--no-balance`): the targets as warnings (fights won: gentle 90%, steady 80%, hard 70%; the foe alone costs 50–80% of hit points, 5–15% falls, under 3% deaths; provisional) and an error when the ready-made Fighter alone at the tale's lowest level wins under half its fights or dies in one in five or more. balance-check's heroes are now made as the checks make them (`readyHeroAt`: a caster's spells for its level; before, a caster kept its level-1 spells at every level, which made the Wizard look far weaker at 3 and 5 than it is).
  - **Measured** (2026-10-22, `tale-balance.cjs`, the ready-made heroes at levels 1 to 6 and 8, 150 fights a cell). The tale's foe with no shape, alone: before (the story foe) a Fighter spent 7 to 25% of their hit points and never fell, a Rogue or Wizard of level 6 or more won 13 to 40%; now every class wins 73 to 99% (most 85 to 99%), spends 19 to 71% (from level 3 mostly 42 to 62%), falls 1 to 27% and dies 0 to 7% (level 1 and 2 cost least: a new hero is not pressed). At levels 9 to 12 (Long tales): 76 to 100% won, 46 to 71% spent. A foe that breaks away (the hero resting before going after it) is won 79 to 100% for 16 to 60%. With minions (drafts with one or two, levels 3 to 8, before the minions were made lighter and paid for) a Fighter won 29 to 53% and the others as little as 0%; now a Fighter wins 85 to 100% and the others 50 to 100%. A pair at a table against the foe: a Fighter or Cleric with anyone 98 to 100%, a Rogue with a Wizard and a Wizard with a Cleric 40 to 95% (the pair loses most when its weaker hero falls first; a side's scaling never shrinks). Set pieces are the creatures as before (with the blows by class). All of it is provisional; `balance-check.cjs --tale` and check 8 report it per tale.
- Written tales (the shelf) (2026-10-22, the build specification's Phase B shelf rows, SPEC.md 1.1 to 1.8 and 2.8; `written-shelf.cjs`, `shelfRules.js`, `journeyLibrary.js`, `AdventureIntros.js` `TaleShelf`/`TaleCard`, `TaleFinished.js`, `TaleState.js`, `verify-written-tales.cjs`): the tales are served for good from `.questbound-written/` (QUESTBOUND_DATA when hosted; git- and docker-ignored by `.questbound-*`; a copy in the host's Desktop folder "Questbound written tales": copy a tale there when it goes on the shelf, and never delete that copy).
  - **The shelf** (`written-shelf.cjs`, stays loaded: `revision` 2 (since `match`, 2026-10-23: see The compendium), the literal in dm-server's `loadWritten`; bump both together. Its check runs the rules through rules-engine.cjs `storyChecks()`, which the running Dungeon Master keeps loaded with rules-files.cjs (neither is reloaded per request, and the revision literal does not reach them): the first deploy of the shelf, and any later change to the rules a tale's copy is checked by, reaches the shelf only after a DM restart; until then every tale is refused as `rules` and the listing's `written` is empty. `writtenCheck` is not the authoring checker: put a tale in the folder only once `check-written-tale.cjs` passes it with `--shelf` on that folder). Reads every `<slug>.json` but `plays.json`, again within 30 s of a change; serves a file only when its own fields are sound (`fileProblem`: format 1, a UUID, the file named `<slug>.json`, edition, order, listed, addedAt, length, minutes, difficulty, party, levels, firstTale, notes, the pitch within 140/300/40/160) and the copy a player would start passes `writtenCheck` (rules-engine.cjs `storyChecks` exports it); each file is checked once per time and size, and a refused one is noted once in `.questbound-diagnostics.jsonl` as `{written:'refused', file, reason, problems}` (unreadable, file:<field>, rules, duplicate, too-large; never the tale's words). Never retired: `plays.json` counts starts for measurement only. `list({cover})`: the listed tales in their `order` (never newest first) as cards (`publicTale`: id, slug, edition, order, addedAt, length, minutes, difficulty, party, levels, firstTale, notes, title, hook, blurb, mood, special, chapter and ending counts, region, terrain, a cover only when free); never a secret, the staging, a chapter's title or the walkthrough. `start(id)` (listed tales only): the story with its own `randomUUID` id, `status:'active'`, `libraryId`, `artId:'journey-'+id` and `written:{slug,edition,length}`. `any(length)`: a listed tale of that length by chance, else any.
  - **The Dungeon Master** (dm-server.cjs `generate`, tests pass a stand-in `written`). The listing (`{mode:'art', journeys:'list'}`) answers `{journeys, written}` (written alone when the story writer's library is off; an older game reads only `journeys`). A start with `journey:<uuid>` is tried on the written shelf first, then the library (409 when neither has it); an older game's `easy`/`surprise` with no journey, idea or next tale gets `written.any('short')` first. A written start (`writtenStart`) costs no AI and spends no guest 'tale' allowance (`spend.allow`), but the host's ceilings and pause (`spend.check`) still hold; the answer is `{story, ahead, journey:true, written:true}`. A table's host begins one through the unchanged `campaigns.cjs` tale route by its id (introId 'easy' there): the staging and the pace stay, as world state.
  - **The New Adventure screen** (`AdventureIntros.js`, `shelfRules.js` pure helpers, a helper file in rules-files.cjs). With written tales on the shelf (`written` prop from App.js: cards, records, tab, titles, the current story, the hero's name): the title (no subtitle and no eyebrow on a phone), "New here? Start with <tale>: about N min, ready at once." on a device with no tale record (not on phones: the card says START HERE), **Written tales** with Short / Medium / Long tabs (`Segmented`, a tab only when it has tales, counts hidden under 360 px; the tier's subline), the tab's cards (`shelfTab`: the shelf's order, finished ones last; two columns from 680 px of width, one on phones; nothing sideways). A card: the cover (the place library, free), the length badge (`lengthBadge`: "SHORT · ABOUT 40 MIN", "MEDIUM · 2–3 HOURS"), mood, difficulty pips and word, START HERE, NEW (14 days after `addedAt`, while unplayed), title, land, hook and, collapsed, its status. Chosen, it opens in place: blurb, special, "2 chapters · 3 endings · Solo or up to four heroes", content notes, status (`shelfStatus`: New / Chapter n of m, with Kara / Finished · k of n endings) and **Begin · about 40 minutes** (on a phone right under the hook: the Start here tale's Begin is in view at 375×667). A tale the hero has lived (its title among their tales and this device's record of it finished; a tale tried and left part-way can be begun again, its card saying "Left at chapter n of m") or is in the middle of (`shelfLived`) says so and Begin is disabled, never an error; the default choice passes over it. The Start here tale's Begin sits under its hook at every width (until this device has finished it, when the START HERE badge goes too). Easy start is hidden for a written tale and for Let fate decide among them (no LONG TALE tag on that card; its copy says it picks from another tab once the open one is played). Then "Or write a new one" (Your own tale, Let fate decide: "A written tale you haven't played, chosen by chance from the tab that is open" (`fateWritten`), the Skirmish) and, folded, "More journeys from the story writer" (the AI journeys, opened by the player or when one is chosen).
  - **The device** (`App.js`, `journeyLibrary.js`, `startTale.js`). The shelf is asked for when the title screen shows and again when the New Adventure screen opens (`loadShelf`, `refreshShelf`). The default choice (`shelfDefault`): Start here on a device with no tale record, else the first unfinished tale of the tab used last (`questbound.shelf.v1` {tab}, per device, guarded); never an AI journey, never kept over Your own tale, Let fate decide, the Skirmish or a journey the player chose. `taleChoice` turns `written:<uuid>` (or Let fate decide's pick) into `journey` with `introId:'written'` and no Easy start; play counts note `w-<slug>`. The device's record of each tale is kept beside `played` in `questbound.journeys.v1` (per profile) as `tales:{<uuid>:{slug, copy, chapter, of, hero, done, finished, endings, at, began, took}}` (`cleanTaleRecords`, the last 200; an older build reads only `played`), written after every change of a written tale, solo and at a table (`noteTaleRecord`, `nextTaleRecord`; a new copy starts a new run, the endings found stay). When a written tale's last scene is played on this device, **TaleFinished** (a small card at the top; the session rating waits behind it) says "You finished a Short tale in 47 minutes.", "Ending found: <title> (1 of 3).", "Try a Medium tale next?" (only a length the shelf has: `nextTier`), with Written tales (which opens that length's tab, its first tale not yet finished here chosen), Next tale here (begun at once beside where the hero stands) and Somewhere new (not at a table, where only the host gets Choose the next tale; since 2026-10-23 the card shows for every tale: see The campaign's screens under Next tales). The card waits until the feed has played the last scene (`useFeedPlaying`/`setFeedPlaying` in cinematics.js, fed by useDmTurn.js, then 1.5 s). The next tale goes on in this region by default only after a story-writer tale the hero finished: after a written tale or one left part-way the screen opens on the shelf, and Continue in this region / "Somewhere new: a written tale or a new one" sit above it.
  - **In play.** Say leads with the tale's own lines (DungeonMaster.js: `taleSayLines`, the three `firstSay` lines on the opening turn in place of the stand-ins, and each open choice's `say` line; a tap fills the box). A device that has never opened or closed Suggestions opens it on Say once when a written tale is under way (`panelUnset`, the spec's H2; a player's own choice is always kept). The Quest tab and the journal show "Short tale · Chapter 1 of 2", the chapter's goal as Aim, and `TaleState` (`taleQuestView` in stagingRules.js): At stake, Out of sight (the latest sign, no counter), Choices made ("You chose: …", "Left to fate: …") and the Ending. The feed reads "At stake: <name>, <state>.", "You chose: …"/"Left to fate: …" and "Ending: …." as cards (`describeEvent` types stake, choice and ending; `data-qb=feed-stake|feed-choice|feed-ending`).
  - **Play counts.** A session of a written tale is a version 2 record (`playMetrics.js` `noteTaleState`; `play-metrics.cjs` accepts v1 and v2): `tale` 'w-<slug>' (kept as itself, not 'other'), `chapter` 1–6, `finished`, `ending` 0–4 (its place in the tale's list), `beats` 0–200 (`pace.told`), `quietMax` 0–200; `play-report.cjs` lists each written tale (sessions, devices past chapter 1, finished, endings, beats, median longest quiet). legalText.js names the new counts (the policy's date stayed 13 October 2026: the same anonymous, opt-out play counts, a few more numbers).
  - **Spoilers.** The listing carries cards only; a player's copy holds the whole tale (as every tale's save does). `verify-written-tales.cjs` checks the shelf, the listing, a start, the spend exemption, a table's start by id, the device's helpers, the counts and the bundle canary (no client file imports or fetches a tale; `dist-phone` holds none of the first 40 characters of the fixture's secret or of any tale in a `.questbound-written` it can find).
- Your own tale (`adventureIntros.json` `idea`, `IdeaBox` in AdventureIntros.js, `ideaInstructions` in adventure-generator.cjs): the first card in the list (then the ready journeys, Let fate decide and the Skirmish; without journeys, Easy to get into then Let fate decide; `App.js` selects the first journey, or Easy to get into without them, never the idea card) where the player writes what they would like to play (8 to 300 characters; Begin stays "Write your idea first" until then), sent as `context.idea`; before the next tale in a region the same box is an optional wish. The writer builds the tale around it, treats it as a wish never an instruction, keeps Questbound's own world (another work's names become its mood with our own people and places), turns other genres into their fantasy counterpart, and stays within the 16+ rating. Ideas are always written fresh (about 3 minutes), never matched to a tale already written; since 2026-10-23 the tale is written the ahead way (no hero, the idea as its wish, with orders in hand) and added to the compendium for other players, never with the idea's text (see The compendium under Ready journeys).
- Easy to get into and varied openings (`adventureIntros.json` `easy` with `errand:true`, `errandInstructions`/`errandDirections`/`openingScenes`/`errandSchema` in adventure-generator.cjs, `validErrand`/`errandPlaces` in storyRules.js, `verify-residents.cjs`): the second card starts the hero with orders from someone with authority (keeper). The writer returns `errand` {title, task, place (a landmark's exact name), reward, threat {template, name, appearance, ally|null}}. `freshStoryGame` makes that landmark a lair held by the threat: below level 3 a pack or the orc becomes a lighter kind; an ally only from level 2 and never of the same kind; a misnamed place falls back to the first risky landmark. It keeps `story.errand` {title, task, place, reward} and adds "Your orders" to the opening journal entry. Chapter 1 is the errand (the writer's instructions), and the game closes chapter 1 once that place is `cleared`, the reward owed (`errand.rewardDue`) until keeper hands it over the next time the hero speaks with them (see Making it move; before 2026-10-22 the hero had to report back and the Dungeon Master move the tale on). Every tale also has `residents` (two at the start, so the opening scene is never two people alone), and Let fate decide, Easy to get into, the next tale and ideas that do not set their own scene get a random `openingScene` (`openingScenes`, 12). Opening lines (`openingDialogue`, asked of every opening since 2026-10-11) may be spoken by residents at the start (`residentN` → their n-id). The DM's PEOPLE ANYWHERE paragraph tells it to name the people a scene puts in front of the player and, with WHO IS HERE, how many to expect (see Who is here); `sceneFor` trims people elsewhere to a brief entry with two memories; `dm-intent.cjs` lists "Named people here".
- Easy start (retired from the screens on 2026-10-23: every tale the story writer writes now begins with orders in hand (dm-server.cjs sharedTale, so that it can join the compendium), a written tale and the Skirmish never had one, so the switch would change nothing and is gone from the New Adventure and next-tale screens; the first-time hint says every tale begins with orders in hand; App.js sends easyStart true; `storyPreferences().easyStart` is kept, unread. What follows is its history.) (2026-10-10, the host's words: "the 'easy to get into' option is supposed to be available for all story starts... I want the new players who don't know what to expect to have something easy to get into"; `easyStart` in storyPreferences.js, `taleStart`, `EasyStart` in AdventureIntros.js, `verify-tales-ahead.cjs`, `verify-residents.cjs`): a per-device switch, on unless turned off, beside the narrator choice on the New Adventure and next-tale screens (hidden for the Skirmish; shown on and locked for Easy to get into, which always begins that way). With it on, `App.playCharacter` sends `context.easyStart:true` and the story writer gives any opening (a written one, Let fate decide, Your own tale, the next tale in a region) the errand schema, opening voices, `errandInstructions` and an errand direction (`easy` in adventure-generator.cjs); `freshStoryGame`/`errandPlaces` seat the errand for any `introId`. Let fate decide with Easy start on is asked for as `easy` (answered with a ready journey, so it starts in seconds), and that is the `introId` the game keeps. Ready journeys always begin with orders in hand (see Ready journeys). The first-time hint says to keep Easy start on and that Easy to get into is ready fastest. Shown on and locked for a ready journey and for Let fate decide while journeys are on offer; dm-server takes no stocked tale for a written opening.
- A clear introduction for every tale (2026-10-11, the host's words: "map out intros with a clear introduction and make sure all adventure beginnings have this rule applied"; `storyRules.js` `validIntroduction`/`introductionFields`/`introductionFor`/`storyIntroduction`/`introductionCard`, `verify-introductions.cjs`): every way a tale begins (the written openings, Let fate decide, Easy to get into, Your own tale, the next tale or somewhere new, ready journeys, Easy start on any of them, the Skirmish) opens by saying who the hero is here, where (the place and the land, by name), what is going on, who is in front of them (by name) and what they could do first. The story writer must return `introduction` {you ≤200, place ≤200, trouble ≤300, people ≤300, first ≤240} (the INTRODUCTION paragraph: the opening tells it as a scene ending on the first step, the five fields say it plainly, never the secret; a tale written ahead speaks only of "you") and `openingDialogue` for every opening (drowned and deep had none before), each speaker with something of their own to say; `tidyStory` keeps a whole introduction trimmed to its limits, else drops it, and `freshStoryGame` then builds one from the story (`introductionFor`: an errand's orders and reward, the first chapter's goal, else the objective; keeper, mara and the residents seated at the start by name and role). The Skirmish writes its own (`hostileEncounter.js`). `freshStoryGame` always makes the opening turn (playback id 1): the card (an `effect` "Your tale begins.\nYou: …\nWhere: …\nWhat is happening: …\nWho is here: …\nWhat you could do first: …", read by `describeEvent` as type `intro` and drawn by TurnPlayback as `data-qb=feed-intro`), the opening, then the people's lines (a line said twice is said once); the journal's first entry ends with the same lines. Until the player's first move `dmContext` sends `context.openingTurn` and `context.introduction` (FIRST TURN in `questInstructions`: hold to it, answer "where am I / who are these people / what now" plainly from it, keep its people present, end with the first step plain); `sceneFor` drops `story.introduction` so the model reads it once. The opening's own turn is not a move: App.js's "A new tale begins" cue and skirmishGuide's first-move line look for a turn with a player event. `validStory` accepts no introduction (old saves) or a valid one; old saves and stocked tales of an earlier generation get one from `storyIntroduction`.
- Not so wordy (2026-10-22, the host's words: "the narration should definitely be kept a bit more brief as well, but I'm thinking more in the aspect of adding a "not so wordy" mode for players to be able to make that choice on their own. it seemed a bit daunting the last time I played and I don't want my players to feel the same way."; `tellingLengths`/`briefTelling` and `length` in storyPreferences.js, `LengthChoice` in StoryControls.js, `lengthNotes`/`lengthNote` in dm-instructions.cjs, `verify-brief-mode.cjs`): a per-device switch, off unless chosen (`length` 'full' | 'brief' in `questbound.story.v1`), in Settings → Story under the narrator and on the New Adventure and next-tale screens beside Told by ("Not so wordy · Shorter turns: a line or three of story, and people keep it brief. Turn it off to hear each turn in full."); a first-time player's hint on the New Adventure screen ends "Rather keep each turn short? Switch on Not so wordy just below.". It reaches the Dungeon Master in `context.storyPreferences.length` (a device sends its whole choice; at a table the acting player's: `table.turn` sends it and server-turn.cjs `preferences` keeps brutality, narrator and length), and only as this turn's note: `LENGTH THIS TURN` (narration one to three short sentences, the essentials only, the narrator's voice in a few words within them; only who must speak, each line one or two short sentences, up to four when the player asks to hear more; a death still told precisely; out of character one to three sentences), which `narratorVoice` puts before the narrator's line, so the narrator stays last, in every phase, retry and second look; `sceneFor` keeps it out of the scene and the standing prompt never has it, so nothing cached changes. The full telling has no note: the standing rule, tightened that day for everyone, says "Narration: vivid and lean, usually 2–3 sentences, never retelling the player's words; a spoken line usually one or two sentences." (it was 2–4 sentences, lines unbounded; the 1800-character cap is unchanged). The game's own tellings follow it (`brief` in fightLines.js `fightTelling` and calmLines.js `calmTelling`/`chosenTelling`, from `routineTelling`'s preferences): each part from the shorter half of its lines (`flShorter`), no second blow, pressing foe, barks or creature sounds in a fight, no companion's aside or answer away from one. Turn lines in the usage log carry `length:'brief'`, and `node dm-report.cjs` shows output a request for each ("Output a request: the full telling … Not so wordy …"). Measured with stand-in replies (no API) on 2026-10-22: an ordinary turn about 28% fewer output tokens with the tighter default and 46% with Not so wordy, a telling 40% and 64% (notes/brief-mode-measure.cjs; live, watch dm-report). Never a rule or a fact: what happens, the fields and the dice are the same either way.
- Narrators (`narrators` in storyPreferences.js, `NarratorChoice` in StoryControls.js, `narratorInstructions` and `narratorVoices` in dm-instructions.cjs): the player chooses who tells the tale (Settings → Story, and Told by on the New Adventure screen): The Chronicler (calm, the default), Wick the Lamplighter (dry, blunt, darkly funny; our answer to Old Greg's edge) or Sable the Bard (warm, theatrical). `context.storyPreferences.narrator` reaches the DM (at a table too: a campaign turn carries `storyPreferences`, of which only brutality, narrator and length are kept: see Not so wordy); the standing instructions say a narrator never changes rules, facts, fields or people's dialogue, and a concrete `NARRATOR THIS TURN` line (with an example the model must not reuse) goes last in each turn's request (the developer note after the scene: see The request in parts), because the play model ignored a style note buried in the cached part (checked live on 2026-10-03 with voices.cjs-style comparisons: same moment, three narrators). The play screen's heading shows the narrator's name and icon; the read-aloud voice takes each narrator's rate and pitch (`setNarratorStyle`). Narrators are our own characters: never use another product's narrator or name.
- The title page, the emblem and the menu backdrops (`HomeScreen.js`, `TitleEmblem.js`, `make-emblem.cjs`, `App.js`, `assets/title/questbound-title.jpg`, notes in `ART-NOTES.md`): the logo is the host's own emblem (supplied 2026-10-10, `assets/title/questbound-seal-source.webp`; the host's words: "make this the logo and title screen and animate it"): a scaled dragon-headed serpent biting its tail round a faceted crimson Q whose tail is a dagger, stars at west, east and south on the ring, a sun and moons inside. `node make-emblem.cjs` (headless Chrome, no internet) turns its dark background into transparency (colour to alpha) and splits it into `public/emblem/ring-v2.webp` (the serpent with its stars and the dragon's jaw, which turns), `core-v2.webp` (the Q, the inner circle and the whole dagger blade with its outline, also where it crosses the ring: the ring layer under the blade is filled with scales from further round, lined up by measurement (`mismatch`: the turn and radial step whose scales best match the serpent just beside the blade, now 13.95° round and 2 px in) and blended in over 8 px past the blade's outline, and the blade's glow off the serpent's body belongs to the still layer, so no seam or streak turns with the ring; over the serpent the blade is kept exactly as painted), two small blurred glows, `seal-v1.webp` (a small whole emblem), `seal-ring-v1.webp` and `seal-core-v1.webp` (small ring and core layers for the boot card and the pairing page) and `assets/title/questbound-emblem.webp` (the whole emblem, for the icons and native builds). They live in `public/` so CSS, the boot card and the pairing page can name them; raise `-vN` in the names (webTheme.js, public/index.html, phone-server.cjs, make-emblem.cjs) when the pictures change. `TitleEmblem` (`size` is the old ring's size; the square is 1.42 times it) stacks them (`[data-qb=emblem]`, `emblem-turn`, `emblem-ring`, `emblem-core`, `emblem-glow`, `emblem-sheen`/`emblem-shine`, `emblem-twinkle`): the ring turns all the way round once every 36 s (`emblemTurn`; the host, 2026-10-10: "make sure the ouroboros animation goes all the way around the circle"), steadily and clockwise, the way the dragon's head faces, under the still Q and blade (`qb-emblem-turn` runs from exactly 0 to 360 degrees; each emblem sets a negative `animation-delay` from `emblemPhase()`, so every emblem on the page, the boot card's too, keeps one clock and a change of screen never turns the ring back); the glow breathes, a light sweeps across the Q every 12 s (masked to the Q's own shape) and the four-point stars twinkle; only transform and opacity move. The Light atmosphere (`html[data-effects=light] [data-qb=emblem] *`), reduced motion and a full-screen cover (except the story seal, `place="story"`, which is on the cover) hold it still; the agreement is such a cover, and the title emblem turns again once it is accepted. The Gold theme's emblem is metal, not a hue turn (`grayscale(1) sepia(1) saturate(2.4) hue-rotate(-2deg) brightness(1.4) contrast(1.25)`: pale yellow-gold highlights, deep bronze shadows; `filteredRgb` in themeRules.js does the browser's sums for every filter function in order, and verify-theme.cjs checks the gold's highlights, body and shadows). It is the launch card's emblem, the title page's (same sizes and places as the old seal: beside the menu on desktop with the saved hero in a slim strip below, above the hero strip and compact menu on an upright phone, beside the name and menu on a phone held sideways, hidden on tiny screens), the story-loading seal, the top bar's mark (`EmblemMark`, a still `seal-v1.webp`), the boot card in `public/index.html` (breathing, its ring turning on the same 36 s) and the pairing page (its ring turning too) (served before pairing: `/emblem/*.webp` is a public asset of the gateway; both servers serve `.webp` as `image/webp`). The drawn serpent (`serpent.js`) is retired. The other non-game screens show the valley painting (a dusk valley: ruined tower on a crag, stone bridge, river, snow peaks, a lone lantern-bearer; restored from b1fcc0f on 2026-10-04 when the host retired the lich battle paintings) full-bleed (`titlePainting`, `resizeMode="cover"`, `data-frame` wide or tall from `homeLayout`; there is no `titleArt` and no `--qb-title-*`), framed at 50% 42% when wide and 62% 50% (the tower and peak) on an upright phone. An image loaded from a file carries its own pixel size as its style, so `s.backdrop` sets width and height to 100% itself; without that the picture stopped at its own width and wide windows were black on the right. The app icon is the emblem on the dark launch glow (`assets/title/icon.html`, `node make-icons.cjs`; the 192, 180 and 48 px icons are cut close to the serpent ring so the Q stays readable; icon links are `?v=4`). A gold icon for the next big update is stored, not deployed, in `assets/branding/next-icon/` (`icon.html` there, drawn by `node make-icons.cjs --next`; its README.md says which file goes where and to raise the links to `?v=5`): do not switch it in until the host asks. verify-presentation.cjs checks all of it.
- Colours and themes (`theme.js`, `themeRules.js`, `ThemeControls.js`, `webTheme.js`, `verify-theme.cjs`): the player picks the game's colour in Settings → Theme (Red, Orange, Gold, Green, Blue, Purple; per device, `questbound.theme.v1`; asked for by the host on 2026-10-02) and a Dark or Light appearance (the Dark mode switch: `setMode`/`currentMode`, `<html data-appearance>`, kept in the same key). Red on Dark is the default and the look every colour is written in: dark red on black with purple accents, which the host chose in place of gold on black. Every screen colour is written as its Red value inside `tint(...)` (a single colour, a shadow, or webTheme's whole style sheet); on the web `tint` turns each into a style variable (`var(--qb-e04a5c)`) and one style rule (`#questbound-colours`) gives the variables their values for the chosen theme and mode, so a change repaints at once with no reload. `themeRules.js` sorts each written colour into a family by its OKLCH hue and chroma (the reds are the main colour, the violets the accent, plum near-blacks and greys the neutrals) and each theme says where a family goes (hue, chroma, a lift for bright tones); anything else is the same in every theme: healing green, arcane teal, enemy and danger red-orange (`kept` holds the few that sit near the reds), amber warnings, real gold for coins (`colors.coin`), warm bone text, parchment maps. The token names are from the first look and are kept: `colors.gold` is the main colour, `goldMid` the accent, `goldBright` the pale accent of whatever is selected or lit, `goldDeep`/`goldLine`/`goldFaint` dark main-colour rules. Write a new screen colour as `tint('#e04a5c')`: a family colour written outside `tint()` fails `verify-theme.cjs`, which also checks contrast in every theme and mode (a literal colour outside `tint()` would not flip in Light). A picture written as text cannot read a style variable, so webTheme's corner filigree is written out per theme and mode (`themeVariables((theme,mode)=>...)`, `themedText(text,theme,mode)`). The emblem is a painting: each theme tints it with a CSS filter (`emblem` in `themeChoices`, `emblemFilter`, `--qb-emblem-filter`; Red is none; verify-theme.cjs checks with `filteredRgb` that its crimson lands on the theme's hue), the same in Light, where it sits on a crisp dark medallion of its own (`--qb-emblem-back`; see Light); the boot card takes the same filter as `--bf`. The loading card in `public/index.html` reads the saved theme and mode itself from a table that `node verify-theme.cjs --write` regenerates (run it after changing a theme or the card). To look at a theme in the test browser, set `QB_THEME` for the capture script or store `{"theme":"green"}` under the key. The app icon and the pairing page stay red in every theme. The pairing page in `phone-server.cjs` carries its own copy of these colours (it is served before the game is), so a change of theme must be made there too and the share gateway restarted. The app icon is `assets/title/icon.html`, photographed at each size by `node make-icons.cjs`; raise `?v=N` in the icon links when it changes (see `assets/title/ART-NOTES.md`).
- Light (`lightPalette`/`lightRgb`/`keptInLight` in themeRules.js, `lightCss` in webTheme.js, `verify-theme.cjs`; 2026-10-12, the host: "light mode looks horrible, rework that to not be so harsh on the eyes. dark mode will be the default status on launch but I want light mode to actually look good."): Light is drawn for paper, not inverted. `lightPalette(themeId)` holds each theme's tones and `lightRgb` works out what each written colour is for from its family and lightness: dark surfaces become layers of paper (page, panel, raised sheet, sunk well: Red's page `#f3e8df`, never pure white; each theme's `paper` hue and chroma in `themeChoices`, Blue a cool stone, Green a pale sage, the rest warm parchment), greys soft rules, light lettering ink in the theme's neutral (never black), the main colour and accent deep and quiet (chroma at most .15; Gold's main goes to bronze, `paper.main`), dark accent fills a pale accent wash (the selected tab or card, its lettering kept deep), dark fills of meanings (fight banners and plates) a blush, light meanings ink of their own hue; the primary trio keeps its roles (`#9e1b32` a deep fill, `#f06e80` its edge, `#ffeef0` cream lettering: use these three only for primary fills); black shadows become a warm brown at 42% strength; white and the parchment map's colours (`keptInLight`) stay. Dark is `themedRgb` exactly as before (`appearanceRgb(…,'dark')`) and stays the default on launch (theme.js and the boot card read `mode` as dark unless `light` was saved). webTheme.js writes the tones as `--ql-*` (`r g b`, so `rgb(var(--ql-main) / .3)`) in Light and adds `lightCss` after the main sheet: every selector under `html[data-appearance=light]`, written outside `tint()`, no animations or blend modes; it draws panels, sheets and plates as layered paper with a hairline of the theme colour, buttons and chips as raised paper, the HUD and phone tab bar as paper, crests and portraits as medallions, paper dice with ink numbers, feed lines as faint washes of their own colour, title lettering in a deep ink with a pressed edge (no glow), the emblem on a crisp dark medallion with a thin crimson rim (`--qb-emblem-back`, the top-bar mark too) and the painting as a faint watermark, and hides grain, shafts and embers. Every screen colour must be written in `tint()` (black and white shadows and meanings too), or Light cannot reach it; AdventureMap.js's parchment-map colours are the deliberate exception. verify-theme.cjs checks the layers, AA contrast of 13 lettering colours on every paper layer in every theme, the primary trio, the softened shadows, each theme's main hue, that Dark equals `themedRgb`, and that the light sheet matches only Light (verify-performance.cjs: it animates and blends nothing). Look at Light in the test browser with `questbound.theme.v1` = `{"theme":"red","mode":"light"}`. The pairing page stays red and dark.
- Fit (`ui.js`, `fitRules.js`, `GameHud.js`, `AdventureMap.js`, `webLayout.js`, `verify-presentation.cjs`, `verify-fit.cjs`; filling the notch and home bar is in Filling the screen): nothing in the game scrolls sideways at any width down to 320 px (the check fails on any `horizontal` prop, overflowX or overflow-x auto/scroll, or scrollLeft). Rows of chips use `WrapRow`: each chip sits in a cell of the row's height (cut to the row with an ellipsis if wider), at most `rows` lines show, and a More/Less chip shows the rest (folded again on a new turn or picker via `resetKey`); opened, a row grows to `openRows` and then scrolls up and down. `chipLayout(width,height,{picker})` gives one row on short screens (under 520 high), two on phones and windows under 700 high, three otherwise, at least three for an open Attack… or Cast… picker or the party wait row (two under 620 high), 36 px chips on phones and 40 on desktop. The map's row of lands is one WrapRow line (opened, four lines that scroll; tabs 36 px), its views (City, Whole land, All lands) chips at least 36 px tall that wrap (on a short screen the full map puts them and the row of lands beside its heading, and its sheet leaves room for both rows), a city sheet grows taller rather than drop a district's name and the all-lands sheet grows larger than its frame with many lands (measured on 2026-10-23 at 320×568, 375×812, 812×375, 768×1024 and 1400×860, Dark and Light, side column folded and unfolded, Full map included: `C:/Users/itsbr/qb-work/notes/map/map-ui.cjs`; zoomed, a sheet's zoom buttons float over it as before), PartyPanel's cards wrap in two, and `Segmented` wraps on phones (two-line labels, two tabs a row with four or more under 420 px). On phones `GameButton` sets its lettering tighter, the pause menu scrolls (four cells a row on a phone held sideways), and the full map puts the row of lands beside the heading on short screens. At window heights under 700 the narrator's heading is dropped. The zoomed region map still pans in two directions (the one deliberate exception). The browser pane's screenshots can come back cropped after a resize or a script reload: navigate afresh, or measure with `getBoundingClientRect`, before trusting one.
- The side column folds away (`Adventure.js` `sideKey` 'questbound.side.v1' {folded}, `sideFolded()`, checked in `verify-presentation.cjs`; the host on 2026-10-12: "add a minimizer for the information on the left side of the screen on pc view to help the player engage fully in conversations"): on wide screens (the desktop side column with Quest, Map, Inventory and Log) a "Hide the side panel" button beside the side tabs folds the column to a slim rail (`data-qb=side-rail`, 44 px) so the story and a conversation get the whole width. The rail has "Show the side panel" and one button per tab (opening the column on that tab), and a mark while a fight or brawl is on (swords) or the hero is dying or dead (a skull), so nothing urgent hides behind it. Folded or not is remembered per device (storage guarded: missing or refusing storage just means unfolded); phones and cramped windows are unchanged (their tabs sit in the bottom bar).
- Hover tooltips (`tooltips.js`, `initializeTooltips()` in index.js, checked in `verify-presentation.cjs`; the host on 2026-10-12: "on the minimize function, make sure the pop up appears above all other layers. make sure all pop ups work the same way when hovered over"): every element with `dataSet={{tip:'…'}}` (`data-tip`; IconButton sets it from `tip` or its label) shows its words in one tooltip element (`#qb-tooltip`, `role=tooltip`) appended to `<body>`, `position:fixed` at z-index 2147483647, so no panel, scroll area, transform or later layer can clip or cover it. `placeTip(anchor,tip,view,side)` puts it below the element, above when there is no room below, and keeps it inside the window; `data-tip-side="right"` (IconButton `tipSide="right"`, the folded side rail's buttons) puts it beside the element. It follows the mouse (pointerover/out), shows on keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`), hides on a press, scroll, wheel, key or blur and when its element leaves the page; touch never shows it. There is no CSS `::after` tooltip any more (`content:attr(data-tip)` must not come back in webTheme.js; tooltips.js also switches any off): add a tooltip with `data-tip`, never with a pseudo-element of its own.
- Dice in the feed (`TurnPlayback.js`): `DieFace` tumbles in, flickers through numbers and lands; `RollNumber` rolls damage and healing up; both only for the live turn and never with reduced motion. A check card names the skill rolled (`describeEvent` → `check`).
- Sound (`audio.js`, `verify-audio.cjs`): the score is pads, a bowed bass, felted keys in a fixed figure and a flute or horn singing composed phrases (A A B A, in key, landing on chord tones); nothing is plucked or ticked, every voice has an attack of at least a hundredth of a second, and the hearth no longer crackles steadily. Keep it that way: the player called the old plucked score "clickety clackety".
- Music that does not cut out (`audio.js`, `verify-audio.cjs`): the score is planned 1.6 s ahead on the AudioContext clock (`LOOKAHEAD`, tick 250 ms). Beats missed during a stall are skipped (state only, `step(time,false)`), never scheduled in the past, and starts are clamped (`audioHealth().late` should stay 0). A change of mood crossfades: each scheduler owns `moodIn`/`padIn`/`hallIn` gains that fade in while the old ones fade out (`retire`), and setMood never touches `out.music`. A change between explore and haven waits 2.5 s and is cancelled if the stage flips back; combat, menu and silence apply at once. Narration ducks `musicVoice`/`ambVoice`, and hits duck `musicDuck`/`ambDuck`, so neither ends the other. Every source disconnects its nodes when it ends. The context uses latencyHint 'balanced', resumes from 'suspended' or 'interrupted', and a watchdog rebuilds it when its clock stops while 'running'. "Keep playing in the background" (Settings → Sound, `settings.background`, on by default where hover and a fine pointer exist) keeps sound going while the window is hidden or covered (for up to 15 minutes); off, it fades and suspends. `audioHealth()` goes into feedback notes.
- Drawing the page (`webTheme.js`, `fullscreen.js`, `perfWatch.js`, `DisplayControls.js`, `verify-performance.cjs`): an animation that runs forever may move only transform or opacity: no left, top, width, height, letter-spacing, box-shadow, background-position or animated filter (put a glow in a ::after and animate its opacity). Grain and rays use no blend mode. Settings → Display → Atmosphere (Auto/Full/Light, `questbound.display.v1` `effects`, and `autoLight`) sets `<html data-effects>`. Light hides grain and rays, stops fog, embers and drift, and drops backdrop blur. Auto turns Light when `startPerfWatch` (after the launch tap) finds more than a quarter of frames over 34 ms. A full-screen cover calls `setCovered(name,on)` (story, agreement, scene, finale), and `html[data-covered=on]` pauses the stage. `usePageWheel` is passive and acts only where nothing under the pointer can scroll that way.
- Loading screens say little by design (`SceneTransition.js`, `StoryLoading.js`, `DynamicArt.js`): a place name or "One moment", a line that fills, a way to continue. Do not list what is being generated. (A new tale takes minutes: after twenty seconds the screen says once that a long tale is written with care.)
- Loadouts (`weaponRules.js`, `verify-loadouts.cjs`): kits follow the 2024 starting equipment exactly (checked item for item). A hero's main weapon is the best close-range weapon they carry for their own scores (`readyLoadout`, `weaponAttacks` order; class order in `classWeapons` settles ties; with a shield carried a one-handed weapon is preferred: see Hands), and `loadoutWarnings` names ill-fitting gear (Chain Mail under Strength 13, heavy weapons, the wrong Fighter kit, a two-hander kept on the back, a slung shield).
- Hands (`weaponRules.js` `shieldSlung`/`shieldInUse`/`twoHanderInHand`/`oneHanded`/`canTakeInHand`/`handsBlocked`, `verify-hands.cjs`; the host on 2026-10-11: "2 handed weapons should take up both hands unless otherwise capable of dual wielding, even so there should be a small bonus added for 2 handedly holding a weapon with one handed potential"): a shield takes a hand and a two-handed weapon (`twoHands`: Greatsword, Greataxe, Maul, Glaive, Halberd and both bows, which the 2024 rules make two-handed) takes both. With a shield on the arm a two-hander is blocked (`handsBlocked`) and the hero fights one-handed, unless the player takes it in hand (`game.wield`, the Inventory's Take in hand, the `wield` campaign edit, checked with `canTakeInHand`) or carries no one-handed melee weapon at all: then the shield is slung (`shieldSlung`), adds nothing to armor class (`combatBasics`; 18 → 16 for a Paladin with a Greatsword in hand) or through its magic (`lootRules` `magicEffects`), and no other hand strikes (`masteryRules` `offhandWeapons`; an attack with a two-hander never sets `lightAttack`). Taking a one-handed weapon in hand raises the shield again. A bow with a shield waits on the back until taken in hand (before this it could never be used). A `versatile` weapon (its two-handed die: Quarterstaff and Spear 8, Longsword, Battleaxe and Trident 10) with no shield in use is held in both hands (`grip:'two'`, `oneHandDie` kept) and deals the larger die; beside a shield, thrown (`thrownAttack`) or while the hero holds a grapple (`oneHanded` in weaponRules.js, applied in creature fights by wildFightRules.js and in brawls by brawlRules.js) it deals its own. A Monk's Martial Arts and Shillelagh take the larger of their die and the grip's. Two-weapon fighting stays Light weapons only (with Nick): no Light weapon is versatile or two-handed (asserted), and no feat or feature allows more. The Attack… picker detail says "+5 · 1d10+3 two-handed" or "one-handed", descriptions "(both hands on it; 1d8 in one hand)" and "versatile (1d10 in both hands)"/"reach", the Inventory's Off hand slot "Both hands on the Greatsword · Shield on your back", LoadoutSummary "in both hands"/"shield slung", and the armor note explains a slung shield; the Dungeon Master gets grip notes in `attackOptions.carried` ("(two-handed)", "(versatile, in both hands: 1d10)", "(versatile, one-handed beside the shield)") and `attackOptions.hands`. Battleaxe (Topple), Trident (Topple, thrown), Maul (Topple), Glaive (Graze, reach) and Halberd (Cleave, reach) are in the weapon table with their 2024 dice and prices (found gear, not in kits; LOOT AND TRADE in dm-instructions.cjs names them); Warhammer and Pike wait for the Push mastery. Companions keep their own attack table (`companionAttack`). The shield by choice (`game.shieldAway`, only ever true, absent meaning on the arm; `inventoryRules.setShield(game,hero,away,weapon?)`, `weaponRules.shieldStowed`; the host on 2026-10-12: "a shield bearer should be able to put the shield away to hold their weapon with 2 hands, they should make that decision before engagement in the equipment screen or specifically request it themselves if they have not set their character to hold one weapon with 2 hands."): `gearedHero` passes it on as `equipment.shieldAway`, which makes `shieldSlung` true (no armor class, no magic shield bonus), frees both hands (a versatile weapon in both for its larger die, two-handers no longer blocked, Light two-weapon fighting allowed) and keeps the weapon in hand in hand (`game.wield` set to the main weapon or the one named, so a found greatsword does not jump into the hands); taken up again it clears `shieldAway` and a two-hander in hand goes on the back. `setShield` refuses with no shield, when nothing would change (a two-hander in hand has already slung it), a weapon not carried, a bow named, or taking it up with no one-handed weapon to fight with. A non-versatile weapon (a mace) may go with it too: no larger die, but a free hand. The Inventory has the chip under the equipment slots ("Hold the Longsword in both hands", or "Put your shield on your back" for a non-versatile weapon / "Take up your shield", with the armor class and die change, e.g. "1d10 in place of 1d8, armor class 18 → 16"), out of a fight only (in one it says to type it: it takes your action); there is no chip in the Actions row. Typed words work anywhere (`dmCommands.shieldCommand`, before companion orders: "I put my shield on my back and grip my longsword with both hands", "I sling my shield", "I hold my sword in both hands" for a hero with a shield, "I take up my shield", "strap … back on my arm", raise/ready/lift/unsling; only when it changes something; questions, negatives and a hero with no shield go to the Dungeon Master). Out of a fight it is free (a log line); in one (stage combat, which covers creature, wild, story-foe and vault fights, or an active brawl) it is the hero's action, as donning or doffing a shield (Utilize) is in the 2024 rules: `{type:'shield',away,weapon?}` in `heroStep` is refused once the action is spent or with a spell pending, the shield changes first so the foe's answer this turn meets the new armor class, then the turn runs as after a Dodge (a bonus action may follow, the foe answers, a party's turn passes on; in a brawl it ends the turn like `npc-dodge`); `turnCost` counts it an action, so "I sling my shield, then attack" plays the shield and the attack waits. At a table `shieldAway` is a hero field (`heroFields`; validated in `validAdventure` and `validParty`: absent or true), each hero's own, and the `shield` campaign edit (`{kind:'shield',away,weapon?}` in campaigns.cjs through `r.setShield`; `rules-engine.cjs` exports `setShield`) makes it out of a fight (refused in a fight, while down or dead, and by anyone but the host for a one-hero campaign's hero); in a fight it is a typed turn. It is kept across rests and level-ups, not carried into the next tale (like `wield`). Loadout warnings, the armor note, the Attack… picker ("1d10+3 two-handed"), the Off hand slot ("Both hands on the Longsword · Shield on your back"), the sheet, the HUD, party stats and `attackOptions.hands`/`carried` follow it. `verify-hands.cjs`, `verify-campaign-edits.cjs` and `verify-server-turn.cjs` check it. Checks that concatenate `combatRules.js` need `weaponRules.js` too (`combatBasics` reads `shieldSlung`).
