- Subclasses (`subclassOptions.js`, `subclassRules.js`, `classFeatureCatalog.json`, `verify-subclasses.cjs`; asked for by the host on 2026-10-04: "make sure each of the classes has 4/5 subclasses"):
  - Roster: every class offers its SRD 5.2 subclass first, then three of Questbound's own. The Artificer has five specialities: Draughtwright, Platewright, Siegewright, Steelwright and Snarewright (the first four were Alchemist, Armorer, Artillerist and Battle Smith until 2026-10-22, another publisher's names: renamed as below). Every feature is in the catalog (source `SRD 5.2` or `Questbound`, automation `engine` or `reference`), and the sheet and the Dungeon Master read it there.
  - Engine table: `subclassRules.js` holds `subclassEngine`: each subclass's features (name, level, a one-line rule of at most 140 characters, engine flag; the same as the catalog, which the check asserts) and the effects the engine plays from their level. The effect kinds are:
    - `passive`: HP per level, `acBase`, `acBonus` armor/unarmored/any, initiative ability or advantage, crit, martial, `extraAttack`, `grappleDC`, `companionBonus`, skills; read in combatBasics, weaponRules, masteryRules `unarmedDC` and skillRules `trainedSkills`.
    - `rider`: once a turn on a weapon or Unarmed Strike hit (`game.featTurn`, a turn field); when melee, weapon, martialArts, wounded, firstRound or companion; marks or Vex; the line "N Type damage (Name: XdY [..])." begins with the number for the feed.
    - `ward`: a Reaction that blunts a blow by a die plus a modifier, once a round (the round spent is kept on the hero as `reacted`); a blow reduced to 0 records no damage, because `updateHealth` throws on 0.
    - `action`: one class action, `class:<id>`, which says whether it is an action or a bonus action and goes through the same turn accounting; heal, temp, mark, vex or strike; its chip, words, description and story allow-list come from the data. In a brawl one that heals or shields the hero is theirs and one aimed at someone goes at the person named, or the only one still fighting (`brawlFeatChoices` in turnResourceRules.js, `personDefenses`); it may begin a brawl, like a blow.
    - `spell`: the modifier once a casting, to damage of listed types or to slot-cast healing.
    - `victory`: temporary HP on a won fight.
  - Uses and rests: at most one effect a subclass spends uses (`game.resources.feat`, 0..20, a hero field). Long rests clear them; short-recharge ones come back on a short rest, and `restWouldHelp` knows it.
  - SRD parity: Hunter's Prey (its extra-die option) and Cutting Words are engine-played too.
  - What stays with the Dungeon Master: later features are the DM's from `context.player.subclassFeatures` (`subclassBriefs`) and the SUBCLASS FEATURES paragraph in `contractInstructions`.
  - Rules for new names: never use a book's subclass or feature name; the check's deny list catches them, matching whole words. A renamed subclass keeps old heroes playing through `renamedSubclasses`/`currentSubclass` in characterRules.js (Warrior of the Hidden Step became the Warrior of the Quiet Step; on 2026-10-22 the Artificer's Alchemist, Armorer, Artillerist and Battle Smith became the Draughtwright, Platewright, Siegewright and Steelwright, and the deny list in verify-subclasses.cjs now holds those four names; an old name stays a valid plan, plays as the new one and is no longer offered; saves keep the name they were written with, read through `currentSubclass`, never rewritten: the adventure is tied to the hero's exact text, and format 1's own upgrade renames only what it renamed then, `formatOneRenames` in saveMigrations.js; spellOptions.js reads subclass spells through `currentSubclass` too).
  - Balance: `node balance-check.cjs --subclass <name|all> --levels 3,5` (with `--day 3` by default, `--class A,B` to narrow) compares each subclass with its class's SRD one; their win rates should sit within about 12 points. The numbers are still provisional.
  - Spells: granted spells are in `spellOptions.js` `subclassSpells`, with `subclassSpellClass`.
- Experience and levels (`experienceRules.js`, `verify-experience.cjs`; 2026-10-04, the host's words: "make sure leveling is spaced out properly, but not too much"): levels come from experience points on the SRD 5.2 thresholds (`xpThresholds`). Every award is a share of the current level's gap (`xpAwards`: creature fight .07, ×1.5 at a lair, ×1.25 with a pack or a creature at its side, a quarter past the 8th fight of a tale; main foe .35 once a tale; brawl .04 per enemy who was hostile or held a grudge when it began (`npcCombat.worthy`, at most 2, only when all are down); chapter .35; lead .12 once per lead; tale end .25 once; first visit to a place .015 (10 a tale); first arrival in a land .05, only with a place that is paid for) times `xpPace(level)`: 8, 2.2, 1.6, .95, .69 (L5–9), .55 (L10–14), .45 (L15–19). That puts level 2 after the first two fights, level 3 at about 35 min, 4 at 1.5 h, 5 at 3 h, 10 at 14 h, 15 at 28 h and 20 at about 45 h (simulated in the check; keep the tuning numbers in `xpPace`/`xpAwards` only). The Skirmish (`introId` 'hostile') pays only below level 3. At most one level waits (`xpCap`). `game.xp` is per hero; `game.xpTally` ({tale, fights, places, leads, boss, complete}) is world state and resets for a new story id. Awards are worked out from before/after only, never the Dungeon Master's words: `withExperience(before,after,hero)` runs in `settleTurn` right after the step (a lead ticked by hand, on a device or by the `lead` campaign edit, is the player's own note and pays nothing since 2026-10-22: see Making it move; the Dungeon Master's `lead-done` pays once, through `xpTally.leads`), only where the move was made (on the device solo, on the campaign server at a table; never in `applyRemote`). It adds 'Experience: +N (…).' lines (the feed's 'xp' card, `cue('xp')` for moves without the DM) and the 'You have earned a level…' line. `fightEnding(before,after)` is the one definition of a won fight, shared by the story log and the spoils (`withSpoils`). `levelUpReady` needs the experience (a victory alone is no longer a level) and refuses in fights, brawls, vaults, with a spell pending or while a party's `turnOrder` stands. A level is always taken where the hero stands (`advancedInPlace`: the party kept, `encounterLevel` changed only when solo; then `foeForLevel` grows a foe not yet fought and `levelCompanions`; at a table the `advance` campaign edit, `campaign-advancement.cjs`). Old saves are read lazily (`heroXp`: `levelsOwed` and an untaken old 'victory' count as experience; validators still accept `levelsOwed` 0..5, and owed levels carry into the next tale only as that experience). The DM gets only `context.levelReady` and the EXPERIENCE AND LEVELS paragraph in `questInstructions`: never award or mention XP or levels. The HUD shows a 3 px line under the HP bar ('XP a/b' on roomy screens and in the pause menu), the Character Sheet an Experience panel (`experienceView`), and the Level Up screen the next threshold.
- Developer options (`DeveloperOptions.js`, `verify-developer.cjs`): at the bottom of the character builder, behind the developer code `6969` (a convenience gate for testers, not a secret), `form.level` and `form.devScores` (each 1–20, with `devLevel`) replace level 1 and the standard array; `finalScores` returns them plus improvements from later level-ups, and the hero passes the same validators as any other. `DeveloperDiagnostics.js` (in Settings) shows the host's diagnostics (`ops-status`, `ops-verify-backups`: recent denials, mail failures, backups), only on the desktop (`X-Questbound-Local`) and for a verified account.
- Models and request size (`ai-request.cjs`, `verify-dm-trim.cjs`): the launcher suggests `gpt-6-luna` for play (`OPENAI_MODEL`, remembered in `.questbound-model`) and an optional story writer (`OPENAI_STORY_MODEL`, `.questbound-story-model`; `Questbound.cmd -Models` changes both without the key) that `generate` uses for `mode:'adventure'` and `mode:'character'`. A running DM reads both files on every live request (`liveModels` in `dm-server.cjs`), so a change of model takes effect at once with no restart and no key; the play model also drives the image tool. Since 2026-10-02 everything runs on `gpt-6-luna` (no `.questbound-story-model`). A long tale takes about three minutes on it at high effort (written as a background job), 25 to 30 s at low; at medium it no longer fits in one 80-second request. To compare models on whether they act when asked, score them with a probe of ordinary requests (go to a known place, search, explore) before switching: both scored about three in four after the `WHAT IS TRUE NOW` instructions were added. A reply that starts two game actions keeps the first (`generate` repairs it) instead of being refused. `modelOptions(model,effort)` adds `reasoning.effort` only for reasoning models (GPT-5 and later, o-series): turns use `QUESTBOUND_DM_REASONING` (default `none`), heroes `QUESTBOUND_STORY_REASONING` (default `medium`, with an 80 s timeout and 6000 more output tokens of room), and stories the effort in `.questbound-story-effort` (read live by `storyEffort`; else that same setting). `sceneFor` trims what the model reads (standing guidance lives in `contractInstructions`, in the cached prefix; portraits, the opening, duplicated place and conversation text and phase-irrelevant parts are dropped) while every validator still sees the full `body.context`; `repairedDiscovery`/`repairedCreature`/`repairedIntroduce` (dm-replies.cjs) shorten overruns instead of refusing the reply. Live requests append token use to `.questbound-usage.jsonl` and rejections to `.questbound-diagnostics.jsonl` (both carry the `guest` label); runs with a fake `fetchImpl` log nothing. `node dm-report.cjs [--days N]` sums cost and rejections; `node balance-check.cjs` simulates fights per ready-made hero, level and creature and reports wins, falls and deaths (a fallen hero rolls death saves to the end, a companion helping first; no AI calls; `--companion Class`, `--party N`, `--magic weapon+1,cloak,ring`, `--day N`, `--subclass`, `--foe`, `--fights`, `--levels`, `--quiet`). A turn has room for 3000 output tokens (`turnRoom`), doubled up to 8000 for a retry after a reply cut short by the limit.
- The request in parts and a leaner turn (`standingPrompt`/`heroPrompt` in dm-instructions.cjs, `replySchemaFor`/`phaseFields` in dm-schemas.cjs, `cacheKeyFor`, `turnRequest`/`sceneMessages`/`sceneParts`, `requestParts`/`requestScene` and `sceneFor` → `leaner`/`lean`/`inCacheOrder` in dm-server.cjs, `standingRules`/`heroRules`/`turnRules` in dm-rules.cjs, `verify-dm-cache.cjs`; the host, 2026-10-17: "put everything that never changes first and the turn-by-turn details last, so OpenAI reuses far more of each request" and "only send what the moment needs (no spell reference for a fighter, less of the long story memory during a plain fight, fewer far-away places)"; done in two steps that day, the cache order (layout 2) and then the request in parts (layout 3, what runs now)): the provider caches a request part by part (message by message): it reuses the leading parts that match a request it saw a little earlier and nothing of the first part that differs (measured live on 2026-10-17: turns were cached to exactly the end of the instructions and schema, never into the scene, and a retry whose note was in the instructions got none). So a turn request is: `instructions` = `standingPrompt` alone (every standing instruction, HOW THE TURN ARRIVES and the shared core and action rules; byte-identical for every player, hero, phase, retry and second look: nothing turn- or hero-specific may go in it), with one reply schema for every phase (`replySchemaFor()`: strict, naming no choice, person or pool id; never put this turn's ids, conditions or phase into it); then as `input` a developer message with this hero's class features (`heroPrompt`), the scene as user messages `Scene: <part>.\n{"context":{...}}` in `sceneParts` order (what lasts the tale, the hero's spell list included; the land and what the hero carries; who is here; the story so far; then this turn: anything unnamed, the hero's hit points, choices, the fight, the conversation, resolved results, `rulesReference` (the spells and features the player's words name, and a spell awaiting a ruling), `now` last, with `input`, the player's words), and last, when there is one, a developer message with this turn's note (the phase text, a written tale's STORY BEAT THIS TURN when one is due (see Written tales (runtime); the standing prompt 9184 tokens with its WRITTEN TALES paragraph after the review of 2026-10-22, 9206 with the watched road of 2026-10-23 in BEYOND THE MAP; verify-dm-cache's budget 9185 → 9207, raised by exactly that sentence; 9243 with the tale's road of 2026-10-23 in THE LONG TALE, the budget 9207 → 9244, and quest.arc's 49 tokens raising the ready journey's town and conversation scene budgets 1420 → 1469 and 1360 → 1409), a retry's or second look's LOOK AGAIN, LENGTH THIS TURN when the player chose Not so wordy, and NARRATOR THIS TURN, the very last thing the model reads). A new context key goes into the part that matches how often it changes (verify-dm-cache shows which parts two turns share). What a phase cannot use is left out when the reply is read (`phaseFields` first in `steadied`, noted as "field not used in this phase": a telling or cue starts nothing, out of character only talks, an ordinary turn never rewinds); a spell awaiting a ruling with no ruling, or with an action beside it, is asked again and then clarified. `judge` holds the reply to the turn (a choice id in other capitals matched, a speaker named mapped to the id, a line from someone who cannot speak now dropped, recruitment the turn does not allow dropped, a check in a brawl or after the action dropped; a wrong choice id, a discovery or an ambush the turn does not allow is asked again). What the model reads is leaner (`leaner`, `leanLimits`): in a fight a short memory (the tale's first line, the chapter under way, facts bearing on who is here), no far lands, other places or people elsewhere, only people here, two journal entries; out of one the nearest ten other lands (five with place names; `world.regions` still names every land), twelve other places and twelve people elsewhere, anything the player names kept; empty fields, duplicated person fields, hp already in npcHP, map.visited and repeated choice notes left out; a fighter's request carries no spell reference. Validators always see the full body.context. `prompt_cache_key` is `qb-dm-<0..7>` (tale and hero hashed, `cacheBuckets` 8 so no key passes the provider's ~15 requests a minute), the same for every phase; a provider 400 naming it turns it off for the DM process (`globalThis.__questboundNoCacheKey`). Measured live on 2026-10-20: no turn is cached past the instructions and schema (about 15,020 tokens), though the hero and the scene before this turn's part are byte-identical from turn to turn; sending the hero and the note as user messages (tried as layout 4 that day) cached exactly as much, so the provider stops where the input begins. Caching more would mean putting the scene in the instructions, and the scene holds what players wrote: do not, and do not try layout 4 again. Turn lines in the usage log carry `layout: 3` (`promptLayout`), `phase` and `aside` (second looks); `node dm-report.cjs` shows a Cache block (goal: about 85% of turn input cached): the cached share, input and cost per turn request for requests sent in parts (by phase, retries and second looks apart), in the cache order (layout 2) and before. The checks read requests with `requestParts`/`requestScene` (exported by dm-server.cjs). Measured on 2026-10-17 without API calls on one 22-request session: 57% cached (layout 2, which matched the live log) to 93%, input about $0.00089 to $0.00031 a request; the scene shrank from about 5.6k to 3.6k tokens a request (a long life's fight 9.6k to 3.1k).
- Said once and briefly (2026-10-21, the host: "optimize token usage even further we shouldn't need that many per turn"; `compact` after `leaner` in `sceneFor`, dm-server.cjs; the standing prompt in dm-instructions.cjs and `standingRules` in dm-rules.cjs; budgets in verify-dm-cache.cjs section 5b). Since only the instructions and schema are cached live, the scene is paid in full every turn, so it is now read as short lines where the game sends objects, and nothing is told twice. Only what the model reads changed: the device still sends the same context, and every validator, `judge`, the second look and `situation` read the whole `body.context`.
  - **Lines.** choices are `[id] label` (an id-only label is just `[dodge]`, `[end-turn]`, `[throw:Javelin]`); the blows at the people here are one line for everyone offered the same ones, `ID` standing for each id listed (`[npc-attack:ID:Mace] [npc-grapple:ID], ID: mara, n1 or n2`; HOW TO ACT says how to read it, and verify-dm-cache reads the lines back into exactly the choices offered), one line for the weapons at the creature fought, the journeys by id when every place is in `world.knownPlaces` (`[travel-p1] [travel-tower]: travel there.`; `now`'s journey list then says only that any travel choice may be taken), the ways beyond the map and the leads as `[beyond-ID], ID: N, NE, …` and `[lead-done:ID], ID: l1 or l2`. Known places are `Name [id]: kind, 3.7 miles S, visited, lair, threat X (wolf). Feature: …` ("visited" only when so; the description, cut to 100, only for a place the player names and the errand's; the feature only within five miles, named or the errand's; a place in another land without its distance, which its land's line gives; the place stood in is `world.current`, with its danger, feature and threat); the nearest other lands `Name: road; places: …` (`world.regions` then names only the rest); spells `Cure Wounds (level 1; Action; Touch; V, S; cast with: slot 1)`; `skillChecks` only the skills that differ from their ability modifier ("Insight +5 (trained), …"; CHECKS says the rest use the modifier); scores, species traits, equipment and loadout one line each; ties `Tamsin Orlan (mara): close friend; the foe: ally. <note>`; people elsewhere `Name [n5]: role; lives at …; attitude; unnoticed; not met; ties: …` (an object while a grudge, bond, fate, memories or class is to be told); journal entries `Title: text`; conversation lines the text alone; the hero's class features one line (`heroPrompt`: "Second Wind (level 1); …").
  - **Once.** The people here are told once, in `nearbyNPCs`, with their standing merged in (attitude, memories, ties, grudge, bond, forgiveness, trainedAs, regard); `npcSocialState` holds only those elsewhere or down; `conversationParticipants` are ids. The story's people keep name, species, gender, motive and personality in what lasts the tale (the same wherever the player is, so that part stays one; their role and ties are told with the people). Left out: the opening once the journal holds it, worldFacts that repeat the premise, recentEvents the journal holds, `enemyHP` equal to the encounter's, `player.spells` beside spellReference, the story's locations, region and painter or library ids, `foeDamageType` (the encounter has its type), `turnStart` (folded into `player.health` as `max`), standard attitude sentences, `knows`, `metHero:true`, false and zero flags, unused turnResources, `npcHP` of the unhurt outside a fight, people elsewhere in a fight (an old bug kept them, with their defenses and hit points), the spell DC and attack bonus of a hero without spells, an encounter's dice and saves outside a spell ruling, `autoShield` and `easyStart`.
  - **Moved into the standing prompt.** The fixed price list (LOOT AND TRADE) and the game's nine creature kinds (DANGER IN THE WILDS): a scene sending exactly these leaves them out (`standardPrices`, `standardCreatures` in dm-server.cjs; change both with inventoryRules.js `priceList` or hostileEncounter.js `hostileFoes`, else the scene simply sends them again). Weapon masteries, grappling, two weapons and hands are a word each in the scene (`masteries` "Greatsword: Graze", `grappleDC`, `twoWeapons:true` only with a light weapon carried, `hands` "shield on arm" / "shield put away" / "shield slung" / "no shield") and explained once in ATTACKS.
  - **The standing prompt** says every rule once and tersely: about 13,600 to 8,850 tokens by the check's count (62.7 to 38.5 thousand characters). Every paragraph heading and every phrase the checks look for is kept (grep the verify files before rewording); `actionRules` keeps Dodge, Help, Hide, Influence, Search and Study, Utilize (attacks, magic, movement and readied actions are in HOW TO ACT, ATTACKS and TURNS). The reply schema has no descriptions to trim (2,021, unchanged).
  - **Measured** (the check's count; before → after): a town turn 3,410 → 1,877, a conversation 3,380 → 1,805, a creature fight 2,509 → 1,322, a brawl's telling 2,977 → 1,913, a party's fight 2,461 → 1,293, a wizard 3,827 → 2,219, a long life 6,833 → 4,786 and in a fight 3,283 → 1,772; four live turns captured on 2026-10-21 (a ready journey's town, conversation) about 6,200 → 3,600 scene tokens, the whole request about 21,900 → 14,500. verify-dm-cache fails past its budgets (about 8% of room): raise one only on purpose and say why here.
  - **Briefer still** (the same day, the host: "optimize efficiency even more, then do it again until we are at the lowest possible cost per turn"; `briefer` after `compact` in `sceneFor`, `briefLimits`, `personLine`): what the moment does not need is left out of what the model reads (validators, `judge`, the second look and `situation` still read the whole `body.context`). The story's `resolution` only in the last chapter (or a tale without chapters); `leadsSeenThrough` never, and `quest.revealedWhenAchieved` never as such (out of a fight the chapter's truth, at most 200 characters, and the next chapter's title are read again since 2026-10-22: see Making it move; between 2026-10-21 and then they were never read, and no chapter could close); lead hooks cut to 110; the errand without its title, its task only when it is not the chapter's goal, and gone after chapter 1; the premise cut at a sentence by 400; the main foe's look cut to 80 outside a fight. The people here are one line each (`Name [id]: role; attitude; trained Ranger 1; species; looks: …; voice: …; wants: …; view of your kind: …; ties: …; memories: …`; an entry holding more than words, a forgiveness count, stays an object), someone already named in the talk or the journal or with memories by a 50-character look, role, voice and motive cut to 80, ties' notes to 80; a view of the hero's kind in the game's own standard words is the stance alone (`standardRegard`), and the first memory in the game's own words for it (`standardRegardMemory`) is left out; people elsewhere not yet met by a 45-character role and without ties. The hero's backstory cut to 300 (120 between 2026-10-21 and 2026-10-22; none in a fight; no age beside a description; no age or background in a fight), species traits the game plays left out and the rest cut to 70; an action's casting time unsaid in spellReference; masteries only in a fight; `shortRests` only with a short rest offered or in a fight; `storyPreferences` only when not gritty and the Chronicler; `conversationWith` by id (and oneOnOne when it holds) when that person is here; world flags that are false or zero, and `world.regions` naming only this land, left out. The journal keeps its last four entries cut to 300 (one cut to 200 in a fight), the tale's opening entry up to its "Goal:" (the story, quest and errand say the rest), and none on the opening turn (`introduction` says it); the talk keeps its last two exchanges in full and the older ones cut to 200 (only the last two in a fight); a chronicle that holds only the tale's and chapter's headings is left out, and the tale's heading line always; a telling does not read again the journal entry and recent events that repeat engineResolved; `enemyHP` is left out when the foe is not the one fought. In a fight with a creature of the wilds the story's secret, resolution and people go; in any fight the errand (but at the errand's own place, since 2026-10-22), the resolution, the foe's look beside `encounter.appearance`, the story's people unless one is here or follows, the leads, the other heroes' looks past 50 characters, a chapter line past 300 and the place's description past 160. `now`'s calm-turn, travel and "is not at … and is not attacking" sentences are said in brief. Measured (the check's count): a town turn 1,877 → 1,308, a conversation 1,805 → 1,254, a creature fight 1,322 → 811 and 1,285 → 808, a brawl's telling 1,913 → 1,340, a party's fight 1,293 → 832, a wizard 2,219 → 1,513, a long life 4,786 → 4,010 (the memory block is by design) and in a fight 1,772 → 970; the four live turns 3,728/3,539/3,572/3,736 → 2,530/2,421/2,454/2,588 (about 31% less).
  - **The reply's effects list** (`replySchemaFor`, `flatReply`, `asFields`, `spelledOutSchema` in dm-schemas.cjs): the provider is asked for `narration` (first, so it streams), `dialogue`, `effects` and `suggestions` (last); every game effect is one item of `effects` holding one field (`{"actionId":"travel-p1"}`, `{"check":{…}}`, `{"remember":{…}}`; anyOf branches, each strict), so a reply that changes nothing writes `"effects":[]` instead of thirteen empty fields (the empty skeleton 67 → 14 by the check's count; output costs five times input). `read` passes every reply through `flatReply` at once (a list kind keeps its first two, any other its first; extras noted "effect given twice"), so `steadied`, `judge`, the validators, `phaseFields`, the second look, the rescue, the device and the campaign server see exactly the reply they always saw; a reply with every field spelled out (the stand-ins in the checks) is read as it is. A provider 400 naming the schema switches the DM process to `spelledOutSchema()` (the schema before this, `globalThis.__questboundSpelledOut`). The checks read the schema field by field with `asFields` (exported by dm-server.cjs too). The REPLY paragraph and the phase notes say "effects" where they said "null"; the schema went 2,021 → 2,226 tokens (cached).
  - **The standing prompt** again, every rule and every checked phrase kept: 8,853 → 8,326 by the check's count (38.5 → 36.0 thousand characters): explanations the engine's own lines make plain (the weapon masteries' meanings, how hands work) are a phrase each, the narrators' voices are only in their turn notes, `standingRules` is terser, and new: REPLY, the `ID` lines and `travel-<place id>` in HOW TO ACT, what an absent field means (the default too) and the people here as lines in HOW THE TURN ARRIVES. Measured on 2026-10-21 without API calls, a conversation turn (at the prices in spending.cjs: input 10¢, cached 1¢, output 50¢ a million): about 10,370 cached + 4,300 uncached + 230 output (≈ $0.00065) → about 10,000 + 2,960 + 150 (≈ $0.00047), some 27% less; the live probes should confirm it (dm-report's Cache block).
  - **Round 3: fewer requests and a lean telling** (the same day, the host: "the lowest possible cost per turn", round after round; `calmLines.js`, `suggestionPanel.js`, `tellingOnly` in dm-server.cjs, `suggestionNote` in dm-instructions.cjs, `rulesFor` in dm-rules.cjs; `verify-calm-lines.cjs`, `verify-lean-turns.cjs`, budgets in verify-dm-cache.cjs 5b). Requests count as much as their size: a player move used to cost about 1.4 requests (a turn, then a telling of what the engine resolved, then any scene cue).
    - **Calm moves told by the game** (see Routine fight moves told by the game): a rest, a healing draught away from a fight, a journey back to a place already visited, a search of the fallen with nobody but companions watching, an order to one companion (wait, come along, sent for) and the shield slung or taken up make no request when the words are a plain request (`plainCalmWords`) and the engine's lines are only that move's own (`routineCalmTurn`); anywhere new, a fight on arrival, the tale's foe or a magic find, a companion who comes round, someone else here who could react, or words that say more go to the Dungeon Master as before.
    - **The reply that chose the move tells it**: when the Dungeon Master's reply picks such a move itself (its actionId: "let us head back", "I take a room for the night"), its own narration, lines and suggestions are kept and the game adds only what the reply could not know (where the road ended, unless the reply names the place; what a search turned up; a rest seen through), instead of a second request only to tell it (`chosenTelling`; `routineTelling(...,chosen)` from `cast` in useDmTurn.js and server-turn.cjs). A recruitment the reply decided (join, decline, offer) is told by that reply too. Where the server mended the reply so that its narration may tell what did not happen (a journey that waits for a join, loot left out beside an action), the reply carries `retell:true` and the telling is asked for as before; a hesitant one (a check) and every check, spell ruling, chapter, lead, first visit, beyond the map and fight are still told after the roll by the Dungeon Master.
    - **Suggestions only when they will be seen**: the Suggestions panel is closed by default out of battle, so a device with it closed sends `suggest:false` (`suggestionsFlag` in suggestionPanel.js, with every request from useDmTurn.js (`post`, and both `table.turn` calls, which useSharedTable.js sends on); the campaign server keeps only false, the acting member's) and the server adds "SUGGESTIONS THIS TURN: none (the player keeps them closed): suggestions []." to this turn's note (before the narrator's voice, which stays last). Never in battle (stage combat or dying, a brawl, a turn order: the Say tab shows whatever a reply offers there), never in the standing prompt, and a reply that offers lines anyway keeps them. Opened later, the panel shows the game's stand-ins (replyIdeas) until the next turn. About 35 output tokens a reply.
    - **A telling reads only what a telling can use** (`tellingOnly`, after briefer, for a resolved turn or a scene cue): never what lasts the tale (sceneParts' first part) and, in a fight, nothing but this turn's part, so a telling still shares its earlier parts with the turn before it; always the journeys on offer and a cue's own words (`sceneTrigger` was read twice: the note says it); out of a fight also the attack figures, the map beyond where the hero stands (a place the engine's lines or the player's words name is kept), people elsewhere, offers to join, the older story so far (the tale's first line, the chapter under way and the facts about who is here stay) and the journal beyond two entries; a cue also the pack and the spoils.
    - **Rules named as whole words**: `rulesFor` matched spell names inside other words ("said" pulled in Aid, "Halloway" Hallow, "lighthouse" Light: about 500 tokens of spell text each time); a name now counts only as a whole word, a spell the hero does not have only when the words speak of casting or magic, and a scene cue (the game's own words) names none.
    - **Measured** (2026-10-21, no API calls; the checks' rough count, which overshoots the provider's): a check's telling 1,310 → 1,115, a greeting 1,993 → 989, four live turns' tellings 2,450–2,616 → 2,102–2,268 and their cues 2,253–2,445 → 1,838–2,030, a fight's telling 811 → 800, a brawl's 1,340 → 1,329; ordinary turns unchanged. A scripted solo session through the engine (`resolveTurn` with a stand-in Dungeon Master: talk, checks, a recruit, journeys typed and chosen, a fight, a search, rests, orders; 33 moves): 46 → 39 requests (1.39 → 1.18 a move, 15% fewer; tellings 19 → 12). At spending.cjs's prices, scaled to the live ordinary turn (about 9,900 cached, 3,100 uncached, 170 output), about $0.00068 → $0.00055 a move with the panel open and $0.00053 closed (19–22% less). What the live probes should watch is in the round-3 report (notes/reports/lean-turns-3.md).
- Fewer requests a turn (2026-10-22, the host: "merge telling into first call as you recommended. implement #3, #4, #5"; measured live that real play took about 1.5–1.8 requests a player turn and that a request after a pause of a few minutes got nothing cached; `toldAhead.js` (in `rulesFiles` after calmLines.js; `checkTelling`, `aheadTelling` exported by rules-engine.cjs, as is `sceneTrigger`), `sceneTriggers.js` `cueSpoken`, `fightLines.js` `routineTelling`, the judge, `steadied`, `secondLooked` and `cacheHoldFor` in dm-server.cjs, `settled`/`fitting` in dm-intent.cjs, `costOf` in spending.cjs (revision 6), `verify-told-ahead.cjs`; #3, greetings from the Speak chip, was another branch's):
  - **Tellings written ahead.** When a reply starts something the dice or the road decide, it also writes what is told once it is decided, so no second request is asked only to tell it. A check carries `outcomes` (`check.outcomes`: success and failure, each narration, up to two lines and up to one relationship; null in a fight or when an outcome hands over loot, brings someone in or reveals a place: CHECKS), and the reply's narration only begins the attempt; `checkTelling` (device: useDmTurn.js `ask`; table: server-turn.cjs) shows the opening and the outcome the dice chose (its lines from those present only) when the roll brought nothing but its own line (no fall, no fight, concentration kept), else the telling is asked for as before. A travel choice to a place not yet visited carries `arrival` (an effect of its own: narration and up to three first words of those there, by id, or of companions: HOW TO ACT); `aheadTelling` (asked by `routineTelling` for a move the reply chose that is not a routine calm one) shows the set-out and the arrival, lines from those there once the road is walked, when the road brought only the journey (no ambush or fight on arriving), else the telling is asked for. A discovery with travel already tells its arrival (OPEN WORLD: companions or someone introduced may speak on arriving). The judge keeps both only when sound (outcomes not in a fight, an arrival only beside a `travel-` choice; texts cut past 900, HP figures checked, lines only from those who may speak, at most one relationship each, checked as relationships are) and otherwise leaves them out (noted "check outcomes"/"arrival"), never a reason to ask again; the rejection log notes only that there were any (never their words). The schema went 2,226 → 2,730 tokens (cached) and the standing prompt 8,487 → 8,641 (budgets 2,800 and 8,810 in verify-dm-cache, raised by exactly that).
  - **Moves the reply chose, told by it.** Besides the routine calm moves (round 3): an offer taken up (`join:`; the engine's one join line) and, in a fight, a move the Dungeon Master read from the player's own words (its actionId or castCommand) whose result is routine (`routineFightTurn` with the words counted as answered): the reply's telling of the attempt, then the game's fight lines (not marked as told by the game: the Dungeon Master spoke). A fight's start or end, a kill, a critical hit, a foe badly hurt and the rest still go to the Dungeon Master after the roll.
  - **A moment's first words in its telling.** A move told by the Dungeon Master after the engine resolved it (a typed or tapped journey, a fight's end) sends `speakFirst` {speakerId, cue} when it brings a moment in which people would speak first (`sceneTrigger`: arriving somewhere for the first time, a return, a fight won, morning): the told phase's note says "PEOPLE SPEAK FIRST: <cue> ... <name> [id] speaks first" (never in the scene: tellingOnly drops it). At a table too (server-turn.cjs), where cues are never played. `withSceneTrigger(before,after,action,turn)` (settleTurn passes the turn recorded) plays no cue when those it would have speak first already spoke in that turn (`cueSpoken`: on arriving where people live, one of them; else anyone here) and keeps the moment as fired, so a device no longer asks the Dungeon Master again for them.
  - **Only those still there are heard.** `chosenTelling` (round 3) kept the reply's lines for a journey back, and a farewell from someone left behind made the game refuse the whole turn ("The reply included a character who cannot join this conversation.": commitDmTurn holds every line to who is present after the move). Lines are now kept only from those present once the move is made, in every telling written ahead, and since 2026-10-23 in every turn: `commitDmTurn` drops the lines of anyone not here once the move is made and returns the conversation as heard (`result.conversation`), never refusing the turn (the server checks speakers against who is here before the move, which it cannot see past; live that day a journey out of a written tale's starting place with farewells from the people left there was refused three times in a row and the tale stalled in chapter 1; verify-told-ahead case 4) "Here once the move is made" is the scene the Dungeon Master was shown: in a written tale the move's beats are applied to the preview before the request is built and to the turn only in settleTurn (withBeats), so `commitDmTurn` counts those present in `stagedPreview` of its result too (live that day the ending that freed The Last Ferry's captive refused the last blow at its foe eleven times in a row, the freed boy speaking in the telling, and the hero could never win; verify-staging, a beat that brings someone in). Someone here neither before nor after (a companion waiting elsewhere) still refuses the reply. Likewise someone the reply brings in (`introduce`) when the move itself takes the scene where nobody can be met (a fight on arriving at a place revealed, another place): the meeting is left out with their lines, never the whole turn (live that day at a shared table, "We head north, beyond the edge of the map." refused with "No one new can be met here right now."; the server keeps an introduction beside a discovery and cannot know how the road ends; verify-allies); where nothing moved, a meeting the game cannot make still refuses it.
  - **Fewer second asks.** An unmistakable request the reply did not act on (dm-intent `sure`: a known place by its whole name or a word only it has, a plain rest, draught or search, a way beyond the map) is acted on by the game at once, without the LOOK AGAIN request (noted "the game acted at once"): `settled(fault,reply)` keeps the reply's own telling and lines when they tell that very move and refuse nothing (`fitting`: the place named and a moving word; resting, drinking or searching words), else the game's one line; doubtful ones are still asked about. Mended in place (`steadied`): a known place given by its bare id ("inn") is the journey there; a search of the fallen (`loot`) when nothing here is left to search (no spoils, no fight: the game offers no loot choice) is told plainly ("You look again, but there is nothing left here to search.", the reply's telling and lines giving way, as they may tell of finds never there) instead of asking again: four of the asks again of 2026-10-21 were this.
  - **The cache held across pauses** (`cacheHoldFor`, `cacheHold`; OpenAI's prompt caching guide and Responses reference, read 2026-10-22). Live, a turn after a pause of more than about three minutes got nothing cached. GPT-5.6 and later (gpt-6-luna) keep a cache at breakpoints for a ttl (30 minutes by default, 12 hours on request; written at 1.25 and 2.5 times the input price, read at a tenth); `prompt_cache_retention` is deprecated there and is not sent (a request-level 12 hours would bill every turn's new scene at 2.5 times). The implicit breakpoint is the end of the last user message (this turn's part, new every turn), which is why nothing past the instructions was ever reused ("without a breakpoint after static content, a changing suffix can prevent reuse of the shared prefix"). So each turn request marks three explicit breakpoints (`prompt_cache_breakpoint` on an `input_text` block; a request may write four, the implicit one included): after the hero's features (instructions, schema and hero: `{mode:'explicit',ttl:'12h'}`), after what lasts the tale (`{mode:'explicit'}`; since 2026-10-23: live, who is here changed on most talking turns (memories, attitudes) and the land with every journey, so the one scene breakpoint after them missed and consecutive turns cached only the instructions, schema and hero, 11,038 of about 14,000 tokens) and after the last scene part that stays from turn to turn (what lasts the tale, the land, who is here; never the story so far, which changes with each turn's journal: `{mode:'explicit'}`, the request's own 30 minutes). Earlier models get `prompt_cache_retention:'24h'` (no write charge there). `.questbound-cache-hold` (read live): 12h (the default), 30m (the breakpoints, the hero's for 30 minutes) or off; checks pass `holdCache` to generate (off unless live). A provider 400 naming the ttl or 12h keeps the breakpoints for the request's own 30 minutes (`globalThis.__questboundNoLongTtl`; the Responses reference read that day still said 30m was the only value, the guide said 12h too); one naming the breakpoints drops them for the DM process (`globalThis.__questboundNoCacheBreakpoints`) and asks again at once with retention; one naming `prompt_cache_retention` drops that (`globalThis.__questboundNoCacheRetention`). requestParts reads a message given as blocks. Usage lines carry `hold` (12h, 30m or 24h) and, when the provider reports them, `written` (cache_write_tokens) and `written12h` (ttl_12h_tokens); spending.cjs `costOf` prices written tokens at 1.25 and 2.5 times the input price (lines without them as before), and dm-report.cjs prices line by line and shows "held across pauses" in its Cache block (the share cached after a pause of five minutes or more). The story writer is not held (a tale is written a few times a day; a 12-hour write would cost more than it saves).
  - **Measured** (no API calls; `C:/Users/itsbr/qb-work/notes/fewer-calls/session.cjs` and `cost.cjs`: a conversation-heavy session of 32 moves played through each worktree's own resolveTurn and generate with a stand-in model: talk, five checks, a recruit, somewhere new found, journeys to new places chosen and typed, arrivals with people, a missed plain request, a short fight with a battle cry, a search, rests, a journey back): 40 → 30 requests (1.25 → 0.94 a move, 25% fewer; tellings 11 → 6, cues 5 → 1, second looks 1 → 0), about $0.00062 → $0.00049 a move at spending.cjs's prices (21% less; replies with outcomes write more), $0.00044 (29% less) if the provider honours the scene breakpoint (about 650 more tokens a request cached). What the live probes should watch is in notes/reports/fewer-calls.md.
- Long requests (`longResponse`, `streamedResponse`, `patientDispatcher` and `requestFailure` in `ai-request.cjs`;
  `recordWriteFailure` in dm-server.cjs; `verify-dm-stream.cjs`, `verify-journeys.cjs`):
  - Why: on 2026-10-13 the first ready journey was cut off after 308 s with nothing logged. Node's own fetch (undici)
    gives up when headers take 300 s, or when a body is silent for 300 s.
  - So every tale the story writer writes (library journeys, story jobs, the old direct request) is asked for with
    `stream:true`. `streamedResponse` reads the server-sent events: text deltas, refusal deltas, and the final
    completed, incomplete or failed response. An `error` event counts as failed.
  - The request goes through an agent of Node's own fetch whose header and silence limits are ten minutes, made from
    the global dispatcher's class under `Symbol.for('undici.globalDispatcher.1')` (no package). The caller's timeout
    (420 s for the library and story jobs) is then the only bound.
  - Usage is recorded as before, cut-short replies included. A stand-in that answers plain JSON is still read.
  - Reasoning summaries are not requested: they need a verified OpenAI organization, and a 400 would fail every tale.
  - A failed library or story-job write is noted in `.questbound-diagnostics.jsonl`, live runs only. The note is
    `{write:'library'|'story-job', failure, status?, code?, reason?, seconds?}`, where failure is timeout, network,
    http, incomplete, failed, cut-off, refused, invalid-json, invalid-story, rejected, too-alike, no-story or ceiling,
    and never the provider's words. `node dm-report.cjs` lists them under "Background tales that failed".
  - `tale-library.cjs` `topUp` takes `onFailure` (revision 2; now 3, since journeys' covers come from the place library).
  - Hero drafts are not streamed: their 80 s limit is far inside undici's.
- Streaming and number checks (`verify-dm-stream.cjs`, `verify-cloud.cjs`): `askDm(..., {onNarration})` sends `stream:true`; a restarted DM answers game turns as NDJSON (`{narration}` lines, then `{reply}` or `{error}`), the gateways pass it through, and `DungeonMaster.js` shows the draft while busy. An older DM answers plain JSON and nothing changes. Campaign turns are streamed too (2026-10-21, table-stream): the account service asks the DM with `stream:true` (`askDungeonMaster`/`readDmStream` in account-server.cjs, still a 90 s timeout and the guest label) and the narration so far becomes the table's draft, which every member sees through their held wait (see The live table under Server turns and campaign edits); the reply is checked exactly as before, and an older DM's plain JSON is read as it always was. `checkedHp` softens HP figures in narration and dialogue that the context or the engine's HP lines do not support ("down to 3 HP" becomes "badly hurt"), and since 2026-10-22 every damage figure (`softDamage`: "for exactly 4 fire damage" becomes "hard", "takes 7 slashing damage" "takes a cut"). A streamed reply cut short (`response.incomplete`/`failed`) keeps what was written and its token use, and is asked again like any unusable reply (the draft restarts with the new attempt).
- Reliable turns (`generate` in dm-server.cjs, `verify-dm-reliable.cjs`, `dm-report.cjs`; the host, 2026-10-13: "a failed Dungeon Master turn should be rare: under 1 in 200, with the player never seeing a parse failure"):
  - Strict structured output: every request schema (turn phases, story, hero) is strict-valid, and verify-dm-reliable checks it. Keep every property required, `additionalProperties:false`, nullable as a type list, and enum values of their type.
  - Time: a turn has 80 s (`turnBudget`; the gateways wait 90). Every request gets at most what is left, and a retry or the second look starts only with 12 s left (`retryFloor`).
  - Mended in place (`steadied`, each noted as a second look for dm-report): a telling given as null is an empty one and a choice id copied with its brackets ("[flee]") is that id (both seen on 2026-10-21); a creature's ally of its own kind, or a pack, is left out (`repairedCreature`; before, the whole place or ambush was refused and asked again, up to a rescue that dropped it anyway); a refused discovery is noted with its shape (kind, travel, danger, lair and ally kinds, lengths, canDiscover: mechanical only); lines with no telling get a framing line, and an action with no telling a plain line; a telling over 1800 characters is cut at a sentence; a ruling with no spell awaiting one is dropped (if it denied a spell the hero knows and can pay for, with no castingIssue, in words that are not a question, it becomes the `castCommand`: the rules are the authority; if it denied the very cast castingIssue refused, the cast is dropped too); a deny or clarify applies nothing, and the DM never sets damage the game rolls; a join or check beside another action is the turn and the journey waits (dm-intent `started` counts recruitment); a decline or offer beside an action is dropped.
  - Quiet retries: a reply that cannot be used (unreadable, cut short, outside the schema or the validators, any error while checking it) is asked again with the reason, up to twice. A cut-short one gets twice the room. A passing provider fault (timeout, 5xx, network) is tried once more.
  - Rescue (`rescued`): after that, the last reply's offending parts are removed one at a time and the rest is checked again with the same validators; nothing invalid is ever applied. A spell awaiting a ruling gets a clarify (nothing spent); a resolved turn whose telling is lost keeps the engine's results with "The moment settles as the dice fell."; an unreadable reply whose narration string was finished keeps that telling alone. The second look still runs on the result. Only if nothing is usable does the player see "The Dungeon Master's reply could not be used this time, so nothing was applied. Try again, perhaps in other words." (with the Report chip).
  - Logs: usage lines carry `ask` (1 = the turn). Diagnostics hold `incomplete reply` rejections with their shape, `aside` on a second look's rejected reply, and `{turn:'asked again'|'rescued'|'failed',reasons,asks,phase}` for each troubled turn. `node dm-report.cjs [--days N] [--dir D]` shows turns, failed turns seen per 200 (goal under 1), host and guests apart, and mended quietly (asked again, rescued, in place).
  - At a table, the cast the DM wrote out is settled with its own words (`normalizedCommand` in server-turn.cjs), as on a device. Copy ai-request.cjs, dm-instructions.cjs, dm-schemas.cjs and dm-replies.cjs with (or before) dm-server.cjs when deploying by hand: dm-server requires them.
- Holding the Dungeon Master to what the player asked (`dm-intent.cjs`, the second look in `generate`, `verify-dm-intent.cjs`, `dm-probe.cjs`): `context.now` is a few plain sentences the game works out (where the player is, whether a fight is on, what became of the foe, which journeys are on offer, what is left of the turn, the named people here) and the model reads first; `WHAT IS TRUE NOW` tells it to believe them over the premise, journal and recent events. After an ordinary reply, `secondLook(body,reply)` checks it against the player's words: a plain request the game offers (a known place by name or by a word only it has, somewhere new while `canDiscover`, a rest, a draught, a lead handed in, a search of the fallen) that the reply did not act on, a known place chosen when the words asked for somewhere new, a fight spoken of as still going on when none is, a telling in which someone joins that recruitment did not record ('unrecorded join'), a way off the map ("beyond the map": sure when a direction is named and its `beyond-` choice is offered), or the arrival at a peopled kind of place (settlement, camp, road, water, shrine, a starting place) told with `peopleHere` 0 and nobody introduced ("nobody here": asks once for the most noticeable person, never settled by the game), gets one more request with `LOOK AGAIN:` in the note after the scene (everything before it stays cached) and `low` thinking; when the request is unmistakable (`sure`: a whole place name or a six-letter word only it has, no question, no hedge such as tell/ask/if/later/tomorrow), the game starts the action itself with a one-line telling (`settled`, with no suggestions); since 2026-10-22 it does so at once, without the second request, keeping the reply's own telling when it tells that move (see Fewer requests a turn), so only doubtful requests are asked about again. Leads are never settled by the game. A written tale's open choice that the player's words make (at least two of one option's words and half of them, as its label says it; one option only; not negated) when the reply recorded none is asked about once (`taleChoiceNamed`, kind "tale choice"; never settled by the game: live on 2026-10-23 "Say it aloud, Ness. Tell everyone why you rang the bell." made The Last Ferry's "Make her say why, aloud" and the choice lapsed to fate at the tale's end). Questions, plans, speech, fights, spell rulings, resolved turns, scene cues and out-of-character talk are left alone. A reply that is not JSON is first repaired when the fault is small: markdown fences or prose around the object, or a reply sent twice as two objects back to back or two output items, read as its first (`repairedJson`/`firstObject`/`readJson` in ai-request.cjs, also used by the story writer and hero maker). Such a reply is noted as a second look "repaired". A refusal is told to the player as such ("declined to answer") and never retried. Anything else that cannot be used is recorded with its shape only (`replyShape`: length, how it starts and ends, the provider's output item types, refusal or incomplete; never its words); see Reliable turns. A provider rate limit (`rate_limit_exceeded`) is waited out twice. Second looks are logged to `.questbound-diagnostics.jsonl` (`secondLook`, `outcome`) and counted by `dm-report.cjs`. `.questbound-dm-effort` sets how hard the play model thinks about ordinary turns, live (`turnEffort`; default none). Measure with `node dm-probe.cjs [rounds]` (acts when asked) and `node dm-judgment.cjs [rounds]` (judges well: grudges and bonds, deeds recorded, gold and goods, fight tempo, resolved results told as they fell; 28 situations since the social wave added an early apology that is not forgiveness, amends that settle a grudge, a one-on-one answer from a grudge-holder and yielding in a brawl; 48 of 48 on the earlier 24 on 2026-10-02) against the running DM (paced at one request every three seconds, so it never uses the provider's per-minute allowance that players need: an unpaced probe did on 2026-10-02): 24 of 30 before the second look, 29 of 30 after; the scratch chapter probe closed leads 16 of 16. Both use the paid AI: run them only when the host asks. `knownPlaces` now carry a compass `bearing` from where the hero stands, and `cleared` once a place's threat is beaten.
- Routine moves told by the game, away from a fight too (calmLines.js, 2026-10-21: see Round 3 under The request in parts and a leaner turn: a rest, a draught, a journey back, a search with nobody watching, an order to a companion and the shield; their line book `calmLineBook` (the same three voices, at least eight ways a case, six for the shield, 498 lines with the companions' answers in `calmBarks`, each about the hero with its third-person twin) is held by verify-calm-lines.cjs to the same rules as the fight lines: no numbers or rule words, seeded, a line said lately passed over; a calm move told by the game is marked `told:'game'` with no fight round; since 2026-10-22 no line announces that nothing happened, the first journey and the first long rest after something that mattered go to the Dungeon Master, and a journey or rest after two calm moves in a row told by the game does too: see Making it move).
- Making it move (2026-10-22; the written-tales spec's Phase 0 and Phase A, C:/Users/itsbr/qb-work/notes/stories/SPEC.md sections 3.4 to 3.6 and 6, with the diagnosis beside it, after the host found recent games "incredibly boring"; for every tale, the AI-written journeys too):
  - **The chapter's truth in the scene** (A1): `context.quest.truth` (the chapter's `turn`, at most 200 characters, cut at a sentence: `truthText` in dmSceneRules.js, `truthOf` in dm-server.cjs for a device that sends only `revealedWhenAchieved`) and `quest.next` as the next chapter's title are read by the model out of a fight (briefer), neither in one, nor in the telling of a chapter closed (engineResolved "Chapter N complete: …": the quest there is already the next chapter, and the found chapter's truth is in that line; only that telling, so the cached part holding the quest changes about once a chapter, where dropping it from every telling would flip it turn by turn). The story-advance choice reads "its truth found where the goal points, told in this reply (never before)".
  - **The standing prompt** (A2, dm-instructions.cjs): PLAY TO FIND OUT, scoped to ordinary turns (every reply moves something; the foe and anyone with a motive who could have learned of it act between scenes, shown in what they leave, never as harm the game has not made; a failure opens another way at a price; looking closely finds something specific, unless nothing is there; people want something now and say what they would share; companions speak up, not one on one; an arrival shows one concrete detail and who is there; most replies end on something to answer); the narration line (one concrete thing seen, heard or smelled, what this moment changed, and, unless the hero is dead, an end the player can act on; dice and harm as action, never numbers or rule words); ENGINE RESULTS ("never subtract again; tell how hurt someone is, not the figure", where it said to quote the figures); CHECKS (the failure text changes the situation: the thing found at a cost in time, trust, noise or position, or a worse way; never gold, items, harm the dice did not deal or someone new, which a failure written ahead cannot record; never "nothing happens"); THE LONG TALE (the chapter's title, goal and truth; when the player pursues the goal where it points, the find is certain, no check: tell the truth in that very reply, any cost in the narration, and select story-advance (the reply's one action); a journey or fight there is told as it falls and the find comes with the next move, since a telling selects nothing; never elsewhere or before; the telling of "Chapter N complete: …" tells the find if not yet told (the errand's, closed by the game, never was), then carries on; paced by the tale's length, not "like a campaign"; a lead `ticked` is the player's own mark); ERRAND (the game closes chapter 1 at the cleared place; keeper pays when next spoken with, `errand.rewardDue`); FIRST TURN (no "(orders, aim or hook)" restated); the dampers ("Never conjure guards" became "Never claim a fight, damage, initiative or movement the engine has not resolved; the law, the grieving and the vengeful arrive as people (introduce), who can be talked to, fled from or fought"; an offered journey: "what waits there may still answer them on arrival"); LOOT ("a failed search finds no treasure", which no longer reads as "nothing"). The now-line "Nothing has to happen first" is gone (dm-intent.cjs `situation`, dm-server.cjs compact and briefer). Still byte-identical for every player, hero, phase, retry and second look. Not yet: the WRITTEN TALES paragraph and THE LONG TALE's "quest.where" (Phase B). The standing prompt is 9,015 tokens by verify-dm-cache's count (8,660 before Phase A, 8,951 before the project review's wording of 2026-10-22), its budget 9,101 (unchanged by the review); the schema is unchanged.
  - **A failure that changes nothing is asked again, once** (dm-replies.cjs `deadEnd`, the judge in dm-server.cjs): a check's failure written ahead whose last sentence ends on the attempt coming to nothing ("you find nothing", "nothing comes of it", "in vain", "no sign of the courier anywhere") with nothing in it that moves the scene (no clause after those words: ", and the guard hears you", "; the drawer slams shut", ", so you must ask at the gate"; none before them in the same sentence: "The clerk sends for the watch; you learn nothing more."; no earlier words of a consequence: an alarm, someone hearing or seeing you, footsteps, a cost, something broken) and nobody speaking is refused with the reason once; a second dead end (and any in a rescue) is kept with its outcomes, noted as the second look "check outcomes: dead end kept" for dm-report. Out of a fight only. Until the project review of 2026-10-22 only the words after the match were read, for a turn word: good failures were asked again up to twice and a rescue dropped the outcomes, so one check cost four requests.
  - **The errand closes where it is found** (A3; `errandFound`, `withErrandFound` and `withErrandReward` in adventureRules.js, `errandPaidTurn` in dm-replies.cjs): a tale with an errand and no `staging` closes chapter 1 in the very step that clears the errand's place (the lair won), out of a fight: `adventureStep` runs it, so a device's preview, its commit and the campaign server's (server-turn.cjs) agree, and the telling of the fight's end holds advanceChapter's "Chapter 1 complete: … <the chapter's truth>" and "Chapter 2 begins" lines (and pays the chapter's experience); a save stalled with its place already cleared closes it at its next move. The reward stays owed (`story.errand.rewardDue`, `validErrand` accepts it) only when this very step cleared the place (`withErrandFound(result,hero,before)`): a save stalled with its place cleared before this build closes its chapter owing nothing, since its keeper may already have paid while it stalled. The model still reads the errand's reward after chapter 1 while it is owed. `withErrandReward` in `settleTurn` settles it once keeper is here and the turn's loot gives gold or goods with no gold paid, in a turn spoken with keeper (`conversation.npcId`) or one whose loot reason names the reward (reward, promised, purse, payment, fee, wages, bounty, owed); a purchase, a gift from someone else or a find while keeper is about does not (or keeper dies, or the tale is two chapters on). On an ordinary turn spoken with keeper while it is owed (`errandPaidTurn`) the turn's note says "ERRAND REWARD: … keeper hands over the promised reward (…) in this reply, said in the telling, with loot", one item of that loot may carry magic (`magicRewardTurn`: the chapter's "Chapter 1 complete" was told with the lair's fall, where the fallen leave no room for loot), and the judge pays the promised purse itself (the old "the game paid") only when the telling or keeper's words hand the reward over (reward, purse, coins, gold, pay, promised...) and the reply's loot is missing.
  - **Calm moves** (A4; calmLines.js): the lines that announced nothing happened ("without incident", "unremarkable", "much as you left it", "Nothing tries to eat you", "Nobody throws a parade" and the like, 31 in the three voices with their table twins) are plain movement lines now, and verify-calm-lines.cjs refuses any that come back. `calmAfterMilestone`: the first journey and the first long rest since a milestone (the story log's quest or death line, or its victory line when the fight before it, "Fought the X at Place.", was at a lair or the victory names the tale's own foe: a lair cleared, the main foe beaten, a chapter, lead or tale closed, a death; an ambush or a risky place won is not one, since the project review of 2026-10-22) go to the Dungeon Master; `calmToldInARow`/`calmInARow` 2: a journey or a rest after two calm moves in a row told by the game does too (orders, draughts, searches and the shield count toward the two but are never sent for it). A move the Dungeon Master chose keeps its own telling either way. Both reasons prefer the Dungeon Master, never require it (`routineCalmTurn` with `lenient` passes them over): when that request fails (a spending ceiling, the guests' pause, a rate limit, an outage, no connection) the game tells the move itself with `calmFallback` (calmLines.js, exported by rules-engine.cjs): on a device for any failure but a scene that changed meanwhile, at a table for any; before the review a guest past the ceiling who had made two game-told moves could never travel or rest again. Spec estimate about +$0.00002 a move.
  - **Story context restored** (A5; dm-server.cjs briefer and compact): `story.errand` while the hero fights at the errand's own place; the hero's backstory up to 300 characters out of a fight (120 before); a place not yet visited within five miles in this land keeps 80 characters of its description in its knownPlaces line (`milesOf`, `placeLine`). Measured by verify-dm-cache's count (before → after): to the mill 717 → 746, a town 1,308 → 1,370, a conversation 1,254 → 1,316, a fight 800 → 827 and 808 → 835, a wizard 1,513 → 1,578, a long life 4,010 → 4,058; every scene budget held without a raise.
  - **Rule talk** (A6): `softDamage` (see Streaming and number checks); `situation` says "The player has just arrived at X." on a turn whose engine lines walked the road (a reply had told a player who had just walked there that they were "already at" it); `bareFoeName` and `foeOpening` (see Fights in the wilds; fixed where the line is made, encounterRules.js startWildFight); `softDamage` since the review: a dice sum is one figure ("for 1d8+3 slashing damage" becomes "hard"), "a total of" goes with it, a softened figure that opens a sentence gets its capital, radiant, necrotic and poison read after "deals" ("a searing wound", "a withering wound", "a dose of poison"), and out of character (no engine lines) nothing is softened, so the Dungeon Master can explain the dice.
  - **Leads ticked by hand are notes** (A7; `noteLead` in storyRules.js, exported by rules-engine.cjs; Adventure.js `markLead`; the `lead` campaign edit in campaigns.cjs): `noted:true` on the lead (validStory accepts it), a journal note "Your note: <title>", the circle ticked but faint with "· your note"; no experience, story-log line or deed; ticked again it is unticked; a lead seen through stays so (its circle disabled). The Dungeon Master reads it as `ticked` in quest.openLeads, still open, and its `lead-done` still pays once. The Quest tab says "Tick a lead as a note to yourself; the tale closes it when it is truly settled." (before, a tick paid 288 XP at level 1).
  - **A first greeting carries an agenda, never a secret** (A8; `greetingAgenda` in greetings.js): the game's written greeting for someone the hero has never talked with, not set against them nor following, adds to the aside a public want, something they would say or show to a stranger: the first clause of their motive (cut before "even if", "without", "before", "while", "though" and the like; "Oona is hoping to …", "Oona looks to be hoping to …"), from keeper's or mara's motive or the library's own motive of someone met from the people library (who carries `pool` and `hook`), never a resident's (the story writer's residents carry the tale's secrets in their motives) nor anyone the Dungeon Master made up. Never a hook: a library hook is by nature the person's secret thread, and its words cannot show which hook is public (on the deployment check of 2026-10-22, 7 of the 18 library hooks that passed the wide hush still held a secret: a predator fed under the ferry, a bell wagered away, a grave marker moved). Nothing at all when the whole motive, not just the part said, touches secrecy or hiding, what someone saw, knows or must not learn, crime, bribes, theft, smuggling, deception or a false name, a hidden past, origin or identity, love kept from others, blackmail, betrayal, debts and what is owed or borrowed, shame, blame, amends, fear, curses, pacts and dreams, a single creature kept or fed, or the hero, the party, outsiders and strangers (`agendaHush`, wide on purpose: no agenda is better than a risky one); never "you". Before the project review of 2026-10-22 the whole motive was "plainly" told, and a hidden prince's "without drawing attention to himself" gave the tale away; the deployment check then found 44 of the 286 library people who got a hook agenda giving away a secret ("paid to keep quiet", "a judge he once bribed", "a forbidden lover"). Of the 690 library people 179 now carry one. verify-agendas.cjs checks the deployment check's leaks (as hooks and as motives) and, when the host's people library is on the PC (this checkout's `.questbound-people/`, QUESTBOUND_DATA's, or the main checkout's for a worktree; `QB_PEOPLE_LIBRARY` to name a file), runs every person through it against a secret-marker list of its own (`--list` prints every agenda). Still no request, so the Dungeon Master's first reply (which reads the feed) carries it on.
  - **Discoveries mended in place** (A9; `repairedDiscovery` in dm-replies.cjs, the judge in dm-server.cjs): a kind, bearing or land in words of its own is read as the map's (a castle a settlement, "north-east" NE, sea coast), a missing danger is safe, a distance in text is read, a description too short is made from the name and kind, and a lair whose creature the game cannot run is a risky place. A place the map still cannot take (`canDiscover` false: a fight, a full map, an old crossroads save, a telling) that is only seen or heard of is never a reason to ask again: a known place named as new, with its journey on offer, is that journey; else the place is left out and kept as words (`worldEvent` "Name, to the north: description") when the reply records nothing lasting of its own, never in a fight or while dying. One the telling walks the hero to (`travel` true) would tell an arrival the game never made (the project review, 2026-10-22: "You tear free of the wolf and scramble up to Thornback Ridge" with the fight still on): it is asked again once with the reason, then the telling becomes "There is no breaking away toward X while the fight goes on." or "The way to X is not open to you just yet." (`shutWay`, also in a rescue). Before, nine replies of one rough session were asked again for it.
  - **Words while dying** (`dyingTelling` and `dyingLines` in calmLines.js, exported by rules-engine.cjs; useDmTurn.js `ask`, server-turn.cjs): typed words that are not a death save (dmCommands.js reads those) are answered by the game, a short line pointing at the Death save in the narrator's own voice (`dyingLines` by narrator: eight each for the Chronicler, Wick and Sable since the project review, each with a twin told of the hero by name at a table of several heroes; the device's narrator, at a table the acting player's), seeded and never the line just said, marked `told:'game'`, with no request; out of character still reaches the Dungeon Master.
  - **The checks**: verify-journeys (the pause, which counts as no failure and no write), verify-chapters (the truth read and cut, not read in a chapter's closing telling, find where found, the errand closed at its place, its reward, what is not the reward, the payment turn's note, magic and purse, a stalled save owing nothing), verify-server-turn (the errand at a table), verify-calm-lines (milestones, an ambush won not one, two in a row, the fallback on a device and at a table, no flat lines), verify-dm-freedom (discoveries walked to and seen), verify-dm-intent (no damper, just arrived), verify-dm-freedom (the standing prompt's words, discoveries), verify-told-ahead (a dead-end failure asked again once, failures with a consequence kept), verify-dm-stream (damage figures, out of character), verify-encounters ("The", a creature's own name, plural names, a creature fled from keeping its name and picture), verify-experience and verify-campaign-edits (notes), verify-greetings and verify-agendas (the agenda: a public want from the motive, never a hook, never a secret, over the host's people library too), verify-death (words while dying, by narrator), verify-brief-mode and verify-story-jobs (the new wording), verify-dm-cache (the budget), verify-subclasses and verify-legal (the Artificer's names), verify-presentation (aria-checked).
  - **Deploying it**: first rebuild `dist-phone` and restart the account service (the rules files close chapter 1 at the cleared place), then copy the DM files: the standing prompt tells the model "the game closes chapter 1" and no longer to move the tale on after a report, so a DM ahead of the rules would leave an errand tale stalled (the project review, 2026-10-22). dm-instructions.cjs, dm-replies.cjs, dm-intent.cjs and then dm-server.cjs are live at once (run `node -e "require('./dm-server.cjs')"`); tale-library.cjs is revision 5 (the pause fix), reloaded through dm-server's literal; the rules files (adventureRules, calmLines, storyRules, turnRules, encounterRules, greetings, dmSceneRules, dmChoiceRules, characterRules, subclass files, spellOptions, weaponRules, saveMigrations) and campaigns.cjs and server-turn.cjs need the account service restarted; `dist-phone` rebuilt for the screens (Adventure.js, ui.js, useDmTurn.js).
  - **What the live A/B should watch** (the spec's six scripted moments, about 1¢, the main session only): whether a chapter's truth is told where its goal points (and story-advance selected) rather than withheld or blurted early (watch the "never before" side: the truth is now in the scene); whether failed checks move the scene (and how often a dead-end failure is asked again or kept: dm-report's asked-again count by reason and the second look "dead end kept"); whether the payment turn's note brings the reward with loot ("the game paid" should be rare); whether replies end on something to answer and stay 2–3 sentences; the walk back from a cleared lair (one DM telling, the find told in the lair's telling, the reward paid by keeper once); "already at" contradictions gone; damage figures gone from tellings; cost per move (spec estimate about +$0.00003 a move for Phase A) and asked-again rate in dm-report.
- Routine fight moves told by the game (2026-10-17, the host's words: "Let the game narrate routine fight moves: a plain attack, Dodge or 'end my turn' currently asks the AI to describe it, even though the dice cards already show what happened. The game could write those itself and call the AI only for a fight's start, kills, critical hits and every few rounds." and "make sure 3 has plenty of dialogue options to pick from."; `fightLines.js` (in `rulesFiles` after playbackRules.js; `routineTelling`, `routineFightTurn` and `fightTelling` exported by rules-engine.cjs), `verify-fight-lines.cjs`): in a creature fight, a brawl or while dying, a move the engine resolves alone (an attack or throw, the off hand, Dodge, End turn, a draught, Second Wind, Action Surge, a class knack, a death save, a spell the rules roll without a ruling, a wand, a two-move turn of these) whose words are a plain move (`plainFightWords`: a chip's sentence or a short typed command; no speech, quotes, questions, joined clauses, tricks or aims at a body part) is told by the game with no request (unless a written tale's beat is due after it: the move then goes to the Dungeon Master with the beat, see Written tales (runtime)): `routineTelling` (asked by `cast` in useDmTurn.js before any request, so it works offline too, and by `cast()` in server-turn.cjs at a table, with the acting player's narrator and brutality) settles it through the same `settleTurn` with `{told:'game', fightRound}`, which `recordedTurn` keeps on the playback turn (extra fields, which older builds and saves ignore). `routineFightTurn(before,after,action,words,{hero,health,afterHealth,events,waiting})` sends to the Dungeon Master as before: a fight's first move, its end or any change of stage, a kill, knock-out or fall (anyone's), a critical hit, the hero's natural 1, a natural 1 or 20 on a death save, a foe dropping to half, anyone joining, a foe's signature trick, a spell ruling, grapple/shove/flee/give-potion/party-pass, any other words, and one turn in every `fightCadence` (3) rounds (`fightRoundKey`; each death save its own). The words come from `fightLineBook` (The Chronicler, Wick the Lamplighter, Sable the Bard; blows by weapon kind and size, misses, grazes, follow-ups, spells, the foe's answer by kind and size, bands, friends and other heroes struck; every case at least twelve ways per voice, `fightLineCases`; 1,171 lines with the barks) and `fightBarks` (taunts and cries of people who fight, band shouts, sounds of beasts, the restless dead and monsters, companions' cheers, winces and warnings): in a brawl a bark is the person's own dialogue line, a talking creature's is quoted in the narration, a following companion's is their line (only people present and standing speak). Seeded by story, turn, action, narrator and the engine's lines (every device and the server write the same words); a line said in the last twelve turns is passed over while others are left; `!` lines are grittier and left out under Restrained. Lines describe and never count: no numbers, rule words, kills or luck (verify-fight-lines checks the book). Measured on 2026-10-17: the game tells about 43% of the moves in a skirmish and 55% in a long fight; `fightCadence`, "foe below half" and "critical hit" are the levers (provisional). A game-told turn is written to the journal like any chip turn. A new line goes into the book under its case in all three voices. At a table of several heroes (2026-10-21, see Named at a table of several heroes) every line telling the hero's own move, and every line with you or your, carries a hand-written third-person twin after a '|' ('You step in and cut low.|{hero} steps in and cuts low.'; 785 twins: the hero by name, they/their after, never a guessed gender); `flForm` takes the twin at a table, the same seeded entry either way, `{hero}` is the acting hero's first name (their whole name when another hero there shares it, `flHeroName`); spoken barks name the hero they are said to ("Is that all you have, Kara?"), and a talking creature's tag says whom ("The bandit snarls at Kara, …", the bark itself plain). verify-fight-lines checks every twin: present where needed, no you/your (speech aside), naming {hero}, the line's other names kept, a name with a singular verb and they with a plural one, short. A new line about the hero needs its twin.
- Legal (`legalText.js`, `Legal.js`, `verify-legal.cjs`, `PRIVACY.md`, `TERMS.md`, `public/privacy.html`, `public/terms.html`, `public/permissions.html`, `PERMISSIONS.md`): the privacy policy, terms of use and licence notices are written once in `legalText.js` and shown in the game (Settings → About, the title screen's Privacy · Terms, a line under account creation) and as pages the share gateway serves before pairing (linked from the pairing page). `node verify-legal.cjs --write` regenerates the pages and documents; the check asserts that the words match the code (during the beta: saves stay in the browser and no account, email, password or recovery-code feature is described, the guest identity's 90 and 60 days, the guest label sent to OpenAI, shared tables with 20 encrypted versions and 7-day invites; what a feedback note carries: the last two turns and any errors the game showed you, "Include what happened", Google Fonts, Cloudflare, OpenAI), that both SRDs are attributed in their licences' exact words, and that no file names an unlicensed book. The host is the controller; the developer receives no data. `legalUpdated` is `23 October 2026` (the texts were rewritten for local saves on 10 October, dated 13 October for the play counts, session ratings, the content check, mutes, reports and bans, and 23 October for tales written for a player being kept in the compendium and offered to other players, never with their hero, words, idea's text or progress (the policy's "Tales written for you" and the agreement's first point; verify-legal checks both against dm-server.cjs), so every browser agreed again each time; verify-legal fails if `accountsRequired` turns true until the texts describe accounts again) and is the one source of the terms version: `account-server.cjs` reads it from `legalText.js` (`currentTerms`), and registering or signing in needs that `termsVersion` and `ageConfirmed`. Playable content: the SRD 5.2 and 5.1 (Half-Elf), the SRD subclass of each class plus three Questbound subclasses a class (2026-10-04), the Artificer (five specialities) and the Goblin at the host's choice (2026-10-02), all in Questbound's own words: never copy text from a book that is not an SRD, never name one in the game, and never use another publisher's settings, creatures or characters (the Dungeon Master and story writer carry an OWN WORLD rule; `Underdark` is not SRD wording).
