The way reaches the rules through the sentence, exactly as a typed cast ("I cast Burning Hands at the enemy using a 2nd-level slot.", "I cast Detect Magic on me as a ritual.", or a started "I cast Hold Person at 3rd level on " for a spell that needs a target), which dmCommand reads, so a table's campaign server reads it too; a friend's heal carries its own action (`partyTarget` and the slot), and `server-turn.cjs` accepts the casts it offers (`spellActions(...).flatMap(c=>[c,...(c.casts??[])])`). `actionCost` keeps Bonus for bonus-action spells in the organizer; the action guide skips its Back chip and says Choose for a spell with a choice.
- Spells for friends and lasting spells (`spellBuffRules.js`, `lastingSpellRules.js`, `spellRules.js`, `masteryRules.js` boons, `storyPreferences.js`, `verify-spell-effects.cjs`): Bless, Heroism, Guidance, Resistance, Shield of Faith, Protection from Evil and Good, Longstrider and Aid reach friends (`allySpell`, `allySpellLimit`: Bless and Aid three, Heroism and Longstrider one more for each slot above): those named ("on me, Bram and Mara", "on the party"; `alliesNamed`, a name that is no friend sends the spell to a ruling) or, with nobody named, `defaultAllies` (Bless and Aid: the caster, then the other standing heroes, then companions here; the rest the caster). On another hero (`h:<id>`) or a companion they leave a boon in `game.boons[key]` ({id, by, amount?, type?, used?}; shared world, `validBoons`), read by `attackBoost`/`blessRoll`, `boonResisted`, `wardEdge` and the turn's saves; Aid raises hit point maxima (`game.aided` for companions). A concentration spell's boons end with its caster's concentration (`endBoons`), and a long rest ends them all. Lasting spells (`lastingSpells`: Moonbeam, Flaming Sphere, Call Lightning, Spirit Guardians, Insect Plague, Wall of Fire, Black Tentacles, Heat Metal, Spiritual Weapon, Vampiric Touch) deal their damage again at the end of a creature's turn in the area (`lastingTurn`) or when the caster uses them again as a bonus action or action (`spellAgain`/`againError`; chips from `sustainChoices`/`sustainOptions`, added by `sustainActions` in turnResourceRules.js in creature fights and brawls); the caster's concentration keeps {level, dc, target, fresh} so another device can play it. Hex and Hunter's Mark move to a new creature with a bonus action once theirs drops (`moveMark`; `markGone` says lost or gone). Fire burns webs away (`websBurn`). A spell that drops a person kills them as a weapon does (`harmCreature`). Settings → Story → Rules "Use Shield when it saves you" (`storyPreferences().autoShield`, on by default) reaches the rules through `rulesHero` (`autoShield:false` on the hero the rules see, never saved; at a table the turn's `autoShield`). Aid, repeating area damage and multi-target conditions beyond these are ruled by the DM or deal only their first effect.
- Species spells (`speciesLineages` in characterRules.js, `lineageSpells`/`speciesSpells`/`speciesUseLimit`/`speciesSpellSource`/`spellSourceLine` in spellOptions.js, `lineageDetails` in characterOptions.js): wherever spells are listed they say where a species spell comes from (the host, 2026-10-21, of a Wood Elf Monk with Druidcraft "without any multiclasses"): the Cast… chip's detail and the slot organizer's Free way name the lineage ("Druidcraft · Wood Elf"; 'Infernal Tiefling', 'Tiefling' for Thaumaturgy), the action guide and the Character Sheet's spell reference add "A gift of your Wood Elf lineage, not of your class." (with the free castings left), and the builder's lineage traits say the spells come "with your lineage, whatever your class"; a spell the hero's class gives them too names no lineage (verify-species.cjs). the SRD 5.2 lineages in our own words. The elves' lineage is their species (see Species; `fixedLineage`, used by `spellOptions.heroLineage`): a Drow (or a hero saved as a Dark Elf) is of the Drow lineage (Dancing Lights; Faerie Fire at 3; Darkness at 5), a High Elf knows Prestidigitation (Detect Magic at 3, Misty Step at 5; the free casting comes before a ritual in the slot organizer), and a Wood Elf Druidcraft (Longstrider at 3, Pass without Trace at 5). A Gnome chooses Forest or Rock and a Tiefling Abyssal, Chthonic or Infernal (every Tiefling knows Thaumaturgy), in the builder (`form.lineage`, validated by buildError). Heroes made before lineages have none, except an old plain Elf, who reads as a High Elf. Each species spell of level 1+ can be cast once a long rest without a slot (cost 'species', `game.speciesUsed`; a Forest Gnome's Speak with Animals as many times as their proficiency bonus), and with slots by a hero who has them; the free cast is preferred while available. `castingAbility(hero)` is the class's ability, or the best of Int/Wis/Cha for a hero without one.
- Killing and dying (`deathRules.js`, `verify-death.cjs`): every drop to 0 HP goes through `fallAtZero` (stage `dying`, attackers turn away; damage past 0 of at least max HP kills outright; `where`, the map location where they fell (`fallPlace`), is kept on `dying` and `death`, optional for older saves). The only dying action is `death-save` (nat 1 = two failures, nat 20 = up with 1 HP where they fell, three successes = wake at the start with 1 HP, three failures = stage `dead`); anything else typed while dying is answered by the game itself (see Making it move: Words while dying). A dead hero comes back only by Revivify (see Death, revival and laying to rest); otherwise they are laid to rest and gone. A player's blow that drops an NPC kills them (`game.npcFate`), unless they set out to knock them out; someone unconscious can be finished off. The story foe ends `foeFate` slain or subdued, and `storyText` writes the matching line. Brutality (Restrained/Gritty/Brutal) is a per-device setting (`storyPreferences.js`, Settings → Story) sent as `storyPreferences`; deaths are always described precisely.
- Death, revival and laying to rest (2026-10-11, the host's words: "make sure that interaction still works after death so that the player can go back to the menu and restart" and "if a character dies, they should be deleted, unless the player can be revived somehow. we can add buyable revives as well further down the line."; `deathRules.js` `revivalSpell`/`revivalOptions`/`reviveHero`/`deathIsFinal`/`partyRevivalError`/`boughtRevivals`, `reviveFriend` in adventureRules.js, `App.js` `finalDeath`, `layHeroToRest` (`heroSaveActions` in useHeroSaves.js), the epitaph in `Adventure.js`, `rosterRules.js`, `verify-death.cjs`, `verify-encounters.cjs`): while dying nothing changed (death saves, a friend's draught, a companion's Medicine or a healer's word carrying them home, a Periapt). Once dead, Revivify (SRD 5.2: 3rd level, a 300 gp diamond consumed; `revivalSpell`) can bring them back with 1 HP where they fell, the foe still where it was (journal "Brought back"). "The last minute" is the moment of death: alone, the dead hero's state stands still until the player chooses; at a shared table, while the party is still where they fell (`death.where` equals the map location; a death recorded before `where` existed cannot be revived at a table). Who: a following, conscious companion trained (`npcClasses`) as Cleric or Druid of level 5+ or Paladin, Ranger or Artificer of 9+ (companions are a level below the hero, so in practice a hero of 6+), paid from the hero's pack (`revivalOptions`, `reviveHero`, `{type:'revive',by:<npc id>}`, asked from the epitaph: "Tobin knows Revivify and can bring you back, for a diamond worth 300 gp." [Ask Tobin] [Let Kara go]; also the DM choice `revive:<id>`, never a chip, since the dead send no turns; it works in the wilds too); or at a table another hero who knows Revivify, with a slot of 3rd level or higher and 300 gp, out of a fight (`partyRevivalError`, `reviveFriend`, `{type:'revive',target:'party:<seat>'}`: the "Revivify <Name>" chip, the DM choice `revive:party:<seat>` and typed "I cast Revivify on Kara" (dmCommands.js, which gives the reason when it cannot) are the same move, which the campaign server accepts like any chip; the slot and gold are the caster's). Buyable revives are not built: `boughtRevivals(game,hero)` returns [] and is the hook (return `{kind:'item',id,name,cost:0,ready:true}` and spend it in `reviveHero`; `revivalOptions`, `deathIsFinal` and the epitaph already honour it). When nobody can (`deathIsFinal`: dead, not at a table, no ready option) or the player lets them go, App.js lays the hero to rest at once (`layHeroToRest`, from `heroSaveActions` in useHeroSaves.js): a memorial line in the graveyard (`layToRest`), out of the roster, and `questbound.character.v1`/`questbound.adventure.v1` removed from this profile (`forgetCharacter`, `forgetAdventure`, queued after any save on its way); nothing saves them again (`restingRef`). The epitaph ("… is gone. Their adventure ends here; they are remembered among the fallen under Heroes." and Begin a new hero) stays until the player leaves the play screen; then the hero leaves memory, the title has no Continue, New Adventure offers a fresh start and The fallen, and a ready-made hero is one tap. Old saves holding a dead hero are laid to rest when first loaded. Not removed: the pre-table local backup, a save code exported earlier and (once accounts are on) the last uploaded copy, until a new hero saves. Never at a shared table (`!game.party` in `deathIsFinal`, `!table.joined` in App): the table keeps the seat as fallen and nothing is deleted on the server; the player brings another hero to their seat from the table screen (Bring a new hero: their roster, a ready-made hero or a new one; `campaigns.join` with the campaign id → `joinParty` replaces a dead member's hero at the same seat; the epitaph says so and its button opens the table screen), or leaves the table (then the device lays the hero to rest). A shared-hero table keeps its dead hero until the device leaves. Test the screens after death with real input at desktop and phone widths: the death card ("You have died") takes the first tap, M/Esc open the menu above it, Main menu, Begin a new hero, New Adventure and a ready-made hero must all work, and a reload during the epitaph must leave no hero.
- Survival odds, hidden from players (2026-10-11, the host's words: "make sure it's a bit easier for the player to survive most situations. there should definitely be a challenge and the opportunity of death but we don't want the game to be overly difficult. give the player a slight luck boost when close to death, but don't hold their hand too much, and don't mention this luck skill to the player."; `deathRules.js` `survivalOdds`/`sparedBlow`, `storyRules.js` `foeEase`/`easedBonus`, `verify-death.cjs`, `balance-check.cjs`): up to 2nd level foes deal 1 less damage, never below +0 (`foeEase={upToLevel:2,damage:1}`; `easedBonus` in `storyFoeStats` and in `foeStatsFor` (`hostileEncounter.js`) for the creatures, a pack's members and a creature at the foe's side included), worked into the stat block when a fight or tale begins, so the feed's dice and totals are the stat block's (a bandit at level 1 shows "1d6 [4] + 0"; tales already under way keep their foe). Close to death, a blow that would drop the hero (damage at least current plus temporary HP) is rolled again half the time and the lower kept (`sparedBlow`: the creature, the creature at its side, a person in a brawl, a foe striking another hero at the table in `foeHitsPartyHero`), and a failed death save (a natural 1 too) is rolled again one time in four and the better kept (`survivalOdds={blow:0.5,save:0.25}`). Both draw from the engine's own `random()`, so the campaign server and devices agree and the same seed gives the same turn. This luck is a secret from players: only the roll that counts is ever written, and nothing a player or the Dungeon Master reads (feed, log, journal, DM context and instructions, dm-rules, descriptions, the primer and loading texts, the chronicle, legal texts, feedback notes) may name it or say luck, fortune or reroll; verify-death checks that the dice shown add up and that those files never mention it. Never describe it in any player-facing text, and never tell players about it. Measured with `node balance-check.cjs` on 2026-10-11 (solo, deaths per fight): against creatures 13% → 7% at level 1 and 8% → 4% at level 2, the tale's foe 7% → 2% at level 1; level-1 falls 31% → 23%; overall wins 82% → 85%. Considered and not taken: a second starting draught, a foe's attack −1. Tune only `survivalOdds` and `foeEase` (both provisional), together.
- Relationships (`relationshipRules.js`, `npcRules.js` forgivenessOf, `verify-relationships.cjs`, `verify-social.cjs`): named characters have an attitude (hostile → devoted), memories in their own words, and two deeper marks: a grudge (the player killed or gravely harmed someone who matters to them; hostile while it lasts, refuses help and travel, leaves the party) and a bond (the player saved their life or someone who matters to them; never below friendly). A grudge outweighs a bond. A grudge can be forgiven, never cheaply: it has a weight (`grudgeWeight`: blood when their family, beloved or own kin was killed, else grave), `grudgeSince` (the story log id when sworn) and `amends` (0–6). `forgivenessOf(game,id)` says what forgiving needs: grave 2 amends and 1 day, blood 3 and 2 days, one fewer with a bond, one more for a grudge sworn again after an earlier forgiveness (`forgave` record: {grudge, weight, memory}). Days are long rests, chapters/tales and journeys to a new land in `game.storyLog` since the grudge. A log that no longer reaches back to it counts as long ago, and old saves weigh a grudge by its words. The DM records `amends` and `forgave` in `relationships`, held to that count by `deedAllowed`/validDeeds and, server side, `relationshipAllowed` in dm-replies.cjs (forgave only when canForgive, or this deed is the last amends with the days passed; or for someone cold without a grudge who lets a lesser wrong go). Saving a grudge-holder's life adds 2 amends and forgives a grave grudge at once ("… forgives you: you saved a life."). A forgiven person is unfriendly (blood) or indifferent (grave), labelled "Forgave you · trust is thin", logs "<name> forgave you." and earns the Mended deed. npcMemory is shared world state at a party table: anyone's amends count. Witnesses remember a victim by the story's name (`npcLore`). Stories give each NPC `ties` (to the other NPC: family … enemy; to the foe: kin … enemy), which `recordConsequences` (run after every engine step) uses for killings, knockouts, lives saved (healing someone at 0 HP; NPC healing heals the NPC, not the caster) and the foe's end. The DM records lesser deeds in its `relationships` reply field (pleased, grateful, offended, angered, bond, grudge, amends, forgave, ally, parts), only on ordinary turns or when narrating a resolved one. The People panel (Quest tab / side column) shows each person's standing.
- Fights in the wilds (`encounterRules.js`, `verify-encounters.cjs`): a found place has a danger (safe, risky, lair), an optional feature and an optional `threat` (a creature built from a `hostileFoes` template under its own name). Arriving at a lair, or at a risky place for the first time (40%; below level 3 never a pack or the orc), or a DM `ambush` reply out in the wilds (below level 3 an orc or a pack comes as a lighter creature of its kind, `errandSingles`) starts `game.wildFight`, which swaps in the creature while the story foe's HP and fate wait in it; winning, fleeing (back the way you came, the creature keeps its wounds) or falling ends it. Every template has a signature move (`signatureMoves`); a creature named with its own "The" keeps it only in its opening line (`bareFoeName`: "The Tallow Walkers burst out", then "the Tallow Walkers"), and the name as given is kept beside the bare one (`wildFight.called`, `calledFoe`), so a creature fled from is remembered by it, keeps its picture key (worldArtRules.js, `wild-the-…`) and is "Defeated the X" in the journal; the opening's subject (`foeOpening`): a name ending on a kind of creature takes "A" or "An" ("A Dire Wolf", "An Ashen Widow"), any other is the creature's own name ("Grimjaw bursts out", "Old Tusk"), and a plural name is many ("burst … They haven't"); companions who follow you (and allies who live there) attack after your turn, can be targeted, and one beside a dying hero tries a Medicine check to carry them home. Dead out there, a companion who knows Revivify brings them back where they fell (`verify-encounters.cjs`). Never use `hostileFoes` at module load in `encounterRules.js` (checks concatenate files in any order). Mixed fights (`verify-allies.cjs`): a lair threat or ambush may carry `ally` (one creature of a different, non-pack template), kept as `game.foeAllies` (60% HP) during the wild fight; it attacks after the leader every round, is aimed at with `{type:'encounter-attack',target:'ally:0'}`, `request.aim` for spells, or the one-action `game.aim`, flees when the leader falls, and returns with a lair owner after the hero flees (`threat.ally`).
- A foe against a bigger side (`foeScaling`, `scaledFoe`, `foeOffense`, `sideStrength`/`brawlStrength`, `foeScaleOf`/`validFoeScale` in encounterRules.js, `withFoeScale` in adventureRules.js, `verify-balance.cjs`): creatures, the tale's foe and the vault's guardians are written for one hero. When more fight on the hero's side (other standing heroes at the table 1.15 each, companions who follow and fight their class's share in `foeScaling.companion` (0.5 to 0.75, less well below the party's level), one without a class 0.25, allies who live where the fight is), the foe is scaled by the side's strength f: f times the hit points and about f^0.9 times the blows (more attacks a turn, each a little harder or softer); a pack gains members instead. A hero fighting alone meets what they always did, but for the softer blows of `foeScaling.alone` by class (see Written tales (fight shape)). A foe that strikes twice a turn of its own (a written tale's foe against a Fighter) keeps its blows and gains more. The strength is kept for the fight (`game.foeScale` {f, key: wild, story or vault:N}): it grows when someone joins mid-fight and never shrinks as the side falls, so a foe's hit points can rise when a party fight starts; a creature left behind keeps its wounds as a share and is scaled afresh. Brawls with people scale the same way by the hero's allies. Tuned with `balance-check.cjs --companion/--party`; the numbers are provisional.
- Open world (`mapRules.js`, `verify-world.cjs`): a written story starts with three linked places (and its landmarks); the DM's `discovery` reply (name, description, kind, bearing, miles, travel, region) adds a place linked to where it was found (`game.world`, up to 150 places, stage `wild` while there; see Beyond the map for what gives way) and travel follows the links (`travelRoute`). `storyText` keeps written place and character names intact. Companions follow.
- People anywhere (`npcRules.js` + `relationshipRules.js`, `verify-people.cjs`, `verify-residents.cjs`): besides the story's two residents (`keeper`, `mara`), a written tale seats its residents (the writer's `residents`, six to ten: two at the start, two or three at the place to investigate and at peopled landmarks, one at a lonely one, none at a wild one; at most `maxResidents` 12) into `game.people` with `seatResidents` (`resident:true`, `motive`; home from `at`: inn/bridge/tower, a starting place's name or a landmark's exact name; never more than `residentsPerPlace` 3 a place and `residentsAtStart` 2 at the starting place; ties by name or id; anything malformed, a known name or the foe's name is left out), and the DM's `introduce` reply adds more (`game.people.nN`, n1–n96, `maxPeople` 96, at most `maxPeopleHere` 4 living per place) where the turn leaves the player, with toughness (frail/common/tough HP and AC, or a class: see Companions and allies), an opening attitude, a foe tie and at most one tie to someone known (`tieBetween` reads it both ways; someone tied to a person the player killed starts with a grudge). The id pattern `n(?:[1-9]|[1-8]\d|9[0-6])` is repeated in npcRules `isPersonId`, adventureStorage `someone` and the concentration target, masteryRules `validMarks` (and its boon keys), storyRules `validStory` and dm-replies `personId`: change them together. A resident is met (`metPerson`) once the hero has been where they live (or they are here or follow); until then the People panel hides them, the DM sees `metHero:false` in brief, and the Log says "Met X, role." on first arrival; someone who lives at a place greets a first arrival (`sceneTriggers.js`). Introducing a known name moves that person instead of adding one. Lines with speakerId `new` in the same reply are theirs (`commitDmTurn` rewrites them and returns `result.conversation`, which is passed to `recordedTurn`). Never hard-code `['keeper','mara']`: use `npcIdsOf`, `npcProfile` (combat stats), `npcLore` (name, role, look), `npcMaxHP(id, game)`. Fights with people (`npcCombat`) happen at inn, bridge, tower and found places; fleeing goes back the way you came. Up to three companions follow at once (`maxFollowing`); see Companions and allies.
- Who is here (2026-10-11, the host's words: "have npcs appear as they are discovered as well as the ones that are immediately noticeable at any given location, not all locations need multiple people, but many should"; `relationshipRules.js` `introducePerson`/`canMeetPeople`/`roomForSomeoneNew`, `verify-who-is-here.cjs`): a resident the writer marks `noticed:false` (one or two a tale, never at the start) is `hidden`: not present, not met, not in the People panel or greetings, sent to the DM as `unnoticed` with where they live; when the DM introduces their exact name (the player searched or asked), `introducePerson` finds them (no longer hidden, met, present, "Met …" in the Log and journal). `canMeetPeople` is true while someone hides here even at a full place, and `roomForSomeoneNew` (96 people in all, four living at a place; at 96 nobody new is met and the DM sees `canIntroduce` false) still keeps genuinely new people out. WHO IS HERE in PEOPLE ANYWHERE: noticeable people are there at once (two to four at a town, inn, market, camp, temple, quay, ferry or crossing; one or two at a farm or lonely shrine; often nobody in the wild), one introduced per reply when there are fewer, others found by exploring, searching or asking, unnoticed residents only when found; dm-intent's "nobody here" look-again backs it (see Holding the Dungeon Master to what the player asked). So that the largest world fits one 64 kB request (`placesInFull` 10, `placesByName` 24, `landsInFull` 10, `peopleInBrief` 6, `peopleByName` 20 in dmSceneRules.js): the nearest places of the land the hero is in, the errand's place and any the player named go in full in `world.knownPlaces`, journeys on offer to other lands as brief entries {id, name, distance, minutes, visited, land}, the rest of this land by name and distance in `world.morePlaces` ("Name (3.1 miles NE, visited)", then `morePlacesNotListed`), other lands in `world.farLands` ({name, road: "312 miles, 4.2 days on the road to X", places by name, "(heard of)" marked, for the ten nearest}; lands with no places left out, `regions` still names every land); people not here: companions, the unnoticed, grudges and debts, then those met, six of this land in brief (no memories until met; grudge and bond cut to 120), the rest of this land met by name in `peopleElsewhere` ("Name, role, at Place (attitude)"), people of other lands not at all (a companion waiting there in brief, with `land`, which sceneFor keeps); the conversation and journal as `sceneFor` trims them; no `travelRoutes` in a story. verify-dm-trim measures 60 lands, 150 places and 96 people at their longest, with six 1000-character exchanges and a 1000-character input, under 60 kB (57,475 bytes on 2026-10-12, 57,962 on 2026-10-16 with all 96 people from the people library and their hooks; that save is about 270 kB, within the campaign's 2 MB) and the context's speed (under 1.5 s).
- Companions and allies (`followerRules.js`, `npcRules.js` npcClasses, `encounterRules.js` companionAttack, `Followers.js`, `verify-companion-classes.cjs`):
  - A companion travels with the hero (`followers[id].status` following) or waits where they were told (waiting). They follow everywhere (travel, discoveries, regions, wild fights, the vault, the next tale, a shared table) until the player orders otherwise.
  - An ally (`npcMemory[id].allegiance` 'player' with no following/waiting entry; `isAlly`) stands with the hero where they live (`npcWhereabouts`): they fight in a wild or bridge fight at that place (`fightingCompanions`) and in brawls there, and never travel.
  - Everyone who joins gets `game.npcClasses[id] = {class, level}`: the Dungeon Master's `class`, else the writer's, else `classFromRole`, at `companionLevelFor` (max(1, hero level - 1)). Trained people from the story writer or `introduce` (`class`) get one at introduction.
  - `npcProfile` takes HP and AC from `classedStats` and returns `trainedAs`. Companions attack by class (`companionAttack`: Extra Attack, Sneak Attack, cantrips; Sacred Flame and Vicious Mockery as saves) and keep the old +2 blow until classed. `withCompanionClasses` (out of fights: an Adventure.js effect solo, the campaign server at a table) classes old saves. `levelCompanions` runs with every level taken.
  - npcClasses and `joinOffers` are shared world fields, never hero fields.
  - Recruitment replies may be `offer` (anyone present, not invited) as well as join/decline/check, and carry `class`. An offer becomes a `join:<id>` choice and a "Take X along" chip (`acceptOffer`; at a table the `accept-offer` campaign edit), or is taken by a typed yes that names them.
  - Orders: typed through `followerCommand` in dmCommands.js ("Mara, wait here", "Everyone wait here", "Mara, follow me", "Send word to Mara", "we part ways"), or as DM choices `follower-wait/resume/call:<id>` (never chips), or as Companions-screen buttons (at a table the `follower` campaign edit). All become `{type:'follower', id|ids, order}`. 'call' fetches a waiting companion along a known road.
  - Parting ways with a friendly or devoted companion leaves an ally. The DM's relationships `ally`/`parts` make or end an ally. Rests revive a downed companion (short: half HP; long: full).
  - Only trained people show Class and Level: `encounterEntities` gives everyone else a role, and creatures `kind:'Creature'`; never write "Commoner".
  - The DM gets COMPANIONS AND ALLIES and CLASSES, `nearbyNPCs[].trainedAs/standing`, `npcSocialState[].standing/trainedAs`, and `context.joinOffers`. dm-intent's 'unrecorded join' second look asks again when the telling has someone join that recruitment did not record.
  - `node balance-check.cjs --companion Fighter` simulates fights with a companion.
- Next tales (called "the next tale" on screen, since a tale now has chapters of its own) (`sequelRules.js`, `verify-sequel.cjs`): with a living hero and a story, New Adventure offers "Continue in this region" (default) or "Somewhere new" (`AdventureIntros.js`). Continuing asks for a tale beside the land where the hero stands (since 2026-10-23: see The campaign's screens, below), then `joinRegion` adds the old story's places and keeps the new tale's residents at their own ids (n1…), numbering the earlier people after them (up to `maxPeople` in all), as world places in a region of their own (old start first, shifted a few miles along a random bearing and joined to the new `inn`, following the old story's own paths; every place of the earlier country the hero went to, or where a companion waits, comes with the places on the roads to them, then up to 20 (`oldPlaceLimit`) places only heard of, after the new story's landmarks, within `maxWorldPlaces` 150; each earlier land comes as a land of its own while there is room (sixty in all; past that a visited place joins the earlier country's first land and a rumour in a land without room is left behind); clashing names become "Old X", then "X (earlier)"; the earlier country's regions are numbered after any far lands the new story has of its own; the new residents' first impressions are kept beside old memories; the story log carries on) and turns the old residents and people met into `people` (ids renumbered, ties remapped, memories, fates, HP and companions kept; foe ties reset to neutral). Somewhere new joins the earlier world too, days down the road (since 2026-10-23). Every new story keeps the pack, draughts, training, experience and deeds (`carriedBase`). Level-ups must carry `game.people` too. At a shared table the host begins a new tale for everyone (`campaign-tale`); it does not carry on in the region yet. Every land of a campaign remembered (2026-10-23, the host's words: "all areas traveled in a single campaign should be remembered so they can be returned to. and NPCs should all have memory of seeing the player before if they have"; `nextTalePlacement`/`sequelMiles` in sequelRules.js, `sequelPlacement(random,{far})` in startTale.js, `verify-sequel.cjs`, `verify-campaign-world.cjs`): `joinRegion(next, game, {sameRegion, bearing, miles, anchor})` joins the earlier world at `anchor`, the place where the hero stands (a starting place id, a p-id, 'wild' for the place they are at, 'dungeon' as the bridge; default the earlier start 'inn'), in whatever land it lies: the anchor is put `miles` off along `bearing` with a road from the new start to it, and the earlier world's roads are followed out from the anchor (each place's `from` re-rooted on the road back, the earlier map's order kept where it can be), so every place the hero went to can be walked back to from the new start (within the 150-place and 60-land caps, which keep their old rules). Near is 4 to 7 miles (the next tale here, a tale in a land the hero reached), far 60 to 200 (somewhere new, the shelf): `sequelPlacement(random,{far})`; the story log says "a new tale began, days down the road" past 20 miles. People of the earlier world keep `resident` and (while still out of sight) `hidden`, so a resident the hero never reached stays unmet and everyone met stays met with their memories (greeted 'again'); someone met where they stood rather than at their home is kept as someone known (`resident` dropped). Place and people names are kept apart with the staging's name key (`stagingNameKey`: case, spacing, curly quotes, a leading "The"), so "The Mill" and "Mill" never both show and a written tale's staged names resolve to its own (an earlier "The Mill" becomes "The Old Mill"). The story writer never knows whom the hero met, so a next tale may cast a library person the hero already knows (the same `pool`): the new tale's one keeps the library's identity (its staging names them), and the earlier one keeps the hero's history (memories, ties, fate) but drops `pool` and `hook` and is told apart as "<name> the Elder", so no library person is in a game twice; every other kept library person still counts in `poolTaken` (`libraryPeopleMet`). `sameRegion:false` still keeps only companions, for older callers and the Skirmish (a practice fight outside the campaign: nothing is joined to it or from it); App.js `playCharacter` joins every other next tale of a living hero with sameRegion true (see The campaign's screens). verify-campaign-world.cjs begins each tale on the host's shelf (read from .questbound-written, skipped when absent) as a next tale beside a world of thirty lands and a hundred places: valid, every staged name the tale's own, every place visited reachable, met people met, and the golden path (written-walk.cjs, `prepare`) still reaching its ending. Since 2026-10-23 the Dungeon Master answers a next tale from the tales already written when one of the land's terrain fits (the device sends `context.land` {name, terrain} and `context.playedTales`), else writes one the ahead way (`continuing`'s places and people never reach the writer: only the land's name and terrain) and adds it to the compendium (see The compendium under Ready journeys); the old region still comes in through joinRegion on the device. The campaign's screens (2026-10-23, stream C of the campaign; `playCharacter(begin,{here})` in App.js, `taleRequest` in startTale.js, `landHere`/`landTaleOffer`/`taleWaitsKey` in sequelRules.js, `playedTales`/`dismissedLandTales`/`dismissLandTale` in journeyLibrary.js, `taleEndCard`/`landTaleCard` in shelfRules.js, `TaleFinished.js`, `writeStory`'s `onWriting` in dmConnection.js; `verify-tales-ahead.cjs`, `verify-written-tales.cjs`, `verify-presentation.cjs`): every next tale of a living hero with a story joins the old world, anchored at `mapLocation(game)`: near (4 to 7 miles) for Continue in this region and a tale begun from a card on the play screen, far (60 to 200) for Somewhere new, a written tale, a ready journey and the hero's own idea. A tale beside where the hero stands sends `context.land` (`landHere`: the land the hero is in, its name and terrain; the home land is `homeRegion`) and `context.playedTales` (`playedTales()`: the journeys played and the written tales recorded on this device, plus the tale under way, at most 200), never the region summary, the story so far or the hero (the server would drop them: every tale is written for any hero), and the optional wish when one was typed (a wish always writes). The Dungeon Master answers a tale already written at once (`matched`) or writes one; `writeStory`'s `onWriting` says a tale is being written, and only then (or when nothing has come back in three seconds) does the story cover show (`taleWriting`; `StoryLoading` `near` is the place the hero stands); a tale that starts at once shows the scene's own short pause. Any tale begun is remembered as played (`rememberJourney(story.libraryId)`). A tale finished (written or the story writer's, never the Skirmish) gets the What next card (TaleFinished, kind 'finished': `shelfFinishCard` for a written tale, `taleEndCard` for the story writer's) once its last scene has played: Next tale here (begun at once, near, no New Adventure screen between: `playCharacter(true,{here:true})`), Written tales (when the shelf has any; opens the suggested length's tab) and Somewhere new (the New Adventure screen, continuing off); at a shared table only the host gets Choose the next tale (the New Adventure screen in table mode), the others are told the host chooses. "A tale waits in <Land>" (TaleFinished, kind 'waits', `landTaleOffer`): while the hero stands in a land whose "Set out for a new land" line is in the story log since the tale began (a land beyond the map, or a far land the Dungeon Master opened), never the home land, a land the tale spoke of from the start (`story.lands`) or a land of an earlier tale; out of a fight, alive, no turn order or spell waiting, solo only (at a table the host chooses the tales; the Skirmish never); shown 1.5 s after the feed has played, not while a What next card, the session rating or the touch keyboard is up. Begin starts a tale for that land beside where the hero stands (near, anchored there; the tale under way is left part-way, and a written one's shelf card then says "Left at chapter n of m"); Not now (and the card's ✕) is remembered on this device for that land of that tale (`questbound.landtales.v1` {not}, the last 100, guarded). A start begun from a card shows its error in the card. The cards are 460 px at most, under the HUD on a layer that lets taps through, their buttons as wide as their words and wrapping to a new row (measured at 320×568, 375×812 and 1400×860: inside the window, nothing sideways or cut short; screenshots in qb-work/notes/campaign/shots). memoryRules.js reads a tale begun "days down the road" as a tale start too.
- Roster and graveyard (`rosterRules.js`, `HeroRoster.js` on the Heroes screen): replacing the current hero (ready-made or newly created, not an edit) sets them aside in `questbound.roster.v1` (under the account profile) with their adventure (max 8, `maxRoster`; refuse rather than drop). The graveyard (`questbound.graveyard.v1`, at most `maxGraves` 40, listed under The fallen) holds memorial lines only (the host confirmed it on 2026-10-12: "let dead heroes leave a short memorial."): `{name, level, species, class, cause, place, story, at}`, no sheet and no save, never playable (graves from before kept `character`; it is dropped when read and from storage the next time the lists are written); one grave per fall (`layToRest`; see Death, revival and laying to rest). A ready-made hero who died can be picked again as a fresh level-1 copy. Playing a roster hero sets the current one aside first and removes the chosen entry last. Windows file names are case-insensitive: never give two modules names that differ only by case (`heroRoster.js` vs `HeroRoster.js`).
- Gold and the pack (`inventoryRules.js`, `verify-inventory.cjs`): `game.pack` = gold (starts at the kit's gold), found items (weapon, armor, potion for the stronger draughts, ammunition, gear, treasure, quest; at most 4 per reply) and `spent.Arrow`; `game.potions` (0..20) are healing draughts. The DM's `loot` reply (gold ± and items ±, with a reason) is applied by `commitDmTurn` through `applyLoot`, which refuses spending gold the hero lacks and giving away what they do not carry (dm-server cuts windfalls over 40 × level + 60 to that before they arrive), turns known weapons, arrows and healing potions into the right kinds (Greater, Superior and Supreme keep their strength), carries an unknown weapon as gear rather than failing the turn, and builds magic items from the catalogue (lootRules.js). `gearedHero` adds found weapons and the arrows left to the hero for every engine step, so a found sword can be swung at once and an empty quiver takes the bow away. `potion` works out of combat; `give-potion` heals or revives a companion (a revived one is bonded). Loot is allowed on ordinary turns and when narrating a resolved one, never mid-fight; the Pack panel shows gold, draughts and items.
- Spoils and magic items (`lootRules.js`, `verify-loot.cjs`): every engine step goes through `withSpoils` (in `adventureStep`), which notes in `game.spoils` (shared world, at most 6) what fell where: a wild or lair creature (lair and feature kept); the creature at its side, only if slain; a Skirmish creature, or the tale's main foe (kind boss); a person newly in `npcFate` who was not following. `{type:'loot'}` (`takeSpoils`, in `heroStep`; out of fights only, never with a pendingSpell or a turnOrder) rolls each entry once with `random()` by template, toughness or role and the looter's tier (`lootTier`: 1–4 for levels 1–4, 5–10, 11–16, 17–20; `creatureSpoils`, `personSpoils`, `bossSpoils`, `lairHoard`): beasts give hides and fangs, no coin; a lair gives a hoard; the tale's foe always gives one magic item that suits the looter (`bossItem`; the story writer's `story.hoard` when its rarity fits the tier). It writes "You search …", "Found: …" and "Among the hoard: …" lines (shown as feed cards), puts the finds into the looter's own pack, and is offered as the Loot chip, the `loot` choice, typed "search the body / loot the lair" (`lootWords`) and a dm-intent plain choice. `magicItems` is the catalogue of SRD 5.2 items whose effects the rules apply: weapon, armor and shield +1 to +3 (pack items carry `base` and `magic:{id,plus}`, named e.g. 'Longsword +1'); Cloak and Ring of Protection, Stone of Good Luck, Bracers of Defense, Gauntlets of Ogre Power, Headband of Intellect, Boots and Cloak of Elvenkind, Eyes of the Eagle, Wand of the War Mage; Wand of Magic Missiles (`item:wand-of-magic-missiles` or `{type:'use-item',target:'ally:N'}`, 7 charges, 1d6+1 back on a long rest); Periapt of Wound Closure; Greater, Superior and Supreme draughts (pack items of kind potion, `drink:<id>`, a bonus action in a fight). `gearedHero` sets `equipment.magic` (`magicEffects`; attuned items count, at most 3) and raises scores to an item's floor. `combatBasics`, `weaponAttacks`, `rollDamage`, saves (`magicSaveBonus`), `skillCheckBonus`, spell attacks and death saves read it. Armor class is always computed from the geared hero, including the party's `members[id].stats` (`statsOf(character, state)`). The Dungeon Master gets `context.spoils` and each found item's magic effect. Its `loot` items carry `magic` (an id or null), allowed only on a chapter-complete or lead-seen-through narration, once, within `rarityFor(level)`; gold over the cap is cut to the cap (`clampedLoot`, noted in the diagnostics), and no loot is accepted on a turn the engine searched or a fight was won. `dm-schemas.cjs` `magicRarity` and `adventure-generator.cjs` `hoardItems` copy the catalogue's ids; verify-loot checks that they agree. Change all three together.
- Ready-made heroes live in `pregens.js` (`verify-pregens.cjs` runs each through the builder checks and a first turn). The Cast… picker is `spellActions` and `castMenu` in `quickActions.js` (see The Cast… picker and its slot organizer).
- Settings → Move your hero exports/imports save codes (`saveCodes.js`, checked with the same validators as saves; `verify-save-codes.cjs`).
- Saves and their formats (`saveVersion.js`, `saveMigrations.js`, `SAVES.md`, `verify-saves.cjs`, `save-fixtures/`, `make-save-fixtures.cjs`; the host on 2026-10-13: "use your best judgement to see how far back our saved data should go and go with that option"; the session lead decided): keep loading every save format any tester-reachable build ever wrote. The first playtest build (235726f, 30 September 2026) already went out on a share link, so that is the whole history: drop nothing. From 13 October 2026 every new hero (`makeCharacter`) and adventure snapshot (`adventureSnapshot`; `partyView` keeps it) carries `saveVersion` (`currentSaveVersion`, now 1; none is format 0); the validators accept none, any number 1 to 99 (a rollback), and every old shape. An older save is upgraded once, on load, in one place (`upgradeCharacter`, `upgradeAdventure`): `loadCharacter`/`loadAdventure` (written back; `sameHeroText` finds the adventure when a load was cut short between the two writes), `restoreEntry` (the roster), and the campaign server when it reads a table or a hero is brought to one (`campaigns.cjs` `upgraded`); a save code, a restored copy or an adopted table is upgraded at the next load. An upgraded copy that fails the save checks is never used. Format 0 → 1 renames a subclass planned under an old name (the renames of that day, `formatOneRenames`; the Artificer's of 2026-10-22 are read where used, never written), turns a solo hero's levels owed and an untaken old victory into experience points, classes companions from before classes (out of fights), folds the story log into the memory and weighs old grudges by their words; an old 'Elf'/'Dark Elf' stays as written (portraits); party heroes keep old experience fields until their next award. A save on the crossroads tale of the first builds (a game with no story that is not the Skirmish) loads and validates as written and is never played: it reads as "no tale yet" (No crossroads) until its player starts a new tale, which carries the hero's pack, experience and deeds (`carriedBase`). A format may be retired no earlier than six months after the last build that wrote it, and never before the full launch (format 0: 13 April 2027 at the earliest); until then every lazy reader stays. A new format: raise `currentSaveVersion`, add an exact step to `saveMigrations.js`, keep the validators accepting older shapes, add the build to `make-save-fixtures.cjs` (run by hand: it takes each listed commit's own files out of git and makes heroes, adventures, a roster entry, a save code and a shared table with that build's own rules) and keep `verify-saves.cjs` passing (167 saves from 15 builds load as written, upgrade through the device's load and the campaign server, mean the same game and play a turn either way).
- Server turns and campaign edits (`turnRules.js`, `rules-engine.cjs`, `server-turn.cjs`, `campaigns.cjs`, `campaign-live.cjs`, `campaign-advancement.cjs`, `useSharedTable.js`, `verify-rules-engine.cjs`, `verify-server-turn.cjs`, `verify-campaign-edits.cjs`, `verify-sync.cjs`, `verify-table-client.cjs`, `verify-table-live.cjs`):
  - One pipeline. `settleTurn` settles a turn the same way on a device (`Adventure.act`, solo) and on the campaign server: the rules on `ruled` (the hero with the device's settings, `rulesHero`), experience for every living hero, the turn recorded, the scene cue (devices only), the story log, then deeds. At a table `Adventure.act` refuses ("Send this action to the campaign server.") and `ask` in useDmTurn.js calls `table.turn` instead.
  - The rules engine. `rules-engine.cjs` loads the rules files in a vm in the order of `rulesFiles` in `rules-files.cjs` (`rulesSource()`, with `spellCatalog.json`/`spellProgression.json` prepended; 3 s timeout; cached per process). Every rules file a listed file imports must be listed (`playerStorage.js` is stubbed). Imports must be one line ending in ';'. Every `r.*` the server calls must be exported (`partyVictory` must not be). A missing name makes `validSnapshot` quietly refuse every campaign ("Start with your own valid solo adventure"); verify-rules-engine.cjs catches it.
  - What a table turn sends. `table.turn(question,{npcId,direct,action,oneOnOne,storyPreferences,autoShield})` (plus `suggest:false` while that device keeps Suggestions closed: see Round 3) posts `campaign-turn` with `baseVersion`, a `requestId` that stays the same for retries of the same payload, and `expectedAccountId`. `storyPreferences` keeps only brutality and narrator; `autoShield` reaches the rules and is never saved. A chip action must equal one the server offers (`dmChoices`, `quickActions`, `attackActions` for every `attackTargets` target, `spellActions` and each chip's `casts`, `mapMoves`); a body carrying `snapshot`, `conversation`, `ruling` or `random` is refused. The server rolls with `crypto.randomInt`, asks the DM with the account's guest label, keeps the last 40 receipts (a repeated request is replayed, never applied twice), validates the result (`validSnapshot`, `validAdventure`) and saves it atomically with an encrypted backup.
  - What a table turn returns: the member's own view, narration, the turn, suggestions (three at most), fault, deeds and xp, none of the last four stored. A plain "I cast" the rules refuse is shown as it is; only a cast the game could not read goes to the DM as castingIssue. The browser adopts the snapshot through `applyRemote` (guarded by epoch and generation; after the first state of a session, `{quick:true}`: applied at once, its new pictures fetched meanwhile, where joining still waits up to 5 s for the scene's pictures) and records a lost conflict with `noteProblem({kind:'table'})`.
  - The live table (2026-10-21, the host's words: "the reaction time definitely needs some improvement. players should be able to see that another player is typing as soon as they start typing and actions should be sent to the shared table instantly."; `campaign-live.cjs`, `verify-table-live.cjs`): in place of the 2.5 s poll the browser holds a wait, route `campaign-wait` {knownVersion, knownPresence}, which the account service answers the moment the campaign's version changes (a turn kept, an edit, a ban, a member joining) or its presence does (who is here, typing or acting: `presence`, a 16-hex key in every state), else after 25 s as it is, with the same member view as `poll` (`partyView`, own hero in the lead; a snapshot only when the version is new); the device asks again at once (after 250 ms when an answer came at once). Presence-only answers to one member are at least 600 ms apart (`presenceGap`); a new version and a turn begun or ended are answered at once. At most two waits are open per member (a third answers the oldest) and 500 in all (past that 503 and the device retries in a few seconds); a wait ends when its caller goes away (`res` close, passed upstream by both proxies), and a removed or banned member's wait ends with the calm `removed` answer. A server without the route (404/405, an older build) makes the device poll every 2.5 s as before. Typing (`campaign-typing` {typing:true|false}, never words): `table.typed(text)` from the message box (DungeonMaster.js) sends true on the first keystroke and again every 4 s while typing, false on an empty box, after 6 s without a keystroke and on leaving; sending a turn needs no ping, because the turn's lock clears it; the server lets it lapse 10 s after the last ping. Every member's `players[].typing` and `acting` come in the state; `table.typers` (others typing, not acting) shows above the message box as "<table name> is typing…" (one line, `data-qb=typing`, members muted on this device left out), and "<name> is taking a turn" (the existing hint and table bar) now appears the moment the turn reaches the server. Waits, typing, presence and the lock live only in the account service's memory (`createLive`: campaigns.cjs's `presence` and `locks` Maps tell the waits when someone arrives, leaves or begins a turn, and `persist` when a version is written), never in the snapshot, the campaign file or a backup. Measured on 2026-10-21 with two headless browsers (desktop server at 1400×860, share gateway in loopback mode at 375×812, stand-in DM, temp data; `C:/Users/itsbr/qb-work/tools/table-live-measure.cjs`): another player's typing shown about 0.1–0.3 s after the keystroke (never before); "is taking a turn" 0.1–0.2 s after the turn reaches the server (0.9 s average, up to 2.5 s, before); a kept turn on the other screen 0.08–0.25 s after it was kept (0.7–1 s average, up to 2.5 s, before). What remains is the Dungeon Master's reply (seconds) and the feed's own pacing (the player's line, then the telling 850 ms later).
  - The draft and single playback (2026-10-21, table-stream; the host: "the reaction time definitely needs some improvement"; `campaign-live.cjs` `setDraft`/`clearDraft`/`draft`/`draftKey`, `campaigns.cjs` `turn`/`wait`/`state`, `account-server.cjs` `askDungeonMaster`, `useSharedTable.js`, `useDmTurn.js`, `dmTurnRules.js` `arrivingTurn`, `TurnPlayback.js`, `verify-table-live.cjs` part 6): a table turn's request to the Dungeon Master is streamed (`campaigns.turn` passes `onNarration` to `ask`; the DM answers NDJSON as it does a solo device), and while the turn is resolved the narration written so far is the campaign's draft {seat, text} (only the lock holder's, at most 1800 characters), in the account service's memory only, never in the snapshot, the campaign file or a backup. Every state carries `draftKey` ('' when none) and, while there is one, `draft`; a device that sends `knownDraft` with its wait (every new build) is answered when the draft grows, draft-only answers to one member at least `draftGap` 400 ms apart and at most `draftBudget` 600 in ten minutes (past that the member just sees the kept turn); an older device (no `knownDraft`) is never woken for it. The draft is dropped the moment the turn ends (cleared before the kept state is written, and with the lock on any failure, refusal or conflict) and when a new turn begins, so the answer that brings the kept turn carries no draft and a failed turn leaves none. The device (`useSharedTable` `draft` {text, mine, name}) shows it in the same quill plate as a solo draft (`data-draft=true`, under "<name> is taking a turn" for the others, and on the acting player's own screen), drops it in the same render that adopts the kept turn, never takes one back from an answer older than its state, and clears its own after a failed turn. Nothing is applied from a draft: the reply is validated exactly as before, and a rejected, retried or rescued reply just restarts the draft. The single playback: another member's turn used to show whole and then play again from its first line, because `setAnimateId` ran in an effect a render after the new turn arrived; now `arrivingTurn` marks it during the render that first holds it (only when the table was already in step, so the first state after a reload or on joining is shown as it is), the narrator speaks it once, and TurnPlayback plays a turn once (`played`), never again when it comes back into view, with its dice and sounds live only that first time. Measured on 2026-10-21 (`C:/Users/itsbr/qb-work/tools/table-stream-measure.cjs`, the two-browser setup above with a stand-in DM over HTTP writing one word every 200 ms after 0.6 s, about 5.9 s a turn): first words on the other member's screen 0.74–0.99 s after the turn reached the server (about 0.09 s after the DM's first word), on the acting screen the same, against 5.8–6.2 s before (the whole reply, and then shown, hidden and played again); the kept turn plays once on both screens with the same pacing (player line to narration 0.88–0.90 s on both); a reload shows the last turn without playing it.
  - Campaign edits (`table.edit`, route `campaign-edit` with `baseVersion`; refused (409 `needsHero`) for a member with no hero of their own there, allowed before the table has a tale): note, wield (checked with `canTakeInHand`), training (inn only), follower (wait, resume, dismiss, call), accept-offer, attune, lead (the player's own note since 2026-10-22: toggles `noted`, no experience; see Making it move) and advance (a level by experience where the hero stands: `advancedInPlace`, `foeForLevel`, `levelCompanions`; no `levelsOwed`). Every state-changing screen action needs one of these at a table: the next state from the table replaces client-only writes.
  - Restarts. The engine is cached per process, so any rules-file change needs an account service restart (port 8086). The live table (campaign-live.cjs and its routes) needs the account service, the desktop server (pid 'desktop') and the share gateway restarted (the gateway with `node phone-server.cjs --public <same link>`, never the tunnel), and `dist-phone` rebuilt; until the account service is restarted the new game simply polls (404). The draft (table-stream) needs only the account service restarted (campaign-live.cjs, campaigns.cjs, account-server.cjs) and `dist-phone` rebuilt; the gateways pass `knownDraft` and the draft through unchanged, and the DM already streams. A new game with an older account service simply sees no draft; an older game with the new service is never woken for one.
- Scripts run by hand (nothing requires them; the checks never run them): `node balance-check.cjs` (fight simulations; `--tale <file>` a written tale's own fights, as check 8 plays them through `tale-balance.cjs`), `dm-report.cjs` (AI cost, rejections, reliability, spending), `feedback-report.cjs` (tester notes), `play-report.cjs` (play counts), `backup-tool.cjs` (verify, list, export encrypted backups), `accounts.cjs <command>` (reset-password, remove, ban, unban, bans), `make-emblem.cjs` and `make-icons.cjs` (the emblem layers and app icons, headless Chrome), `fetch-fonts.cjs` (typefaces), `make-save-fixtures.cjs` (a build's saves out of git, for verify-saves), `make-people-library.cjs` (`--plan`, then the people, then `--portraits`; `--batch 2` for the second batch), `import-painted-faces.cjs` (dry run, then `--write`: faces painted in play into the people library) and `check-written-tale.cjs` (the written-tale checker: see Written tales (format)), `make-creature-library.cjs` (`--list` first) (the host's libraries, through the host's own DM; host jobs the host funds, outside every ceiling: see The people library and The creature library), `check-dm-setup.cjs` (run by the launcher), the disposable QA harnesses `qa-cinematic-server.cjs`, `qa-companions-server.cjs`, `qa-playback-server.cjs`, `qa-table-server.cjs` (the notes files describe them) and `qa-security-server.cjs` (the account and desktop servers on temp data, for UI checks); and the paid-AI ones, only when the host asks: `dm-probe.cjs`, `dm-judgment.cjs`, `verify-new-story.cjs --live`. `test-fixtures.cjs`, `journey-fixtures.cjs` and `creature-fixtures.cjs` are modules the checks load. Unused code found on 2026-10-13 was removed (WeaponAttacks.js, preview-test.cjs, the test-live-*.cjs scripts of 30 September, and nine exports nothing called); verify-rules-engine.cjs keeps top-level names unique across the rules and helpers, and every .cjs passes `node --check`. Because the checks concatenate the rules into one context, a module that uses a name it never imports still passes them (and the web build): `verify-globals.cjs` (2026-10-22, after f9c0d4f called `calmFallback` in useDmTurn.js without importing it and broke every Dungeon Master turn on a device) parses every `.js` module with Babel and fails on a name that is not declared, imported, an ECMAScript built-in or one of its few platform globals (`platformGlobals`: window, document, timers, fetch, URL, Blob…, require, process, __DEV__; add one only for a real platform global; a JSX tag is never one), on an assignment to an undeclared name, and on a relative named or default import its module does not export; `node verify-globals.cjs <file>…` scans just those files (a copy elsewhere resolves its imports beside it, else in the checkout). About 3 s.
- The public GitHub repository must never include `project-memory/`, conversation backups, `assets/art/`, `.questbound-*` files, logs or builds (see `.gitignore`); check `git status` before every commit.
- Approved by the host on 2026-10-13: pushing to GitHub, deleting old work copies (worktrees), a small amount of paid AI use (spent by the main session through the live Dungeon Master after deployment, never by checks or helper agents), and merging the pull request. The place-art library's 171 paintings live in `.questbound-place-art/` (git-ignored) with a copy on the host's Desktop ("Questbound place art"), and the paintings are also backed up on the Desktop ("Questbound paintings backup"); the people library (`.questbound-people/`) and the creature library (`.questbound-creatures/`) have copies on the Desktop too ("Questbound people", "Questbound creatures"): never delete any of those copies. The host funds the jobs they ask for outside the testing ceilings (2026-10-16, the host's words: "don't include the functions that I ask you to do in the limitation of the api usage for the month I'll personally fund all of that outside of the testing"; see Spending ceilings): a library or other job the host asks for is not held to the game's daily or monthly ceilings, though the OpenAI organisation's own limit still applies.
- One list of rules files (`rules-files.cjs`, `verify-rules-engine.cjs`; 2026-10-13, after a check broke tonight because its own copy of the list lacked memoryRules.js): `rulesFiles` is the ordered list of rules files that the campaign server's engine, every check, dm-probe, dm-judgment and balance-check concatenate, and `helperFiles` the screens' pure modules (art subjects, descriptions, feed cards, roster, save codes, map drawing, tale card...) that checks add after them. Read it with `rulesSource({extra:[...helpers]})` (LF-normalised, imports and `export` stripped, the spell data first; `data:false` for a check that brings its own catalogue) or `readText(file)`. Add a new rules file there once, after any file whose top-level constants it reads. verify-rules-engine.cjs checks that every listed file's imports are listed (rules import only rules; helpers may import helpers), that top-level names are unique across rules and helpers and that they all load together, and that no other script keeps a copy of the list: the few checks that deliberately load a handful of modules alone, to test them in isolation, are named there (`subsets`: casting enforcement, combat, weapons, companions, dialogue triggers, foes, playback, legal, map layout, new story, world art, theme).
- The adventure step (2026-10-20, the code cleanup, the host: "sounds like we should start with cleaning up the code"; moved, not changed): `adventureRules.js` keeps the step's spine (`adventureStep`, `wholeTurn`, `heroStep`, `livingStep`, `dyingStep`, `adventureStepEngine`, `adventureStepCore`, `resolveAdventureStep`, `reviveFriend`, `advanceChapter`, the foe numbers (`lanternFoe`, `encounterFoe`, `withFoeScale`, `foeStanding`) and `newAdventure`, which screens import from there); `resolveAdventureStep` runs the checks every move meets, then `stepHandler(action)`: one table of named handlers keyed by an action object's `type` or an action word (or its prefix, such as 'drink:'), each returning the result or nothing to let the move go on to the creature round; the shared ending is `finishStep`. The handlers and the fights live by theme, each listed in `rulesFiles` right after adventureRules.js: `wildFightRules.js` (`creatureCombatStep`, `creatureRound`: the round of a creature fight; `ambushStep`; a wild fight's ending, `wildFightEnds`, and the ambush on arrival, `arrivalAmbush`), `partyTurnRules.js` (a party's fight in turns: `partyStep`, `beginPartyFight`, `passTurns`, `foeTurn`, `partyAftermath`, `partyFightOn`, `toldOf`), `brawlRules.js` (`brawlStep`, `runNpcTurns`, `brawlWorthy`, a blow at a person, a held spell used again on a person), `recoveryRules.js` (`givePotion`, `giveDraught`, `drinkPotion`, `drinkFromPack`, `drinkDraught`, `shortRest`, `longRest`), `travelRules.js` (`travelStep`, `discoverStep`, `beyondStep`) and `crossroadsRules.js` (the crossroads tale's inn, tower and bridge, `abilityCheck`, `restartAdventure`). These modules import each other in cycles, which is safe only while no module's top level reads another's bindings (the handler table is built on first use for that reason); a re-export (`export {x} from`) must not be used (rules-files.cjs strips only import lines). A new kind of move gets a handler in the table, never another `if` in resolveAdventureStep; a new function goes at column 0 (verify-rules-engine checks top-level names there).
- Spells and the Dungeon Master's context (2026-10-20, the code cleanup, the host: "sounds like we should start with cleaning up the code"; moved, not changed): `spellRules.js` keeps casting itself (the effect tables and the top-level code that builds `automaticEffects` from `narratedSpells`, `spellConditions` and `allyTargets`, `knownSpells`, `castError`, `castableNow`, `requestSpell`, `resolveSpellRuling`, `concentrationAfterDamage`, `inspectSpellCast`); what spells leave behind lives by theme: `spellConditionRules.js` (conditions on creatures: the marks, their edge on attacks and saves, harm breaking sleep and charms, `endSpellMarks`, the creature's turn), `spellDamageRules.js` (`spellDamageMax`; defences: `personDefenses`, `creatureNature`, `withDefenses`, `defended`; `riderDamage`; `harmCreature`), `spellBuffRules.js` (buffs on the caster, `spellDefense`, Shield, Guidance and Resistance, spells for friends and their boons, `partyHeal`) and `lastingSpellRules.js` (`lastingSpells`, `lastingTurn`, `spellAgain`, `moveMark`, `markGone`, the sustain chips). `dmContext.js` keeps `dmContext` and the turn commits; `dmChoiceRules.js` holds `dmChoices`, `offeredChoices`, the journeys (`travelChoices`, `beyondChoices`, `mapMoves`), rests (`shortRestsLeft`, `restWouldHelp`, `canShortRest`) and companions' orders; `turnResourceRules.js` the bonus-action choices (`bonusChoices`, `brawlBonusChoices`, `brawlFeatChoices`, `featChoices`, `sustainActions`); `dmSceneRules.js` the written story's part of the context (`storyWorld`: the tale, the quest, people not here, the world) with `placesNamed`, `withNamedJourneys`, `peopleAway` and the size limits. Each sits in `rulesFiles` right after the file it came from. A table that top-level code reads stays in the module that runs that code.
- The running DM server reloads `dm-server.cjs` and the modules it drops from require.cache at its top (`dm-rules.cjs`, `ai-request.cjs`, `dm-intent.cjs`, `dm-instructions.cjs`, `dm-schemas.cjs`, `dm-replies.cjs`) and `adventure-generator.cjs` on every request, so an edit there is live for testers at once: run `node -e "require('./dm-server.cjs');require('./adventure-generator.cjs')"` straight after editing them (watch apostrophes inside single-quoted instruction strings). A new module that dm-server.cjs requires must be dropped from require.cache there too (before any module that requires it: dm-schemas before dm-replies), or hot reload stops reaching it. Modules that stay loaded between requests (spending.cjs, tale-library.cjs, people-library.cjs, world-art.cjs (which loads the place, people and creature libraries and hero-faces.cjs afresh when it loads), moderation.cjs, story-jobs.cjs) are reloaded only when their `revision` differs from dm-server's literal. The Dungeon Master's code is split by job (2026-10-20, the code cleanup, the host: "sounds like we should start with cleaning up the code"; moved, not changed): `dm-instructions.cjs` holds the standing instructions (every paragraph: CONTRACT, QUEST, MEMORY, PEOPLE ANYWHERE, LOOT AND TRADE, one on one, suggestions, narrators, party...) and the narrators' voices and builds `standingPrompt` and `heroPrompt`; `dm-schemas.cjs` the reply schema (`replySchemaFor`), its parts, the kinds they allow, the magic list (`magicRarity`) and `phaseFields`; `dm-replies.cjs` (requires dm-schemas.cjs) the reply's checks, repairs and tidying (`validLoot`, `validIntroduce`, `validRelationships`, `relationshipAllowed`, `personId`, `repaired*`, `clampedLoot`, `checkedHp`, `spellNamed`, `distinctDialogue`/`echoes`, `cleanRemember`, `cleanSuggestions`, `unusable`/`transient`, `tellingCut`); dm-server.cjs keeps the scene (`sceneFor`, `sceneParts`, `sceneMessages`, `turnRequest`), the modes (`generate` routes art, journeys, story jobs, diagnostics, people batches, moderation, tales and heroes), the logs, `createServer` and a turn's phases (`playTurn`: `turnPlan`, `turnAsker`, `replyReader` with steadied/judge/rescued, `answered`, `secondLooked`; their shared state in `turn.state`), and re-exports every name the checks import from it. Deploy the four together, the three modules first and dm-server.cjs last.
- One-tap action chips (`quickActions.js`) are built from `dmChoices` and send the engine action with the player's sentence. Keep `dmChoices` limited to actions the engine will accept right now (travel is filtered through `travelError`); `verify-quick-actions.cjs` runs every chip through `adventureStep` and `commitDmTurn`.
- Testing: run the checks in a separate worktree, never the live checkout (the spell checks rewrite SPELL-COVERAGE.json and SPELL-INTEGRATION-REPORT.json); `verify-spell-routes.cjs` alone takes about 270 s. Test accounts and mail with `test-fixtures.cjs` and synthetic mail, never real accounts, and never run the paid-AI scripts (`dm-probe`, `dm-judgment`, `verify-new-story --live`) or scripts that touch ports 8081–8087 unless the host authorises it. On a Windows clone with CRLF endings `verify-legal.cjs` can fail only on line endings in the generated pages.
- Edit source files with UTF-8-safe tools. Windows PowerShell 5.1 `Get-Content`/`Set-Content` round-trips corrupt non-ASCII characters; keep `launch.ps1` pure ASCII.

The parent AGENTS.md still applies, including read-only `sources/` files.


## Account/campaign security update (8 October 2026)

This section replaces earlier descriptions of anonymous whole-table writes and browser-stored login tokens. Verified accounts (and, during the beta, anonymous guest identities) use HttpOnly cookies via `/api/sync`; never restore token-bearing request bodies. `account-server.cjs` composes accounts, operations and campaigns. `campaigns.cjs` and `server-turn.cjs` own shared state and dice; `rules-engine.cjs` loads the existing trusted rules. `useSharedTable.js` sends intents with version and request identity, never autosaves client snapshots. The old `sharedTableSync.js` was removed. Local saves use `playerStorage.js` account namespaces. Keep delayed work bound to the profile and campaign generation that started it.

Remote HTTP and the old LAN gateway are retired; give desktop and HTTPS phone links. `launch.ps1` uses the HTTPS sharing flow for phones. The dedicated game email is not configured yet; do not default to the user's personal mailbox. `Connect-Game-Email.cmd` supplies a hidden local prompt. Test with `test-fixtures.cjs` and synthetic mail, never real accounts or paid AI unless authorized. `verify-identity-campaigns.cjs`, `verify-sync.cjs` and `verify-table-client.cjs` cover the new contracts. Keep SECURITY.md accurate about mail, hosting, backup and legal-review limitations.

Beta (9 October 2026, the host's words: "don't require a login yet, we can add that when we fully release. the beta testers can't play the game right now"): guest play is the default. A signed-in, email-verified account is not needed to play while `accountsRequired` in `accountRules.js` is false; each browser gets an anonymous guest identity instead (see Guest play), and accounts stay available as an offer. Turn the requirement on at release by setting `accountsRequired=true` (or `QUESTBOUND_REQUIRE_ACCOUNTS=1` when hosted), rebuilding `dist-phone` and restarting the account service and the gateways.

Local saves (10 October 2026, the host's words: "make sure the login page is gone entirely for now, all data will be saved locally"): while `accountsOffered()` (accountRules.js: `accountsRequired`, or the host's service requiring accounts) is false, players see nothing about accounts: Settings shows no AccountSettings and no CloudSaveSettings (recovery code), the title has no "Keep your progress safe" prompt (`unprotected`), a held account does not open Settings, the boot line says "Opening Questbound…", and the developer checks drop the account and host checks. `scheduleCloudSave` and `uploadSave` send nothing (no `cloud-save`, no `account-save`). Settings → Move your hero (save codes) stays. The silent guest identity stays (the shared table and per-player AI budgets need it; it is not a login). A browser already signed in to an account keeps its account save namespace locally but uploads nothing. The server still answers the account routes if called directly; only the game hides them. Turning `accountsRequired` on brings all of it back, and needs `legalText.js` rewritten for accounts in the same change (verify-legal enforces it). `verify-accounts.cjs` runs `scheduleCloudSave` with the real switch (no request) and switched on (uploads again).
